{"type":"user","session_id":"a4d71fd7-efc6-4444-8141-7fa312a2977a","message":{"content":[{"type":"tool_result","tool_use_id":"toolu_01378SzntdkKoU7uLDZBSkSb","content":"1\t# Complete U3 source review at 9288ea2939f56eeb75fa53b6260f6cf1e91f31f0\n2\t\n3\t\n4\t## docs/site/durable-workflows/streams.md\n5\t\n6\tOriginal-byte SHA256:0e7d33a08910bc441f0cf573f13326dfdd76f26e90ebe9eb93349204afb279f5\n7\t\n8\t---\n9\tlayout: layouts/base.vto\n10\ttitle: Durable streams\n11\ttemplateEngine: [vento, md]\n12\tprev: { label: \"Triggers & ingress\", href: \"/durable-workflows/triggers/\" }\n13\tnext: { label: \"Database & Prisma\", href: \"/data-persistence/database/\" }\n14\torder: 2\n15\t---\n16\t\n17\t# Durable streams\n18\t\n19\tA NetScript **stream** is a typed change-log using the Durable Streams protocol: producers write entity\n20\tstate into a durable-stream server, and any number of HTTP/SSE consumers\n21\tmaterialize the latest value per key. {{ comp.badge({ status: \"alpha\" }) }}\n22\t\n23\t{{ comp.diagram({\n24\t  src: \"/assets/diagrams/streams-pipeline.svg\",\n25\t  alt: \"A producer defines a typed stream schema and writes upsert/delete operations into the durable-stream server on its assigned port; the stream log fans out over HTTP/SSE to Fresh consumers that materialize the latest value per key.\",\n26\t  caption: \"Streams pipeline: producer (defineStreamSchema + createDurableStream) → stream log on its assigned port → HTTP/SSE → Fresh consumers (latest value per key).\"\n27\t}) }}\n28\t\n29\tNetScript's streams capability is the typed, change-data backbone the other\n30\tplugins lean on — workers, sagas, and the auth service all publish their live\n31\tstate through it. The producer half is implemented: you define a\n32\ttyped stream schema with [`defineStreamSchema`](/reference/streams/), open a\n33\tproducer with `createDurableStream` (or the Service-facing\n34\t`createServiceStreamProducer`), and `upsert`/`delete`/`flush` entity state\n35\tover a durable-stream server that runs as an Aspire resource on its assigned port.\n36\t\n37\tThe producer runtime in `@netscript/plugin-streams-core` writes through\n38\t`@durable-streams/client` with idempotent delivery. `@netscript/plugin-streams` carries the service manifest and\n39\t`defineStreamTopic` definitions. Runtime producers use the core package; consumers read the durable-stream server's HTTP/SSE\n40\tprotocol to materialize state.\n41\t\n42\t## What it is\n43\t\n44\tA NetScript stream is an **entity-oriented change log**. You describe a set of\n45\tcollections — each a named entity type with a primary key — and the producer\n46\tpublishes `upsert` and `delete` operations keyed by that primary key. Downstream\n47\treaders materialize the latest value per key and observe a live, replayable view\n48\tof your domain state. This is the same contracts-first instinct as oRPC services,\n49\tbut applied to **state replication** instead of request/response: the schema is\n50\tthe type contract that both producer and any HTTP/SSE consumer are locked to.\n51\t\n52\tStreams sit alongside the other long-running capabilities rather than replacing\n53\tthem. Reach for a {{ comp.xref({ key: \"cap:durable-sagas\", text: \"durable saga\" }) }}\n54\twhen you need message-driven orchestration with compensation; reach for a\n55\t{{ comp.xref({ key: \"cap:triggers\", text: \"trigger\" }) }} when inbound HTTP or a\n56\tfile-watch should kick off work. A stream is the **read-model fan-out**: each of\n57\tthose plugins runs a thin `streams/producer.ts` that mirrors its execution state\n58\tthrough `createDurableStream`, so a Fresh dashboard can watch saga, worker, and\n59\ttrigger progress live without polling a request/response API.\n60\t\n61\tThe workflow this replaces is familiar: a dashboard polls `GET /executions` on an interval, the\n62\tinterval is always wrong — too fast for the server, too slow for the operator watching a stuck\n63\timport — and every new consumer re-implements the same polling loop against a slightly different\n64\tendpoint. With a stream, the contract moves to the data itself. The\n65\t[live-dashboard tutorial](/tutorials/live-dashboard/05-live-stream/) builds exactly this: worker\n66\texecution state published once through a producer, read by a Fresh page that updates without a\n67\tpolling loop anywhere in it. And because the schema is a frozen, typed collection map, what a\n68\tstream carries is enumerable from its definition — `inspectStreamTopic` renders it as a JSON-stable\n69\treport, which is as useful to a CLI doctor or a coding agent as it is to a test.\n70\t\n71\t### Worker execution progress ordering and replay\n72\t\n73\tWorker handlers report progress through an execution-local outbound channel. Calls are persisted in\n74\tinvocation order for that execution, including repeated percentages and decreasing values; the\n75\truntime does not debounce, deduplicate, or coalesce them. A terminal success or failure waits for\n76\tall accepted progress writes to drain, so an unawaited progress call cannot race past completion.\n77\tProgress persistence failures fail the execution instead of being reduced to log messages.\n78\t\n79\tEach accepted transition publishes the existing full execution-record upsert. Durable-stream replay\n80\ttherefore remains entity replay: offsets order the retained updates, and reducing by execution id\n81\tmaterializes the newest record with its last progress value and terminal status. Intermediate\n82\tupdates may be visible in the retained log, but this is not a separate progress-history API.\n83\t\n84\tFor React Native screens, follow [Consume durable streams in Expo](/durable-workflows/expo-streams/).\n85\tIt uses the same TanStack DB engine through an injected fetch and includes the native runtime probe.\n86\t\n87\t## Learn → / Do →\n88\t\n89\t{{ comp.featureGrid({ items: [\n90\t  {\n91\t    title: \"Learn — stream a live dashboard\",\n92\t    body: \"Track D 05: publish execution state from a producer and read it over HTTP/SSE into a live Fresh dashboard.\",\n93\t    href: \"/tutorials/live-dashboard/05-live-stream/\",\n94\t    icon: \"→\"\n95\t  },\n96\t  {\n97\t    title: \"Do — add the streams plugin\",\n98\t    body: \"Public package install adds the stream plugin dependency and user-owned glue; local netscript-dev scaffolding supports contributor-source samples.\",\n99\t    href: \"/orchestration-runtime/how-to/add-a-plugin/\",\n100\t    icon: \"◆\"\n101\t  },\n102\t  {\n103\t    title: \"Understand — the plugin system\",\n104\t    body: \"How thread-isolated plugins like streams register contributions and wire into the Aspire resource graph.\",\n105\t    href: \"/explanation/plugin-system/\",\n106\t    icon: \"◎\"\n107\t  }\n108\t] }) }}\n109\t\n110\t## Minimal example — produce, then consume\n111\t\n112\tThe producer side is two calls: freeze a typed schema, open a stream, write\n113\tentity state. The consumer side is an HTTP/SSE read of the same stream path — there is no in-process `subscribe()` handle, so a Fresh island (or any\n114\tSSE client) reads the durable log directly and materializes the latest value per\n115\tkey. This is deliberate: exposing one HTTP/SSE surface instead of an in-process\n116\t`subscribe()` means a browser island and a server-side consumer read the durable\n117\tlog through the exact same path and wire format, with no separate in-VM\n118\tsubscription API to keep in sync.\n119\t\n120\t{{ comp.tabbedCode({ tabs: [\n121\t  {\n122\t    label: \"Define a schema (real)\",\n123\t    lang: \"ts\",\n124\t    code: \"// streams/executions-schema.ts\\n// Author against the core package — this is the live surface.\\nimport { defineStreamSchema } from '@netscript/plugin-streams-core';\\nimport { z } from 'zod';\\n\\n// Each collection is an entity type with a primary key. The schema is the\\n// type contract producers and HTTP/SSE consumers are locked to.\\nexport const executionsSchema = defineStreamSchema({\\n  execution: {\\n    schema: z.object({\\n      id: z.string().min(1),\\n      status: z.enum(['queued', 'running', 'succeeded', 'failed']),\\n      updatedAt: z.string().datetime(),\\n    }),\\n    type: 'execution',\\n    primaryKey: 'id',\\n  },\\n});\"\n125\t  },\n126\t  {\n127\t    label: \"Open a producer & write (real)\",\n128\t    lang: \"ts\",\n129\t    code: \"// streams/producer.ts\\nimport { createDurableStream } from '@netscript/plugin-streams-core';\\nimport { executionsSchema } from './executions-schema.ts';\\n\\n// createDurableStream returns a singleton producer per streamPath and begins\\n// connecting to the durable-stream server immediately.\\nconst producer = createDurableStream({\\n  streamPath: '/workers/executions',\\n  schema: executionsSchema,\\n  producerId: 'workers-service',\\n});\\n\\n// upsert/delete are synchronous enqueues keyed by the collection primary key.\\nproducer.upsert('execution', {\\n  id: 'exec-1',\\n  status: 'running',\\n  updatedAt: new Date().toISOString(),\\n});\\nproducer.delete('execution', 'exec-0');\\n\\n// flush before graceful shutdown; it rethrows the connect error if the\\n// producer never connected (see known limitations).\\nawait producer.flush();\"\n130\t  },\n131\t  {\n132\t    label: \"Consume over HTTP/SSE (Fresh client)\",\n133\t    lang: \"ts\",\n134\t    code: \"// islands/ExecutionsView.tsx — read the durable log directly\\nimport { buildStreamUrl, getStreamsUrl } from '@netscript/plugin-streams-core';\\nimport { bindStreamEventSourceV1 } from '@netscript/fresh/streams';\\n\\n// Native EventSource needs the real stream route, an opaque replay offset, and\\n// live=sse. It cannot attach arbitrary authorization headers, so use this from\\n// a same-origin/cookie-compatible route or proxy when the service is protected.\\nconst url = new URL(buildStreamUrl('/workers/executions', getStreamsUrl()));\\nurl.searchParams.set('offset', '-1');\\nurl.searchParams.set('live', 'sse');\\nconst source = new EventSource(url);\\n\\nconst latest = new Map<string, unknown>();\\nconst binding = bindStreamEventSourceV1({\\n  source,\\n  onEvent(event) {\\n    // The helper registers named data/control listeners and validates both\\n    // payload schemas before delivering this exhaustive outcome union.\\n    if (event.event === 'error') { latest.set('$error', event.payload); return; }\\n    if (event.event !== 'data') return; // control / derived heartbeat\\n    for (const change of event.payload) { // data is always an ordered array\\n      if (change.headers.operation === 'delete') latest.delete(change.key);\\n      else latest.set(change.key, change.value);\\n    }\\n  },\\n});\\n\\n// An island should call binding.dispose() from its effect cleanup. This plain\\n// browser example closes on page teardown; W3-A owns reconnect policy.\\naddEventListener('pagehide', () => binding.dispose(), { once: true });\"\n135\t  }\n136\t] }) }}\n137\t\n138\t## Breaking migration in 0.0.8\n139\t\n140\t`@netscript/plugin-streams` no longer exports `defineStreamProducer`, `defineStreamConsumer`,\n141\t`StreamProducerHandle`, `StreamConsumerHandle`, `StreamUnsupportedOperationError`, or\n142\t`unsupportedStreamOperation`. `streamsPlugin`, `defineStreamTopic`, `StreamTopicDefinition`, and\n143\t`StreamPayloadSchema` remain available for manifest declarations.\n144\t\n145\tFor producers, import `defineStreamSchema` and `createDurableStream` (or\n146\t`createServiceStreamProducer` in backend services) from `@netscript/plugin-streams-core`. Convert\n147\tthe topic payload schema to a named collection with a `primaryKey`, as shown above. Replace\n148\t`publish(payload)` with `upsert(collection, payload)` or `delete(collection, key)`; call `flush()`\n149\tand `close()` during shutdown. This is an entity-state transport: generic message payloads need\n150\tstable entity ids and an appropriate collection model.\n151\t\n152\tFor consumers, replace `subscribe(handler)` with HTTP/SSE reads of the same stream path.\n153\t`buildStreamUrl` and `getStreamsUrl` from `@netscript/plugin-streams-core` resolve that URL;\n154\t`bindStreamEventSourceV1` from `@netscript/fresh/streams` validates named SSE events in browser\n155\tclients, as shown above. Dispose the binding when the client tears down. There is no in-process\n156\ttopic subscription replacement.\n157\t\n158\t## Key types first — the stream definition API\n159\t\n160\tA stream schema is a map of **collections**. Each collection is a\n161\t`CollectionDefinition` — a Standard-Schema validator, a State-Protocol `type`\n162\tdiscriminator, and the `primaryKey` property the producer keys writes by.\n163\t`defineStreamSchema(collections)` freezes that map into a `StateSchema` that both\n164\tthe producer and any HTTP/SSE consumer are locked to.\n165\t\n166\t{{ comp.apiTable({\n167\t  caption: \"CollectionDefinition — one entry per collection in defineStreamSchema\",\n168\t  rows: [\n169\t    { name: \"schema\", type: \"unknown (Standard Schema validator)\", desc: \"Standard-Schema-compatible validator (e.g. a zod object) used by durable-streams to validate the collection payload.\" },\n170\t    { name: \"type\", type: \"string (required)\", desc: \"State Protocol type discriminator emitted for every event in this collection (e.g. 'execution').\" },\n171\t    { name: \"primaryKey\", type: \"string (required)\", desc: \"Property name on the value used as the entity primary key; upsert/delete are keyed by this property.\" }\n172\t  ]\n173\t}) }}\n174\t\n175\t`defineStreamSchema` returns a frozen `StateSchema<TDef>` — the durable-streams\n176\truntime attaches per-collection event helpers (`insert`/`update`/`upsert`/`delete`,\n177\tthe `CollectionEventHelpers`) so the schema can both validate and emit\n178\tState-Protocol `ChangeEvent`s. The supported `Operation` set is\n179\t`insert | update | delete | upsert`.\n180\t\n181\t## Producer options — `createDurableStream`\n182\t\n183\t`createDurableStream(options)` takes a `DurableStreamProducerOptions` and returns\n184\ta `DurableStreamProducer`. It is a **singleton factory keyed by `streamPath`**:\n185\tcalling it twice with the same path returns the same live producer (a closed one\n186\tis replaced). Writes are idempotent via `@durable-streams/client`'s\n187\t`IdempotentProducer` (stable `producerId` + auto-claim), so duplicate enqueues do\n188\tnot double-apply downstream.\n189\t\n190\t{{ comp.apiTable({\n191\t  caption: \"DurableStreamProducerOptions (createDurableStream argument)\",\n192\t  rows: [\n193\t    { name: \"streamPath\", type: \"string (required)\", desc: \"Stream path relative to the base URL, e.g. '/workers/executions'. This is the singleton key and the path consumers read over HTTP/SSE.\" },\n194\t    { name: \"schema\", type: \"StateSchema<TDef> (required)\", desc: \"The frozen schema returned by defineStreamSchema; binds the producer to its collection map.\" },\n195\t    { name: \"producerId\", type: \"string (required)\", desc: \"Stable producer identity used for idempotent delivery (IdempotentProducer auto-claim). Keep it stable across restarts for duplicate-safe writes.\" },\n196\t    { name: \"signal\", type: \"AbortSignal?\", desc: \"Optional abort signal consulted while opening the stream connection; aborting cancels the connect (an AbortError is treated as expected, not a failure).\" }\n197\t  ]\n198\t}) }}\n199\t\n200\tThe `DurableStreamProducer` it returns exposes a small, synchronous-write surface\n201\twith an async flush/close for shutdown. (`StreamProducerPort` is the\n202\timplemented-by interface — the same four members, with `entityType` widened to\n203\t`string`.)\n204\t\n205\t{{ comp.apiTable({\n206\t  title: \"DurableStreamProducer — methods\",\n207\t  columns: [\"Member\", \"Shape\", \"Behavior\"],\n208\t  rows: [\n209\t    [\"<code>upsert(entityType, value)</code>\", \"<code>(K, Record) =&gt; void</code>\", \"Enqueue an upsert keyed by the collection <code>primaryKey</code>; skipped (warns) if the key is missing/empty or the collection is unknown.\"],\n210\t    [\"<code>delete(entityType, key)</code>\", \"<code>(K, string) =&gt; void</code>\", \"Enqueue a delete by primary key; skipped (warns) on an empty key.\"],\n211\t    [\"<code>flush()</code>\", \"<code>() =&gt; Promise&lt;void&gt;</code>\", \"Await pending writes before shutdown; <strong>rethrows</strong> the connect error if the producer never connected.\"],\n212\t    [\"<code>close()</code>\", \"<code>() =&gt; Promise&lt;void&gt;</code>\", \"Flush, close the underlying handle, and release the singleton for this <code>streamPath</code>.\"],\n213\t    [\"<code>streamPath</code>\", \"<code>string (readonly)</code>\", \"The stream path this producer owns.\"],\n214\t    [\"<code>closed</code>\", \"<code>boolean (get)</code>\", \"Whether shutdown has begun; further <code>upsert</code>/<code>delete</code> calls are ignored.\"]\n215\t  ]\n216\t}) }}\n217\t\n218\t## Bound stream retention\n219\t\n220\tSet `retention: { kind: 'ttl', ttlSeconds: 604800 }` or\n221\t`retention: { kind: 'expires-at', expiresAt: '2030-10-08T00:00:00Z' }` on a producer before creating\n222\tits stream. On the shipped server, TTL is a sliding inactivity window renewed by reads and appends;\n223\tHEAD and reopening with PUT do not renew it or change the policy. Use absolute expiry or rotating\n224\tday segments for a hard bound on an active stream. Absolute expiry does not slide. Both policies\n225\texpire the whole stream. Omit retention for the current unbounded behavior. For existing day\n226\tsegments, `headDurableStream` reads metadata and `deleteDurableStream` deletes the whole stream\n227\tthrough a versioned administrative port.\n228\t\n229\tRun deletion from background workers or triggers with a service identity, independently of any app\n230\tsession. Entity `producer.delete(...)` appends a tombstone and does not shrink the log. See the\n231\t[stream retention how-to](/durable-workflows/how-to/bound-stream-retention/) for server TTL,\n232\tabsolute expiry, scheduled segment deletion, typed failure handling, and document day baselines.\n233\t\n234\t## Service-side producers — `createServiceStreamProducer`\n235\t\n236\tWhen the writer is a backend **Service** — for example an ingestion worker that\n237\temits a `doc.ready` completion event from the callback where the work finishes —\n238\treach for `createServiceStreamProducer` instead of wiring `createDurableStream`\n239\tand the URL/auth resolvers by hand. It is a thin wrapper over `createDurableStream`\n240\tthat reuses the exact same singleton producer and Aspire discovery\n241\t(`getStreamsUrl` / `getStreamsAuth`). Stream discovery is resolved at\n242\tconstruction, so a Service that lacks the `streams` plugin reference **throws\n243\timmediately** rather than blocking or silently dropping writes.\n244\t\n245\t```ts\n246\t// services/ingestion/emit-completion.ts\n247\timport {\n248\t  createServiceStreamProducer,\n249\t  defineStreamSchema,\n250\t} from \"@netscript/plugin-streams-core\";\n251\timport { z } from \"zod\";\n252\t\n253\tconst completions = defineStreamSchema({\n254\t  completion: {\n255\t    schema: z.object({ id: z.string().min(1), status: z.string() }),\n256\t    type: \"completion\",\n257\t    primaryKey: \"id\",\n258\t  },\n259\t});\n260\t\n261\t// This throws now if neither DURABLE_STREAMS_URL nor\n262\t// services__streams__http__0 is wired.\n263\tconst producer = createServiceStreamProducer({\n264\t  streamPath: \"/support-chat/completions\",\n265\t  schema: completions,\n266\t  producerId: \"support-chat-ingestion\",\n267\t});\n268\t\n269\tproducer.upsert(\"completion\", { id: \"run-1\", status: \"done\" });\n270\tawait producer.flush();\n271\t```\n272\t\n273\tThe CLI writes the required plugin reference when the streams plugin is\n274\tinstalled. If construction reports a missing `streams` reference, install the\n275\tstreams plugin and run `netscript service generate` to reconcile the generated\n276\tconfiguration. `ServiceStreamProducerOptions` has the same fields as\n277\t`DurableStreamProducerOptions`; the returned `DurableStreamProducer` is\n278\tidentical, so the write/flush/close surface below applies unchanged.\n279\t\n280\t## Runtime & transport — URL and auth resolution\n281\t\n282\tThe producer never hardcodes a host. `createDurableStream` resolves the\n283\tdurable-stream base URL through `getStreamsUrl()` and the auth header through\n284\t`getStreamsAuth()`, both of which read the environment so the same code works\n285\tunder Aspire, in a browser build, or against an explicit override. `buildStreamUrl`\n286\tjoins a stream path onto that base, and `inspectStreamTopic` returns a JSON-stable\n287\tdiagnostic report for a schema (handy in tests and CLI doctors).\n288\t\n289\t{{ comp.apiTable({\n290\t  caption: \"Runtime resolvers (@netscript/plugin-streams-core)\",\n291\t  rows: [\n292\t    { name: \"getStreamsUrl()\", type: \"() => string\", desc: \"Resolves the durable-stream base URL. Server: DURABLE_STREAMS_URL override, else Aspire's services__streams__http__0 discovery var. Browser: VITE_services__streams__http__0 (or the VITE_STREAMS_URL shorthand). Throws a descriptive error if none resolve.\" },\n293\t    { name: \"getStreamsAuth()\", type: \"() => Record<string,string>\", desc: \"Builds the auth header from STREAMS_SECRET (or DURABLE_STREAMS_SECRET) as { Authorization: 'Bearer <secret>' }; returns {} when no secret is set.\" },\n294\t    { name: \"buildStreamUrl(path, baseUrl?)\", type: \"(string, string?) => string\", desc: \"Joins a stream path onto the resolved base (or an explicit baseUrl), trimming a trailing slash on the base.\" },\n295\t    { name: \"inspectStreamTopic(input)\", type: \"(input) => StreamTopicInspectionReport\", desc: \"Diagnostic: returns a JSON-stable report (package, target, summary, details with collections/streamPath/producerId) for a schema + optional producer metadata.\" }\n296\t  ]\n297\t}) }}\n298\t\n299\t{{ comp.apiTable({\n300\t  caption: \"Environment variables read by the resolvers\",\n301\t  rows: [\n302\t    { name: \"DURABLE_STREAMS_URL\", type: \"server override\", desc: \"Explicit base URL; takes precedence over Aspire discovery (e.g. your custom stream server URL).\" },\n303\t    { name: \"services__streams__http__0\", type: \"Aspire (server)\", desc: \"Injected by the Aspire resource graph; the default server-side discovery path.\" },\n304\t    { name: \"VITE_services__streams__http__0\", type: \"browser\", desc: \"Vite-injected reference for browser/Fresh consumers; VITE_STREAMS_URL is the convenience shorthand.\" },\n305\t    { name: \"STREAMS_SECRET / DURABLE_STREAMS_SECRET\", type: \"auth\", desc: \"Bearer secret for getStreamsAuth(); when set, every connect sends Authorization: Bearer <secret>.\" }\n306\t  ]\n307\t}) }}\n308\t\n309\t### Caller-provided reader headers\n310\t\n311\tFresh `createNetScriptStreamDB` and workers `createWorkersStreamDB` accept native\n312\t`HeadersRecord` values from `@durable-streams/client`: each value is a string or\n313\ta sync/async callback. The native client resolves callbacks per request, including\n314\treads after a Fresh reconnect.\n315\t\n316\t```ts\n317\timport { createWorkersStreamDB } from \"@netscript/plugin-workers/streams\";\n318\tdeclare const readReaderToken: () => Promise<string>;\n319\tconst workers = createWorkersStreamDB({\n320\t  headers: { authorization: async () => `Bearer ${await readReaderToken()}` },\n321\t});\n322\t```\n323\t\n324\tAny supplied record, including `{}`, replaces ambient producer auth completely;\n325\tonly omission or `undefined` selects `getStreamsAuth()`. Fresh's exported custom\n326\t`createStreamDB` port now accepts native header values instead of strings only:\n327\texternal serializers must forward the record unchanged to the native client.\n328\tHeader forwarding neither issues credentials nor authenticates a reader; callers\n329\tstill own reader credential and browser bootstrap policy.\n330\t\n331\t### Resolved stream URLs\n332\t\n333\tWhen configuring `streamPath` (e.g. `streamPath: '/workers/executions'`), the framework automatically prepends `STREAMS_URL_PREFIX = '/v1/stream/netscript'` inside `buildStreamUrl`.\n334\t\n335\tConcrete example:\n336\t- `streamPath`: `'/workers/executions'`\n337\t- `baseUrl`: `'https://streams.example.test'`\n338\t- Resolved endpoint: `'https://streams.example.test/v1/stream/netscript/workers/executions'`\n339\t\n340\t{{ comp callout { type: \"warning\", title: \"Storage durability — in-memory by default\" } }}\n341\tWhen <code>STREAMS_DATA_DIR</code> is unset, the streams service uses <strong>in-memory, non-durable storage</strong> — stream event data will be lost when the process restarts. File storage is an explicit opt-in: provision an existing persistent writable directory, then set <code>NetScript.Plugins.streams.Environment.STREAMS_DATA_DIR</code> in <code>appsettings.json</code> and regenerate Aspire helpers with <code>netscript generate aspire</code>. The generated AppHost forwards this environment value. Empty, missing or unwritable paths fail startup; there is no memory fallback. A startup write/read probe must pass before health reports file mode.\n342\t{{ /comp }}\n343\t\n344\t## Storage mode and restart operations\n345\t\n346\tThe plugin manifest declares memory as the ephemeral default. The detailed `/health` payload\n347\tcontains `checks[name=\"streams-storage\"].storage` with `mode: \"memory\" | \"file\"`, `durable`, and\n348\t`probe: \"not-applicable\" | \"passed\"`. Memory may be healthy while explicitly reporting\n349\t`durable: false`: all stream history disappears on service restart. “Durable Streams” names the\n350\tprotocol, not a persistence guarantee for memory storage.\n351\t\n352\tFor the default resource working directory, create `.netscript/data/streams`, ignore\n353\t`.netscript/data/` in Git, and set the plugin environment entry to that path. Deployments must mount\n354\tpersistent storage separately. File mode has an executed real `DurableStreamProducer` write, flush,\n355\tservice-process stop, restart and HTTP replay proof; memory has a restart/data-loss control. The\n356\tproof is in `plugins/streams/services/src/tests/storage-restart_test.ts` and supports orderly\n357\tprocess restart only, not power-loss durability, replication or multiple concurrent owners.\n358\t\n359\tRetention is unbounded without a configured expiry/deletion policy. Entity deletes append\n360\ttombstones; they do not compact history. Protocol TTL slides on reads/appends; absolute expiry is\n361\tfixed. Both expire whole streams. Use fixed expiry or scheduled whole-stream/day-segment deletion\n362\tfrom workers/triggers under service identity for a hard bound; monitor disk space. There is no\n363\tautomatic compaction or archival.\n364\t\n365\tRecovery stops at the last complete native frame, so an incomplete final append can be lost. Missing\n366\tsegment files can read empty; corrupt LMDB metadata may refuse startup. The startup probe verifies\n367\tdirectory I/O, not historical integrity or repair. Preserve damaged files and restore a known-good\n368\tbackup if recovery fails.\n369\t\n370\tStop producers and streams before copying the **entire** data directory (metadata and segments) for\n371\tbackup. Restore the complete copy while streams is stopped, retain compatible storage/protocol\n372\tversions, then verify replay before restarting producers. Live file copies are not a supported\n373\tconsistent backup. One process owns a directory. Durable-by-default provisioning and migration\n374\tremain the 0.0.9 enhancement [#2114](https://github.com/rickylabs/netscript/issues/2114).\n375\t\n376\t## Known limitations\n377\t\n378\tBe deliberate about what the alpha producer does and does not guarantee.\n379\t\n380\t{{ comp callout { type: \"warning\", title: \"Writes are dropped after a connect failure (no reconnect)\" } }}\n381\tIf the producer cannot reach the durable-stream server (on its assigned port) at startup, it logs a\n382\t<code>console.warn</code> and then <strong>silently skips every subsequent <code>upsert</code>/<code>delete</code></strong>\n383\t— there is no reconnect loop in the current alpha. <code>flush()</code> rethrows that connect error so a\n384\tgraceful shutdown surfaces the failure. (Writes issued <em>before</em> the connection completes are\n385\tbuffered and drained once it opens; the drop only applies after a connect <em>error</em>.) Treat a healthy\n386\tstreams service as a hard precondition for durable delivery; do not assume buffered writes will\n387\tbe replayed once the server returns.\n388\t{{ /comp }}\n389\t\n390\t{{ comp callout { type: \"note\", title: \"No in-process consumer — read over HTTP/SSE\" } }}\n391\tThere is no in-process <code>subscribe()</code> handle. Consumption happens over the durable-stream\n392\tserver's HTTP/SSE protocol, which Fresh clients read to materialize the latest value per key. Model your\n393\tread side as an HTTP/SSE consumer of the stream, not as an in-process callback.\n394\t{{ /comp }}\n395\t\n396\t{{ comp callout { type: \"warning\", title: \"Local HTTP can limit concurrent stream consumers\" } }}\n397\tThe local AppHost serves the generated app over <code>http://</code>. Under HTTP/1.1, browsers\n398\ttypically allow about six concurrent connections per origin, so several long-lived HTTP/SSE streams\n399\tfrom one Fresh page can starve later requests or stream subscriptions. Use HTTPS for HTTP/2 when a\n400\tlocal dashboard needs many simultaneous stream consumers.\n401\t{{ /comp }}\n402\t\n403\t{{ comp callout { type: \"note\", title: \"Operator visibility is console.warn (alpha)\" } }}\n404\tThe producer reports skips and connect failures through <code>console.warn</code> today — tracked as\n405\t<strong>AP-13</strong> architecture debt until the telemetry-integration wave supplies a structured\n406\treporter. Until then, scrape the <code>[DurableStreamProducer]</code> warn lines if you need to alert on\n407\tdropped writes.\n408\t{{ /comp }}\n409\t\n410\t## Endpoints & manifest\n411\t\n412\tThe streams plugin is registered as a utility/infra plugin — note it requires\n413\t**neither a database nor KV** (`requiresDb=false`, `requiresKv=false`), unlike\n414\tworkers, sagas, and triggers. Its durable-stream service listens on its assigned port and\n415\tis wired into the Aspire resource graph so workers, sagas, and auth can publish\n416\tthrough it. The port is overridable via `STREAMS_PORT` or `PORT`.\n417\t\n418\t{{ comp.apiTable({\n419\t  title: \"Streams plugin — runtime facts\",\n420\t  columns: [\"Property\", \"Value\"],\n421\t  rows: [\n422\t    [\"Plugin location\", \"<code>plugins/streams/</code>\"],\n423\t    [\"Producer package\", \"<code>@netscript/plugin-streams-core</code> (<code>createDurableStream</code>, <code>createServiceStreamProducer</code>, <code>defineStreamSchema</code>)\"],\n424\t    [\"Manifest import\", \"<code>@netscript/plugin-streams</code> — <code>streamsPlugin</code> and typed topic definitions\"],\n425\t    [\"Transport client\", \"<code>@durable-streams/client</code> (<code>IdempotentProducer</code>)\"],\n426\t    [\"Dev service port\", \"its assigned port (durable-stream Aspire service; override with <code>STREAMS_PORT</code>/<code>PORT</code>)\"],\n427\t    [\"provider.kind\", \"<code>stream</code> · category <code>plugin</code> · pluginType <code>utility</code>\"],\n428\t    [\"Requires DB / KV\", \"<code>false</code> / <code>false</code>\"],\n429\t    [\"First-party producers\", \"workers, sagas, triggers, auth (each <code>streams/producer.ts</code> → <code>createDurableStream</code>)\"],\n430\t    [\"Consumer surface\", \"HTTP/SSE from the server (Fresh clients) — <strong>no</strong> in-process <code>subscribe()</code>\"]\n431\t  ]\n432\t}) }}\n433\t\n434\tThe plugin is referenced from `netscript.config.ts` as\n435\t`./plugins/streams/mod.ts`. Because workers, sagas, and triggers each list\n436\t`streams` in their `dependencies`, it is installed first in the dependency graph\n437\tand its service comes up so dependent producers have somewhere to write.\n438\t\n439\t## Production notes\n440\t\n441\t{{ comp callout { type: \"important\", title: \"Footguns before you ship\" } }}\n442\t<ul>\n443\t<li><strong>Use the producer contract.</strong> Import\n444\t<code>createDurableStream</code> / <code>defineStreamSchema</code> from\n445\t<code>@netscript/plugin-streams-core</code> and write entity state keyed by collection primary key.</li>\n446\t<li><strong>A healthy streams server is a hard precondition.</strong> A startup connect\n447\tfailure drops every later write with no reconnect — bring Aspire (or an explicit\n448\t<code>DURABLE_STREAMS_URL</code>) up first, and treat a <code>flush()</code> rejection on shutdown as a\n449\treal delivery failure, not noise.</li>\n450\t<li><strong>Keep <code>producerId</code> stable across restarts.</strong> Idempotent delivery is keyed\n451\t"}]}}

A NetScript stream scopes the same promise differently: sync is a property\n16\tof a **declared contract**, not of the database. You freeze a schema of collections, a producer\n17\twrites latest-state per key to the durable-stream service in your own Aspire graph, and any\n18\tHTTP/SSE consumer materializes that view — independent of which store actually holds your data\n19\t(the plugin itself requires neither a database nor KV). The practical consequence: you choose\n20\tper-collection what becomes live state, and everything outside that schema stays on the\n21\trequest/response and persistence paths you already have.\n22\t\n23\t## Reference\n24\t\n25\tThis hub is intentionally thin — the full generated API lives in the reference.\n26\t\n27\t{{ comp.xref({ key: \"ref:streams\" }) }}\n28\t\n29\t{{ comp callout { type: \"tip\", title: \"Where the real surface comes from\" } }}\n30\tThe producer you build on lives in <code>@netscript/plugin-streams-core</code>:\n31\t<code>createDurableStream</code>, the Service-facing <code>createServiceStreamProducer</code>,\n32\t<code>DurableStreamProducer</code>, <code>defineStreamSchema</code>,\n33\tthe <code>buildStreamUrl</code>/<code>getStreamsUrl</code>/<code>getStreamsAuth</code> resolvers,\n34\t<code>inspectStreamTopic</code>, and the <code>StreamProducerPort</code> /\n35\t<code>DurableStreamProducerOptions</code> types. parsed : [parsed];\n334\t            const batch: JsonBatch<T> = {\n335\t              items,\n336\t              offset: session.offset,\n337\t              cursor: session.cursor,\n338\t              upToDate: session.upToDate,\n339\t              streamClosed: session.streamClosed,\n340\t            };\n341\t            try {\n342\t              await subscriber(batch);\n343\t            } catch (error) {\n344\t              subscriberFailed = true;\n345\t              throw error;\n346\t            }\n347\t            signal.throwIfAborted();\n348\t            state.offset = batch.offset;\n349\t            state.cursor = batch.cursor;\n350\t            state.upToDate = batch.upToDate;\n351\t            state.retries = 0;\n352\t            state.status = 'live';\n353\t            if (batch.streamClosed) break;\n354\t          } catch (error) {\n355\t            if (signal.aborted) {\n356\t              state.status = lifetime.signal.aborted ? 'stopped' : 'failed';\n357\t              if (state.status === 'failed') throw error;\n358\t              return;\n359\t            }\n360\t            if (subscriberFailed) {\n361\t              state.status = 'failed';\n362\t              throw error;\n363\t            }\n364\t            await retry(error);\n365\t          } finally {\n366\t            session.cancel();\n367\t            current.release();\n368\t          }\n369\t          current = await connect();\n370\t          session = current.session;\n371\t        }\n372\t        state.status = 'stopped';\n373\t      }\n374\t      void consume().then(resolveClosed, rejectClosed);\n375\t      return () => {\n376\t        lifetime.abort();\n377\t        state.status = 'stopped';\n378\t        session.cancel();\n379\t        current.release();\n380\t      };\n381\t    }\n382\t\n383\t    return new Proxy(first.session, {\n384\t      get(target, key) {\n385\t        if (key === 'closed') return closed;\n386\t        if (key === 'subscribeJson') return subscribeJson;\n387\t        if (key === 'offset') return state.offset;\n388\t        const value = Reflect.get(target, key, target);\n389\t        return typeof value === 'function' ? value.bind(target) : value;\n390\t      },\n391\t    });\n392\t  }\n393\t\n394\t  const stream = new Proxy(native, {\n395\t    get(target, key) {\n396\t      if (key === 'stream') return read;\n397\t      const value = Reflect.get(target, key, target);\n398\t      return typeof value === 'function' ? value.bind(target) : value;\n399\t    },\n400\t  });\n401\t  return {\n402\t    stream,\n403\t    get status() {\n404\t      return state.status;\n405\t    },\n406\t    stop() {\n407\t      state.status = 'stopped';\n408\t      lifetime.abort();\n409\t    },\n410\t  };\n411\t}\n412\t\n413\t\n414\t## .agents/skills/netscript-harness/SKILL.md\n415\t\n416\tOriginal-byte SHA256:09fb65b00dc0c2a7f8df6aa8f815af91219b0ede2ab0e846bf09091b268eb215\n417\t\n418\t---\n419\tname: netscript-harness\n420\tdescription: >\n421\t  Operating model for NetScript harness runs. Use whenever the user says\n422\t  \"use harness\", references a harness run, asks about archetype/profile\n423\t  selection, run artifacts, resource aggregation, commit tracking, evaluator\n424\t  protocol, rescoping, or where a lesson/doctrine update should live.\n425\t---\n426\t\n427\t# NetScript Harness (V3) — Orchestration Skill\n428\t\n429\tThis skill coordinates harness-mode runs. The authoritative harness docs live under `.llm/harness/`;\n430\tthis skill tells you what to load and in what order.\n431\t\n432\tAgent lanes and their model bindings are configuration, not dogma — the single source of truth is\n433\t[`workflow/lane-policy.md`](../../../.llm/harness/workflow/lane-policy.md). Four rules are hard:\n434\t**generator-session ≠ evaluator-session**, **generator vendor family ≠ evaluator vendor family**,\n435\tthe **slice review gate** (no implementation lane self-certifies), and **complex/architecture tier\n436\tselection requires explicit owner or milestone-coordinator authority with a recorded rationale**.\n437\tWorkload tier, role, route, and fallbacks are recorded in the run dir's `supervisor.md`/`drift.md`.\n438\t\n439\t## When to Use\n440\t\n441\t- The user says `use harness` or asks for a harnessed run.\n442\t- Selecting archetypes, scope overlays, or gate sets.\n443\t- Tracking run artifacts, commits, or drift.\n444\t- Understanding evaluator protocol (PLAN-EVAL or IMPL-EVAL).\n445\t- Deciding where a lesson or doctrine update should live.\n446\t\n447\t## When Not to Use\n448\t\n449\t- For package/plugin architecture decisions — use `netscript-doctrine`.\n450\t- For JSR readiness audits — use `jsr-audit`.\n451\t- For frontend/framework-specific questions — use `deno-fresh` or the relevant domain skill.\n452\t\n453\t## Key Concepts\n454\t\n455\t| Concept                 | Meaning                                                                                                                                                                        |\n456\t| ----------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |\n457\t| **9-phase model**       | Bootstrap → Research → Plan & Design → Plan-Gate → Implement → Gate → Evaluate → Release → Close.                                                                              |\n458\t| **PLAN-EVAL**           | Conditional pre-implementation pass for complex/decision-heavy work; hard stop when selected.                                                                                  |\n459\t| **IMPL-EVAL**           | Mandatory final pass after implementation unless the owner explicitly waives it.                                                                                               |\n460\t| **Routing matrix**      | Five workload tiers and four coordinator tiers with role-specific routes; single source `workflow/lane-policy.md`.                                                             |\n461\t| **Privileged tiers**    | `complex` and `architecture` fail closed unless the owner or milestone coordinator explicitly authorizes the row and records why.                                              |\n462\t| **Slice review gate**   | Tier-A supervisor substantively reviews each landed slice before the sign-off commit; no lane self-certifies (A1).                                                             |\n463\t| **Supervisor identity** | Every run dir carries `supervisor.md` (model, session, host, paths, branch, baseline, lanes).                                                                                  |\n464\t| **Plan-Gate**           | Checklist (`gates/plan-gate.md`) that PLAN-EVAL enforces.                                                                                                                      |\n465\t| **Archetype**           | Package/plugin shape profile from `archetypes/ARCHETYPE-*.md`.                                                                                                                 |\n466\t| **Scope overlay**       | `SCOPE-frontend.md`, `SCOPE-service.md`, `SCOPE-docs.md`.                                                                                                                      |\n467\t| **Seed run**            | Planning-only run shape (`workflow/seed-run.md`): discovery → roadmap → owner-ratified one-shot GitHub filing. Use when the deliverable is a board (epics + issues), not code. |\n468\t| **Milestone cluster**   | Release run shape (`workflow/milestone-run.md`): Step 0 intake/cleanup/DAG → four topic orchestrators → direct-to-main leaves → canaries/stable cut.                           |\n469\t| **Run artifact**        | File in `.llm/runs/<run-id>/` that preserves state across sessions.                                                                                                            |\n470\t| **Debt**                | Recorded in `.llm/harness/debt/arch-debt.md`.                                                                                                                                  |\n471\t\n472\tFor a **supervisor run** (two or more capability-scoped phase groups), also read\n473\t`.llm/harness/workflow/supervisor.md` and `.llm/harness/workflow/escalation.md`, and track the\n474\tgroups in `phase-registry.md`.\n475\t\n476\tFor a **release milestone**, use the milestone-cluster route instead: read\n477\t`workflow/milestone-run.md` and `agent-milestone-orchestrator`. Do not apply the generic supervisor\n478\tintegration branch. Create the intake, inventory, DAG, cluster-state, and generated status\n479\tartifacts; Step 0 must validate before any implementation or evaluation lane starts.\n480\t\n481\tFor OpenHands or local-agent handoffs during a run, also read\n482\t`.llm/harness/workflow/agent-handoff.md` and `.agents/skills/openhands-handoff/SKILL.md`.\n483\t\n484\t## Workflow\n485\t\n486\tThe user may still write `profile: package`, `profile: docs`, or similar. In V3 that field is an\n487\tintent hint, not the final profile.\n488\t\n489\t| User hint                  | V3 selection                                                |\n490\t| -------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------- |\n491\t| `package`                  | identify `ARCHETYPE-1` through `ARCHETYPE-6`                |\n492\t| `plugin`                   | normally `ARCHETYPE-5`, unless sibling packages also change |\n493\t| `frontend`                 | affected archetype(s) plus `SCOPE-frontend.md`              |\n494\t| `service`                  | affected archetype(s) plus `SCOPE-service.md`               |\n495\t| `docs` or `knowledge-base` | `SCOPE-docs.md` plus any described archetypes               |\n496\t\n497\t1. Read `workflow/activation.md` and `workflow/run-loop.md`.\n498\t2. If resuming, read `.llm/runs/<run-id>/context-pack.md`.\n499\t3. Identify the target surface and select archetype + overlays.\n500\t4. Read `gates/archetype-gate-matrix.md` and `gates/plan-gate.md`.\n501\t5. Scaffold run artifacts from `templates/`.\n502\t6. Produce `research.md`, then `plan.md` with locked decisions.\n503\t7. Record Design checkpoint in `worklog.md`.\n504\t8. **For complex/decision-heavy work, run PLAN-EVAL in a separate session before implementation;\n505\t   otherwise record a justified `PLAN-EVAL: N/A`.**\n506\t9. Implement one commit slice at a time; push + comment on the draft PR (the commit trail) and keep\n507\t   `worklog.md`/`context-pack.md` current after each.\n508\t10. Run gates; record results in `worklog.md`.\n509\t11. **Run IMPL-EVAL (separate session) unless the owner explicitly waives it.**\n510\t12. Close: update `context-pack.md`, `arch-debt.md`, and promote lessons if warranted.\n511\t\n512\tWhen the opening diff is docs-only, proactively apply `ci:skip-e2e` to the draft PR and also apply\n513\t`ci:skip-scaffold` when scaffold-static is not applicable. Use `ci:full` as the escape hatch when\n514\tthe docs-only change must exercise the full CI surface. Record the selection in the opening phase\n515\tcomment so the evaluator can verify that the cheap lane is intentional.\n516\t\n517\t## Human Review Surface\n518\t\n519\tKeep user-facing plans, issues, PRs, benchmark reports, and handoffs compact. Lead with the\n520\tdecision, measured evidence, total footprint, risk, and next action. Link raw evidence instead of\n521\tcopying run machinery or phase ledgers into the review surface. If the original goal is not obvious\n522\ton one screen, consolidate and simplify before asking for coordination or review.\n523\t\n524\t## Agent Delegation Contract\n525\t\n526\tLane assignments and model bindings are configuration. Operational execution tiers still describe\n527\thow work is launched, while workload tiers and role bindings are defined once in\n528\t[`workflow/lane-policy.md`](../../../.llm/harness/workflow/lane-policy.md). Do not restate lane\n529\trouting here — defer to that file. The items below are the parts of the contract that hold as\n530\t**invariants** regardless of which lane implements:\n531\t\n532\t- **Generator ≠ evaluator (hard invariant).** The session that generates a plan or an implementation\n533\t  is never the session that evaluates it. Which model implements is configurable per run and\n534\t  recorded in `supervisor.md`/`drift.md`; the session separation is not.\n535\t- **Slice review gate (A1, hard invariant).** After any implementation lane lands a slice and its\n536\t  automated gates pass, the Tier-A supervisor substantively reviews the slice content before the\n537\t  sign-off commit — and the sign-off commit is the supervisor's, not the implementer's. This holds\n538\t  for every implementation tier (B Opus sub-agents, C Workflow-generated slices, D WSL Codex); no\n539\t  lane self-certifies. See `workflow/lane-policy.md` for the rule and `workflow/run-loop.md` for the\n540\t  step placement.\n541\t- **Evaluator route binding.** Select the evaluator from the workload tier and phase in\n542\t  `workflow/lane-policy.md`, skip candidates from the selected generator's vendor family, and record\n543\t  the complete route in the run. PLAN-EVAL is risk-selected. Re-steer the same evaluator and obey\n544\t  the tier-specific round/notification policy; do not substitute a global retry rule.\n545\t- **Privileged-row authority.** `complex` and `architecture` require explicit owner or\n546\t  milestone-coordinator authorization plus a recorded rationale. Never promote a task into either\n547\t  row from complexity inference, file count, or evaluator role alone; cap it at `feature` otherwise.\n548\t- **Tier-D mobile-visibility proof.** A Tier-D (WSL Codex) implementation slice is launched only via\n549\t  skills + `.llm/tools/agentic/` (never ad-hoc `wsl.exe`), and only when the run artifacts include\n550\t  the WSL worktree path, concrete Codex thread id, daemon-managed `remote-control` proof, and the\n551\t  follow-up steering command for that same thread. Without those, record the launch as\n552\t  failed/not-attached. Claude helpers (`codex:rescue`, `codex:codex-rescue`, `codex-companion.mjs`,\n553\t  internal `general-purpose` agents) are local tool surfaces, not daemon-attached Codex threads.\n554\t- **Per-slice trackability.** Every implementation slice is independently trackable: branch/worktree\n555\t  identity, agent/thread identity, files touched, tests run, commit hash, push status, and PR\n556\t  comment/status. Each slice commits, pushes, and comments on the draft PR before the next slice is\n557\t  considered complete (the draft-PR commit list + per-slice PR comments are the commit trail).\n558\t- **Green-gate merge bar.** Merge, publish, or release gates require all relevant tests green with\n559\t  required features intact. For catalog-related work, do not delete, skip, de-catalog, or bypass\n560\t  tests unless the evaluator verdict explicitly classifies the test as stale/irrelevant and the PR\n561\t  comment records the rationale.\n562\t- **Blocked-lane handling.** If an evaluator or implementation launch path is blocked, record the\n563\t  missing launch mechanism in `worklog.md`/`drift.md`, then proceed only with an owner-authorized\n564\t  fallback lane recorded in `supervisor.md`/`drift.md`.\n565\t\n566\t## Common Pitfalls\n567\t\n568\t- **Skipping a selected Plan-Gate** — for complex/decision-heavy work, implementation before\n569\t  PLAN-EVAL `PASS` is a process failure. Small/mechanical work records `PLAN-EVAL: N/A` first.\n570\t- **Self-evaluation** — The evaluator must be a separate session. The generator does not\n571\t  self-certify.\n572\t- **Wrong evaluator route** — select from the workload tier and phase, preserve separate sessions,\n573\t  and skip same-vendor-family candidates. Apply the provider order Claude → Codex → Google →\n574\t  OpenCode Go → Ollama → OpenRouter only among capabilities for the selected logical model. Paid\n575\t  routes require a fresh expense decision. Record every fallback and requested/observed identity.\n576\t- **Self-certifying a slice** — a green automated gate is not a sign-off. The Tier-A supervisor must\n577\t  substantively review the slice before the sign-off commit, for every implementation lane\n578\t  (`workflow/lane-policy.md` invariant 2). No lane self-certifies.\n579\t- **Treating lanes as dogma** — the \"Claude coordinates / OpenHands evaluates / Codex implements\"\n580\t  fixed-lane assertion is retired. Lanes are configuration; consult `workflow/lane-policy.md` and\n581\t  honor the run's recorded lane assignments/overrides in `supervisor.md`/`drift.md`.\n582\t- **False attached-agent claims** — for Tier-D slices, a Claude `codex:*` skill/helper is not a WSL\n583\t  Codex daemon thread. Require daemon status plus thread id before claiming the user can see or\n584\t  steer the subagent from phone/Desktop.\n585\t- **Carried-in plans as ground truth** — Re-baseline against current `main` before locking the plan.\n586\t- **Monolithic commits** — Commit by slice, not by monolith. Each slice has its own gate.\n587\t- **Raw root CLI noise as a verdict** — Package-quality check/lint/fmt evidence must come from the\n588\t  scoped wrappers, not raw root `deno fmt --check` over Markdown, generated targets, or legacy\n589\t  line-ending drift (which is not a package-quality verdict source unless the plan explicitly owns\n590\t  repo-wide formatting). The wrapper invocations and verdict rules live in\n591\t  `.agents/skills/netscript-tools`.\n592\t- **Scoped wrappers as a full quality verdict** — the scoped check/lint/fmt wrappers pass code\n593\t  containing `any` and honor inline `// deno-lint-ignore no-explicit-any`, so a green wrapper run\n594\t  does NOT prove doctrine/jsr compliance. For any slice touching `packages/**` or `plugins/**`, the\n595\t  Tier-A slice review MUST additionally run `deno task quality:scan` (fails on `any`+casting and\n596\t  host-side hardcoded plugin names — the two classes that reached `main` in #745) and\n597\t  `deno task arch:check` (doctrine fitness), plus the jsr-audit skill for package/plugin waves. A\n598\t  new `// deno-lint-ignore` or `as unknown as` introduced to green a wrapper is a review-blocking\n599\t  finding, not a pass. Reviewing on wrappers alone is the exact hole that let #745 merge.\n600\t\n601\t## Run Artifacts\n602\t\n603\t"}]}}

Verify run paths with a direct filesystem listing\n41\twhen needed:\n42\t\n43\t```powershell\n44\tdir /s /b \".llm\\runs\\<id>\" 2>&1\n45\t```\n46\t\n47\t## Resource Aggregation\n48\t\n49\tWhen external docs or examples matter:\n50\t\n51\t1. check `.resources/deps-docs/`,\n52\t2. check `.llm/tmp/docs/`,\n53\t3. fetch official/primary docs with available docs tooling,\n54\t4. save useful extracts to `.llm/tmp/docs/<source>-<topic>.md`,\n55\t5. cite the extract in the run artifact.\n56\t\n57\t## Evaluator Separation\n58\t\n59\tThere are **two** separate-session evaluator phases. Both use the workload tier's typed route from\n60\t`workflow/lane-policy.md`, and both must differ from the generator by session and vendor family.\n61\tProvider fallback is capability- and allowance-aware; no lane may self-certify.\n62\t\n63\t**PLAN-EVAL** (before implementation):\n64\t\n65\t- Runs only for genuinely complex or decision-heavy work; quick fixes record `PLAN-EVAL: N/A`.\n66\t- Runs in a separate session on the evaluator lane selected from `workflow/lane-policy.md`.\n67\t- Reads `evaluator/plan-protocol.md` + `gates/plan-gate.md`.\n68\t- Reads `research.md`, `plan.md`, and the `## Design` section.\n69\t- Writes `plan-eval.md`.\n70\t- Emits `PASS` or `FAIL_PLAN`.\n71\t- Follow the selected tier's PLAN-EVAL repair and escalation policy.\n72\t\n73\t**IMPL-EVAL** (final pass, after implementation):\n74\t\n75\t- Runs in a separate session on the evaluator lane selected from `workflow/lane-policy.md`.\n76\t- Generator writes `worklog.md`, `context-pack.md`, and `drift.md`.\n77\t- Evaluator reads `.llm/harness/evaluator/protocol.md`, the plan, worklog, context pack, drift, the\n78\t  draft-PR commit list + per-slice PR comments (the commit trail), selected archetype, overlays, and\n79\t  gate docs.\n80\t- Evaluator writes `evaluate.md` with `PASS`, `FAIL_FIX`, `FAIL_RESCOPE`, or `FAIL_DEBT`.\n81\t- Re-steer the same evaluator and follow the selected tier's IMPL-EVAL maximum and\n82\t  owner-notification point. Documentation is capped at two rounds.\n83\t\n84\t## Commit Trail\n85\t\n86\tWhen a run requires commits, the draft PR's commit list + per-slice PR comments **are** the commit\n87\ttrail — there is no `commits.md`. Per slice:\n88\t\n89\t1. commit by implementation slice (message names what the slice proves, not what it contains),\n90\t2. push the branch,\n91\t3. comment on the draft PR with slice scope, commit hash, and gate/test evidence,\n92\t4. update `worklog.md` + `context-pack.md` as part of the same slice (a slice whose commit does not\n93\t   touch the run dir is incomplete),\n94\t5. continue to the next slice.\n95\t\n96\tThe draft PR is opened in the same session as the first commit, so its commit list is live and\n97\treviewable from mobile without cloning or diffing.\n98\t\n99\t## Rescoping\n100\t\n101\tRescope when the real work is materially larger than the approved plan or when the selected\n102\tarchetype is wrong. Confirm with the user before expanding scope.\n103\t\n104\tRecord rescope evidence in `drift.md` with severity `significant` or `architectural`.\n105\t\n106\t## Where Lessons Belong\n107\t\n108\t| Content type                                  | Destination                          |\n109\t| --------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------ |\n110\t| Generic run mechanics                         | `.llm/harness/workflow/`             |\n111\t| Archetype-specific gates or false-done states | `.llm/harness/archetypes/`           |\n112\t| Stable repeated cross-run lessons             | `.llm/harness/lessons/`              |\n113\t| Package/plugin doctrine navigation            | `.agents/skills/netscript-doctrine/` |\n114\t| Deep domain expertise                         | a focused skill                      |\n115\t| Deferred doctrine violations                  | `.llm/harness/debt/arch-debt.md`     |\n116\t\n117\t## Quick Decision Tree\n118\t\n119\t```text\n120\tUser says \"use harness\"\n121\t  -> read workflow/activation.md and workflow/run-loop.md\n122\t  -> resuming? read context-pack.md\n123\t  -> deliverable is a board (epics/issues/milestones from a major feature, refactor, replan,\n124\t     or triage)? read workflow/seed-run.md — drafts only until owner ratification\n125\t  -> deliverable is a release milestone landed through multiple PRs? read workflow/milestone-run.md\n126\t     — Step 0 sweep/cleanup/DAG, four topic orchestrators, direct-to-main leaves\n127\t  -> deliverable is code for a single scoped change? stay on run-loop.md (a seed run here is\n128\t     ceremony)\n129\t  -> package/plugin? select ARCHETYPE-* and load netscript-doctrine\n130\t  -> frontend/service/docs? apply SCOPE-* overlay\n131\t  -> two or more phase groups? read workflow/supervisor.md + escalation.md, keep phase-registry.md\n132\t  -> read gate matrix + plan-gate.md\n133\t  -> complex/decision-heavy plan or genuine need for adversarial advice? run PLAN-EVAL (separate session); otherwise record PLAN-EVAL: N/A\n134\t  -> supervised? assign lanes per workflow/lane-policy.md (generator != evaluator session)\n135\t  -> slice landed + gates green? Tier-A supervisor reviews before the sign-off commit\n136\t  -> OpenHands/local/cloud handoff? read workflow/agent-handoff.md\n137\t  -> update run artifacts while working\n138\t  -> commit slice? push + comment on the draft PR (the commit trail); keep worklog/context-pack current\n139\t  -> discovered violation not fixed? update arch-debt.md\n140\t  -> evaluator is separate session\n141\t```\n142\t\n143\t## Reference Files\n144\t\n145\t| File                                            | Load when checklist                                                             |\n156\t| `.llm/harness/evaluator/plan-protocol.md`       | PLAN-EVAL instructions .agents/skills/netscript-doctrine/SKILL.md\n185\t\n186\tOriginal-byte SHA256:76ff1dcfc7eb00478784fcde4cd024cd4ac7f3ef5c63ea3824f5e084ab5c99a7\n187\t\n188\t---\n189\tname: netscript-doctrine\n190\tdescription: >\n191\t  Navigator for the NetScript Architecture Doctrine. Use for any work that\n192\t  writes, reviews, plans, or evaluates framework-layer code in packages/ or\n193\t  plugins/, and for docs that describe package/plugin architecture. Points to\n194\t  doctrine sections, archetypes, anti-patterns, fitness gates, layering rules,\n195\t  folder vocabulary, and architecture debt handling without duplicating the\n196\t  doctrine text.\n197\t---\n198\t\n199\t# NetScript Doctrine Skill\n200\t\n201\tThis skill is a navigator. The doctrine remains authoritative; read the relevant doctrine file\n202\tbefore changing or evaluating package/plugin code.\n203\t\n204\t## When to Use\n205\t\n206\t- Work touches or describes `packages/` or `plugins/`.\n207\t- Public `@netscript/*` exports need design or review.\n208\t- Package READMEs or JSR readiness need evaluation.\n209\t- Architecture debt entries need creation or closure.\n210\t- Harness plans/evaluations for package or plugin work.\n211\t\n212\t## When Not to Use\n213\t\n214\t- For app, service, frontend, or infrastructure work that does **not** change or evaluate\n215\t  package/plugin surfaces — use the relevant scope overlay or domain skill instead.\n216\t- For JSR publishability audits — use `jsr-audit`.\n217\t- For harness orchestration — use `netscript-harness`.\n218\t\n219\t## Key Concepts\n220\t\n221\t| Concept               | Meaning                                                                                            |\n222\t| --------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |\n223\t| **Doctrine**          | The 10-file architecture specification under `docs/architecture/doctrine/`. Append-only.           |\n224\t| **Archetype**         | One of six package shapes (1 Small Contract … 6 CLI/Tooling). Determines minimum viable structure. |\n225\t| **Axiom**             | A1–A14: foundational assumptions every package must respect.                                       |\n226\t| **Anti-Pattern (AP)** | AP-1..AP-20: common mistakes the evaluator checks for.                                             |\n227\t| **Fitness Gate (F)**  | F-1..F-18: automated checks per archetype.                                                         |\n228\t| **Layering**          | `domain` → `ports` → `application` → `adapters` → `presentation`.                                  |\n229\t| **Debt**              | Recorded violations in `.llm/harness/debt/arch-debt.md` with owner, target, and closing gate.      |\n230\t\n231\t### Archetype Selection\n232\t\n233\tUse `.llm/harness/archetypes/README.md` for the run-time decision tree.\n234\t\n235\t| Archetype            | Use                                                  |\n236\t| -------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------- |\n237\t| 1 Small Contract     | types, schemas, invariants, little runtime           |\n238\t| 2 Integration        | external systems, ports, adapters                    |\n239\t| 3 Runtime/Behavior   | long-running behavior, state, lifecycle, supervision |\n240\t| 4 Public DSL/Builder | fluent builders and definition APIs                  |\n241\t| 5 Plugin Package     | first-party `plugins/*` packages                     |\n242\t| 6 CLI/Tooling        | binaries, commands, scaffold/deploy flows            |\n243\t\n244\tIf two archetypes apply, choose the larger one and fold the smaller concern inside it.\n245\t\n246\t### Axiom Quick Reference\n247\t\n248\t| Axiom | Summary                                      |\n249\t| ----- | -------------------------------------------- |\n250\t| A1    | Public types first.                          |\n251\t| A2    | Simple over easy at published boundaries.    |\n252\t| A3    | 80 percent path is one chained call.         |\n253\t| A4    | Base classes are stub-only contracts.        |\n254\t| A5    | Composition over inheritance.                |\n255\t| A6    | Helpers must be justified.                   |\n256\t| A7    | Web Platform and `@std/*` first.             |\n257\t| A8    | One concern per folder; one reason per file. |\n258\t| A9    | Archetype drives package shape.              |\n259\t| A10   | Composition root over container.             |\n260\t| A11   | Name extension axes before abstraction.      |\n261\t| A12   | Durable workflows are state machines.        |\n262\t| A13   | Crash boundaries are explicit.               |\n263\t| A14   | Tests and gates preserve doctrine.           |\n264\t\n265\t### Layering Quick Reference\n266\t\n267\tSource: doctrine file 05.\n268\t\n269\t- `domain/` imports no package implementation.\n270\t- `ports/` imports `domain/` only.\n271\t- `application/` imports `domain/` and `ports/`; not `adapters/`.\n272\t- `adapters/` imports `domain/`, `ports/`, and external clients.\n273\t- `presentation/` imports `application/` and `domain/`; not `adapters/`.\n274\t- `testing/` imports public surface and testing adapters only.\n275\t\n276\t### Folder Vocabulary\n277\t\n278\tUse doctrine file 05 for definitions. Allowed role names include:\n279\t\n280\t`domain`, `ports`, `application`, `adapters`, `runtime`, `state`, `middleware`, `presets`,\n281\t`registry`, `diagnostics`, `presentation`, `testing`, `internal`, `tests`, and `examples`.\n282\t\n283\tGeneric `utils`, `helpers`, `common`, `lib`, and `interfaces` folders are doctrine findings unless a\n284\tmigration plan and debt entry explicitly cover them.\n285\t\n286\t## Workflow\n287\t\n288\t1. Identify the affected package/plugin surfaces.\n289\t2. Select the smallest archetype that fits (use `.llm/harness/archetypes/README.md`).\n290\t3. Read the relevant doctrine file(s) from the table below.\n291\t4. Apply the axiom and anti-pattern checks.\n292\t5. Verify layering and folder vocabulary.\n293\t6. Record any new or deepened debt.\n294\t\n295\t## Common Pitfalls\n296\t\n297\t- **Treating archetype as a folder template** — The archetype is a design constraint, not a\n298\t  checklist of folders to create. Every file must trace back to a concept named in the design\n299\t  checkpoint.\n300\t- **Duplicating doctrine into skills** — Skills point to doctrine files; they do not copy passages.\n301\t  The doctrine is the single source of truth.\n302\t- **Ignoring debt registry** — New violations must be recorded with a closing gate and owner.\n303\t  Unrecorded violations are `FAIL_DEBT`.\n304\t- **Using raw root CLI output as package evidence** — For package/plugin quality gates, check, lint,\n305\t  and formatting evidence should use the scoped wrappers under `.llm/tools/` with explicit roots,\n306\t  `--ext ts,tsx`, and generated/future-wave excludes. Use doctrine file 09 for the full fitness-gate\n307\t  rule.\n308\t\n309\t## Reference Files\n310\t\n311\t| File                                                                   | Load when                                   |\n312\t| ---------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------- |\n313\t| SHA256:e85a8e970fe53e26279203e7c77faf34a0a16c739b9e10d0ae83a2f29bf3e01f\n340\t\n341\t---\n342\tname: netscript-tools\n343\tdescription: NetScript repository tooling and validation guide. Use whenever a task involves repo-native Deno wrappers, rtk, raw git verification, gate evidence, PR comments, OpenHands automation, lock hygiene, root check/lint/fmt wrappers, doc-lint, publish dry-run, JSR audits, worktree synchronization, or deciding which command is a trustworthy verdict source.\n344\t---\n345\t\n346\t# NetScript Tools\n347\t\n348\tUse this skill when choosing commands or recording validation evidence in the NetScript repo. The\n349\tgoal is to avoid false green gates, stale git reads, and stale automation comments.\n350\t\n351\t## Tool Locations\n352\t\n353\tUse `.llm/tools/` for harness, evaluator, agent, and package-quality helper scripts. Fitness gates\n354\tlive under `.llm/tools/fitness/`; keep new doctrine, standards, JSR, and release-readiness helpers\n355\tthere unless they are product-facing CLI features.\n356\t\n357\tUse `tools/` only for reusable product/repository tooling that should ship as part of the public\n358\tdeveloper surface. Do not create a parallel fitness tree outside `.llm/tools/`.\n359\t\n360\t## Git And Status\n361\t\n362\tRTK can cache read-heavy git output. For ground-truth branch state, spawn git directly:\n363\t\n364\t```powershell\n365\tdeno eval 'const p = new Deno.Command(\"git\", { args: [\"status\", \"--short\"] }).outputSync(); await Deno.stdout.write(p.stdout); await Deno.stderr.write(p.stderr); Deno.exit(p.code);'\n366\tdeno eval 'const p = new Deno.Command(\"git\", { args: [\"ls-remote\", \"origin\", \"refs/heads/<branch>\"] }).outputSync(); await Deno.stdout.write(p.stdout); await Deno.stderr.write(p.stderr); Deno.exit(p.code);'\n367\t```\n368\t\n369\tUse `rtk` for exploratory read-heavy commands when token reduction matters. Do not use filtered RTK\n370\toutput as the sole PASS/FAIL source for gates.\n371\t\n372\t## Validation Wrappers\n373\t\n374\t### Durable gate receipts\n375\t\n376\tFor CI or long-lived harness workers, invoke allowlisted gates through\n377\t`.llm/tools/gates/run-gate.ts`; do not redirect an ad-hoc command and call the log a receipt. The\n378\trunner records the exact argv/cwd, immutable Git head, request hash, attempt, timing, real\n379\texit/signal, and bounded hashed output, then atomically replaces one JSON receipt. Same\n380\tinvocation-ID/same-hash calls share one lifecycle-local promise; ID reuse with another hash and\n381\tcapacity exhaustion fail closed. This is at-most-once only within the live broker lifecycle, not a\n382\tdistributed or crash-safe execution lock.\n383\t\n384\tThe runner resolves the repository's actual `HEAD` itself. A caller-supplied `--git-head` must\n385\tmatch; `--allow-git-head-mismatch <reason>` exists only for explicit diagnostic fixtures and records\n386\tboth values. Such a receipt cannot satisfy an evidence set. Receipts attest that one process ran in\n387\tone working tree at the recorded commit; they do not attest remote branch currency, source-tree\n388\tcleanliness, semantic gate sufficiency, or publication. Those remain separate gates.\n389\t\n390\t`PASS` certifies only the named command. Sufficiency is recomputed from exact receipts; never trust\n391\tor hand-edit a `sufficiency` string. Duplicate gate IDs, duplicate receipt IDs, contradictory\n392\treceipts, `SKIPPED`, `NOT_RUN`, missing/nonterminal receipts, or an immutable-head mismatch are\n393\tinsufficient. Package/plugin evidence also needs `quality-gate`, dependency evidence, and the\n394\tapplicable JSR audit. E2E tools keep their domain JSON reports; reference them with `--child-report`\n395\tinstead of double-wrapping or replacing them. A declared child report is part of the verdict: it\n396\tmust be freshly written, parse as JSON, and is hashed into the receipt. Missing, malformed, or stale\n397\tchild reports fail closed.\n398\t\n399\t**MANDATE (harness runs).** TS type-check / test / lint / format gate **evidence MUST come from the\n400\tstructured wrappers** — `.llm/tools/run-deno-check.ts`, `.llm/tools/run-deno-test.ts`,\n401\t`.llm/tools/run-deno-lint.ts`, and `.llm/tools/run-deno-fmt.ts` (invoked directly, or through the\n402\t`deno task check|test|lint|fmt:check` aliases that already wrap them). Raw root `deno check .` /\n403\t`deno fmt --check` / `deno lint` over the repo is **not a verdict source**: it walks Markdown,\n404\tgenerated output, scratch workspaces, and future-wave packages, producing false reds/greens. Use\n405\tthese wrappers for package/plugin quality checks:\n406\t\n407\t```powershell\n408\tdeno task check\n409\tdeno task test\n410\tdeno task lint\n411\tdeno task fmt:check\n412\tdeno run --allow-read --allow-run .llm/tools/run-deno-check.ts --root <path> --ext ts,tsx\n413\tdeno run --allow-read --allow-write --allow-run .llm/tools/run-deno-test.ts -- --allow-all <test-path>\n414\tdeno run --allow-read --allow-run .llm/tools/run-deno-lint.ts --root <path> --ext ts,tsx\n415\tdeno run --allow-read --allow-run .llm/tools/run-deno-fmt.ts --root <path> --ext ts,tsx\n416\t```\n417\t\n418\tThe source wrappers accept roots, extensions, include/exclude filters, and batching; the test\n419\twrapper accepts native test arguments after `--`. All emit structured compact output.\n420\tPackage-quality formatting gates target source TypeScript only (`--ext ts,tsx`) and exclude\n421\tgenerated output, scratch workspaces, and future-wave packages. All scoped wrappers fail closed when\n422\ttheir own selection is empty. Check/test/lint/fmt child crashes must appear structurally in JSON,\n423\ttest failures are grouped without losing affected test locations, and doc-lint preserves its child\n424\texit code; an empty findings array is not a PASS when a child failed.\n425\t\n426\t**Code-quality gate (required for `packages/**`/`plugins/**` waves).** The scoped check/lint/fmt\n427\twrappers above are necessary but NOT sufficient: they pass code containing `any`, honor an inline\n428\t`// deno-lint-ignore no-explicit-any`, and do not flag host-side hardcoded plugin-name coupling —\n429\tthe two doctrine violations that reached `main` in the beta.9 CLI wave (#745). Run\n430\t`deno task\n431\tquality:gate` (which chains `deno task quality:scan` + `deno task arch:check`) on any\n432\tframework-wave slice; `deno task quality:scan:repo` audits the whole `packages/cli/src`+`plugins`\n433\tsurface. The scanner (`.llm/tools/quality/scan-code-quality.ts`) fails on\n434\t`any`/`as unknown as`/`as any`, blanket `no-explicit-any` ignores, and host-side plugin-name checks;\n435\tthe only suppression is an inline `// quality-allow: <reason>` on the offending line (reason\n436\trequired, count reported, `--max-allow <n>` bounds it). CI mirrors this in `code-quality.yml`. A\n437\tgreen scoped wrapper is never a substitute for `quality:gate` on framework source.\n438\t\n439\t## Dependency Evidence\n440\t\n441\t**MANDATE (harness runs).** Any \"is this the latest / is this outdated / is this import dead / does\n442\tthe published surface install / any advisories\" question MUST be answered through the\n443\t`.llm/tools/deps/*` wrappers, exposed as `deno task deps:latest|outdated|why|audit|prod-install` —\n444\tnever a hand-rolled registry `curl`, and never `deno outdated --latest` for \"latest\" (it ignores\n445\tsemver and surfaces pre-release tags as latest). The canonical command map, the\n446\t`deno outdated\n447\t--latest` trap rationale, and the `catalog:` npm-only law live once in the\n448\t**netscript-deno-toolchain** skill — read it there; this skill does not restate those gotchas.\n449\t\n450\tFor targeted `deno check` commands that touch workspace code, include `--unstable-kv`:\n451\t\n452\t```powershell\n453\tdeno check --unstable-kv packages/<pkg>/mod.ts\n454\t```\n455\t\n456\tDo not run mutating root `deno task fmt` unless the user explicitly asks for repo-wide formatting\n457\tchanges. Use scoped formatting for owned files only.\n458\t\n459\t## Publish And Docs\n460\t\n461\tRelease automation keeps volatile JSR management endpoints in\n462\t`.llm/tools/release/config/endpoints.ts`; the non-agentic suite maintenance map is in\n463\t`.llm/tools/README.md`. Change the config value, not individual release feature modules.\n464\t\n465\tPackage publishability gates commonly use:\n466\t\n467\t```powershell\n468\tdeno doc --lint packages/<pkg>/mod.ts\n469\tdeno task doc:lint --root packages/<pkg> --pretty\n470\tdeno publish --dry-run --allow-dirty\n471\tdeno run --allow-read --allow-run --allow-env .llm/tools/fitness/audit-jsr-package.ts --root packages/<pkg> --text\n472\t```\n473\t\n474\t`deno task doc:lint` wraps `.llm/tools/run-deno-doc-lint.ts` — the structured runner for the\n475\t`deno doc --lint` publish bar. Prefer it when the raw `deno doc --lint` output is too noisy to\n476\tattribute per-entrypoint/per-file: it auto-discovers entrypoints from `deno.json` exports (lint the\n477\t**full export map**, not `mod.ts` alone, or sibling re-exports false-flag) and emits grouped JSON\n478\twith per-file attribution.\n479\t\n480\tRun `deno publish --dry-run --allow-dirty` from the package directory unless the checked-in task or\n481\tplan says otherwise. Treat raw dry-run output as authoritative for slow types; helper scripts may\n482\tovercount banner text.\n483\t\n484\t## OpenHands And PR Comments\n485\t\n486\tThe `gh` CLI is not reliably available. Use GitHub MCP tools for PR comments. For OpenHands, comment\n487\ton the PR with the exact trigger text needed for the run. The persistent\n488\t`<!-- openhands-agent-summary -->` comment can be stale; trust committed artifacts like\n489\t`plan-eval.md`, `evaluate.md`, and run logs over the summary comment.\n490\t\n491\tWhen posting automation requests, specify:\n492\t\n493\t- exact one-pass command\n494\t- repository root as working directory\n495\t- raw exit code requirement\n496\t- lock hygiene: do not commit `deno.lock` or source churn unless a reviewed fix requires it\n497\t\n498\t## Supervisor Automation (agentic tools)\n499\t\n500\t### Resource-hygiene symptoms\n501\t\n502\tIf a run failed and you do not know what is still running, `behavior.service-health` timed out,\n503\tports are already in use, or a `postgres-*` container exists that you did not start, begin with the\n504\tread-only `deno task maint:leak-check -- --slice-dir <run-dir> --worktree <worktree>`. It reports\n505\t"}]}}
