The full table includes separate Deep Research default and fallback columns.\n37\t\n38\tPure UI/UX specialist routing is an explicit `ui_ux` role; incidental UI changes stay on the normal\n39\timplementation role. Owner overrides require the typed override record and the exact grant in a\n40\trepo-relative `.llm/runs/**/worklog.md`. Never bypass the launcher directly: the override does not\n41\twaive separate-session or different-family evaluation.\n42\t\n43\tThe rest of the family (`launch-codex-slice`, `codex-resume`, `codex-status`, `codex-watch`,\n44\t`dispatch-openhands`, `openhands-status`, `gh-pr`) is indexed in `.llm/harness/workflow/tooling.md`.\n45\t`.llm/tools/agentic/` is the **only** interface for driving Codex — never ad-hoc `wsl.exe`. The\n46\tsuite is concern-grouped (`codex/`, `openhands/`, `github/`, `wsl/`, `claude/`, `runtime/` +\n47\t`runtime/cli/`, `lib/`); its `README.md` is the canonical map.\n48\t\n49\t**Monthly maintenance:** fleet route model IDs live in pinned Harness; transitional local provider\n50\tpresets live in `.llm/tools/agentic/config/models.ts`, tool versions in `config/versions.ts`,\n51\tendpoints in `config/endpoints.ts`; workload and coordinator bindings live in pinned Harness, while\n52\t`runtime/routing-policy.ts` resolves local provider availability. The \"Maintenance map\" table in the\n53\tsuite README says exactly where to change a model, version, policy, agent, or dep. A guard test\n54\t(`config/no-hardcoded-volatile_test.ts`) fails the suite if any of these values is hardcoded outside\n55\t`config/`.\n56\t\n57\t## Lock Hygiene\n58\t\n59\tDo not delete lock files or caches. Do not run `deno cache --reload` without approval. If automation\n60\tor validation mutates `deno.lock`, inspect the diff against the true branch base before accepting\n61\tit. PLAN-EVAL should not commit lock churn.\n62\t\n63\t## Worktree Sync\n64\t\n65\tIf push rejects due to a remote-only evaluator/bot commit, inspect the remote commit before\n66\tintegrating:\n67\t\n68\t```powershell\n69\tdeno eval 'const p = new Deno.Command(\"git\", { args: [\"show\", \"--stat\", \"--oneline\", \"--name-status\", \"origin/<branch>\"] }).outputSync(); await Deno.stdout.write(p.stdout); await Deno.stderr.write(p.stderr); Deno.exit(p.code);'\n70\t```\n71\t\n72\tIf the remote commit is unrelated and the local commit should sit on top, use a normal rebase onto\n73\t`origin/<branch>` and push. Do not discard user or bot commits.\n74\t\n75\t## Common Gotchas\n76\t\n77\t- `deno task check:packages` and `deno task check:plugins` may not exist; inspect root `deno.json`\n78\t  and use the available wrapper plus focused checks.\n79\t- Raw root `deno fmt --check` can include generated/scratch/future-wave files. Prefer scoped wrapper\n80\t  verdicts.\n81\t- `deno check .` can walk generated caches and deferred package surfaces. Prefer explicit roots and\n82\t  entrypoints.\n83\t- OpenHands may commit `deno.lock` churn; inspect before merging.\n84\t- The final full scaffold/plugins E2E command lives in the `netscript-cli` skill.\n85\t\n86\t\n87\t## .agents/skills/jsr-audit/SKILL.md\n88\t\n89\tOriginal-byte SHA256:b34002e35bd00656f1a68f54126eb7e316e9099ca6d4be92dc460a2d50ee7a79\n90\t\n91\t---\n92\tname: jsr-audit\n93\tdescription: \"This skill should be used when the user asks to 'audit for JSR', 'check JSR readiness', 'review JSR config', 'verify package for JSR', 'publish to JSR', 'prepare for JSR publishing', 'JSR compliance check', 'run JSR audit', or wants to ensure their Deno/TypeScript package meets JSR standards before publishing.\"\n94\tversion: 1.0.0\n95\t---\n96\t\n97\t# JSR Publishing Audit\n98\t\n99\tComprehensive audit guide for JSR (JavaScript Registry) compliance and scoring optimization. This\n100\tskill is a **required Plan-Gate input** for package/plugin waves — apply it to the planned public\n101\tsurface before slicing.\n102\t\n103\t## When to Use\n104\t\n105\t- Auditing a package for JSR readiness.\n106\t- Preparing a package for JSR publishing.\n107\t- Reviewing JSR config (`deno.json` or `jsr.json`).\n108\t- Checking for slow types before publish.\n109\t- Verifying documentation quality for JSR score.\n110\t\n111\t## When Not to Use\n112\t\n113\t- For package architecture decisions — use `netscript-doctrine`.\n114\t- For harness run orchestration — use `netscript-harness`.\n115\t- For non-JSR publishing (npm-only, private registries) — this skill is JSR-specific.\n116\t\n117\t---\n118\t\n119\t## Understanding the JSR Score\n120\t\n121\tJSR assigns packages a quality score from 0-100%, displayed with color coding:\n122\t\n123\t- **Red**: Below 60%\n124\t- **Orange**: 60-90%\n125\t- **Green**: 90%+ (target this)\n126\t\n127\tThe score directly influences search ranking. View any package's breakdown at\n128\t`jsr.io/@scope/package/score`.\n129\t\n130\t### The 9 Scoring Factors\n131\t\n132\t| Category            | Factor                                 | Impact |\n133\t| ------------------- | -------------------------------------- | ------ |\n134\t| **Documentation**   | Has README or module doc               | High   |\n135\t|                     | Has examples in README/module doc      | High   |\n136\t|                     | Has module docs in all entrypoints     | High   |\n137\t|                     | Has docs for most symbols              | High   |\n138\t| **Best Practices**  | No slow types                          | Medium |\n139\t|                     | Has provenance (SLSA attestation)      | Medium |\n140\t| **Discoverability** | Has description (≤250 chars)           | Low    |\n141\t| **Compatibility**   | At least one runtime marked compatible | Low    |\n142\t|                     | At least two runtimes compatible       | Low    |\n143\t\n144\t**Key insight**: Documentation carries the heaviest weight. Comprehensive JSDoc + README + `@module`\n145\ttags will score higher than perfect types with minimal docs.\n146\t\n147\t---\n148\t\n149\t## Audit Checklist\n150\t\n151\t| Check                | Command/Location         | What to Look For                            |\n152\t| -------------------- | ------------------------ | ------------------------------------------- |\n153\t| Required metadata    | `deno.json`              | `name`, `version`, `exports` fields         |\n154\t| Scoped package name  | `name` field             | Format: `@scope/package-name`               |\n155\t| Package description  | `description` field      | ≤250 characters for discoverability         |\n156\t| Valid exports        | `exports` field          | Entry points exist and are correct          |\n157\t| No slow types        | `deno publish --dry-run` | No slow type warnings                       |\n158\t| Clean file list      | `deno publish --dry-run` | Only intended files included                |\n159\t| ESM only             | Source files             | No CommonJS (`module.exports`, `require()`) |\n160\t| Module documentation | Entry point files        | `@module` JSDoc tag present                 |\n161\t| Symbol documentation | Exported symbols         | JSDoc with `@param`, `@returns`, `@example` |\n162\t\n163\t---\n164\t\n165\t## Audit Process\n166\t\n167\t### Step 1: Check Required Metadata\n168\t\n169\tRead `deno.json` (or `jsr.json`) and verify:\n170\t\n171\t```json\n172\t{\n173\t  \"name\": \"@scope/package-name\",\n174\t  \"version\": \"1.0.0\",\n175\t  \"description\": \"Concise package description under 250 characters\",\n176\t  \"exports\": \"./mod.ts\"\n177\t}\n178\t```\n179\t\n180\t**Package name rules:**\n181\t\n182\t- Must start with `@scope/`\n183\t- Lowercase letters, numbers, hyphens only\n184\t- 2-40 characters (excluding scope)\n185\t\n186\t**Config file choice:**\n187\t\n188\t- Deno projects: Use `deno.json`\n189\t- Node/Bun/other: Use `jsr.json`\n190\t- Never split config between both files\n191\t\n192\t### Step 2: Verify Exports\n193\t\n194\tCheck all entry points exist:\n195\t\n196\t```json\n197\t{\n198\t  \"exports\": {\n199\t    \".\": \"./mod.ts\",\n200\t    \"./utils\": \"./src/utils/mod.ts\"\n201\t  }\n202\t}\n203\t```\n204\t\n205\tFor each entry point, confirm:\n206\t\n207\t- File exists at specified path\n208\t- Exports are properly defined\n209\t- Type annotations are explicit (no slow types)\n210\t- Has `@module` JSDoc tag\n211\t\n212\t### Step 3: Audit Documentation Quality\n213\t\n214\t**Module-level documentation** (top of entry points):\n215\t\n216\t````typescript\n217\t/**\n218\t * A module providing string utilities for common transformations.\n219\t *\n220\t * @example\n221\t * ```ts\n222\t * import { camelCase } from \"@scope/cases\";\n223\t * camelCase(\"hello world\"); // \"helloWorld\"\n224\t * ```\n225\t *\n226\t * @module\n227\t */\n228\t````\n229\t\n230\t**Symbol documentation**:\n231\t\n232\t````typescript\n233\t/**\n234\t * Converts a string to camelCase format.\n235\t *\n236\t * @param input - The string to convert\n237\t * @returns The camelCase formatted string\n238\t *\n239\t * @example\n240\t * ```ts\n241\t * camelCase(\"hello world\"); // \"helloWorld\"\n242\t * camelCase(\"foo-bar-baz\"); // \"fooBarBaz\"\n243\t * ```\n244\t */\n245\texport function camelCase(input: string): string {\n246\t  // ...\n247\t}\n248\t````\n249\t\n250\t**JSDoc tags reference:**\n251\t\n252\t- `@param name - description` - Document parameters\n253\t- `@returns description` - Document return value\n254\t- `@example` - Runnable code examples (use triple backticks)\n255\t- `@deprecated message` - Mark deprecated APIs\n256\t- `@see SymbolName` - Cross-reference related symbols\n257\t- `@throws ErrorType` - Document thrown errors\n258\t- `{@link SymbolName}` - Inline links\n259\t- `{@linkcode SymbolName}` - Inline links with monospace\n260\t\n261\t### Step 4: Run Dry-Run Verification\n262\t\n263\t```bash\n264\tdeno publish --dry-run --allow-dirty\n265\t```\n266\t\n267\tThis reveals:\n268\t\n269\t- **Slow types**: Exports without explicit type annotations\n270\t- **File list**: Everything that would be published\n271\t- **Metadata errors**: Invalid name, version, etc.\n272\t\n273\t**The dry-run is a static gate, not proof of a working publish.** It never loads the module over the\n274\treal `https:`/`jsr:` graph, so it cannot catch the runtime-only publish failures listed in\n275\t[JSR publish gotchas](#jsr-publish-gotchas-netscript-grounded) — a green `--dry-run` is necessary\n276\tbut not sufficient. The authoritative post-publish check is the production CLI e2e (`e2e-cli-prod`),\n277\twhich installs and runs the published package over the remote graph.\n278\t\n279\t### Step 5: Audit the File List\n280\t\n281\tReview dry-run output for files that should NOT be published:\n282\t\n283\t**Common offenders:**\n284\t\n285\t- `.claude/`, `.zed/`, `.vscode/` - IDE settings\n286\t- `.github/`, `.gitlab/` - CI/CD configs\n287\t- `.mise.toml`, `.tool-versions` - Local tooling\n288\t- `coverage/` - Test coverage data\n289\t- `docs/`, `*.md` (except LICENSE/README) - Documentation\n290\t- `deno.lock` - Lock files\n291\t- `*.test.ts`, `**/test_utils/**` - Test files\n292\t- `sonar-project.properties`, `*.config.js` - Build configs\n293\t- `.env`, `*.local.*` - Local configuration\n294\t\n295\t### Step 6: Configure Publish Filtering\n296\t\n297\tUse `include` (whitelist) + `exclude` (filter):\n298\t\n299\t```json\n300\t{\n301\t  \"publish\": {\n302\t    \"include\": [\n303\t      \"LICENSE\",\n304\t      \"README.md\",\n305\t      \"deno.json\",\n306\t      \"mod.ts\",\n307\t      \"src/**/*.ts\"\n308\t    ],\n309\t    \"exclude\": [\n310\t      \"**/*.test.ts\",\n311\t      \"**/test_utils/**\"\n312\t    ]\n313\t  }\n314\t}\n315\t```\n316\t\n317\t**Why both?**\n318\t\n319\t- `include` whitelists only intended files\n320\t- `exclude` filters test files from `src/**/*.ts` glob\n321\t- `exclude` takes precedence when both match\n322\t\n323\t### Step 7: Verify Clean Output\n324\t\n325\tRun dry-run again. Only these should appear:\n326\t\n327\t- `LICENSE`\n328\t- `README.md`\n329\t- `deno.json`\n330\t- Entry point files (`mod.ts`, etc.)\n331\t- Source code (`src/**/*.ts` minus tests)\n332\t\n333\t---\n334\t\n335\t## Fixing Slow Types\n336\t\n337\tSlow types are exports without explicit type annotations. They prevent JSR from generating `.d.ts`\n338\tfiles efficiently and degrade npm compatibility by 1.5-2x slower type checking.\n339\t\n340\t### Function Return Types\n341\t\n342\t```typescript\n343\t// SLOW - inferred return type\n344\texport function greet(name: string) {\n345\t  return 'Hello, ' + name + '!';\n346\t}\n347\t\n348\t// FIXED - explicit return type\n349\texport function greet(name: string): string {\n350\t  return 'Hello, ' + name + '!';\n351\t}\n352\t```\n353\t\n354\t### Constants\n355\t\n356\t```typescript\n357\t// SLOW - inferred type\n358\texport const GLOBAL_ID = crypto.randomUUID();\n359\t\n360\t// FIXED - explicit annotation\n361\texport const GLOBAL_ID: string = crypto.randomUUID();\n362\t```\n363\t\n364\t### Class Properties\n365\t\n366\t```typescript\n367\t// SLOW - inferred property type\n368\texport class Config {\n369\t  timeout = 5000;\n370\t}\n371\t\n372\t// FIXED - explicit property type\n373\texport class Config {\n374\t  timeout: number = 5000;\n375\t}\n376\t```\n377\t\n378\t### Detection Commands\n379\t\n380\t```bash\n381\t# Catch slow types before publishing\n382\tdeno publish --dry-run\n383\t\n384\t# Validate documentation generation\n385\tdeno doc --lint\n386\t```\n387\t\n388\t**Note**: TypeScript 5.5's `isolatedDeclarations` mode produces code that automatically satisfies\n389\tJSR's no-slow-types requirement.\n390\t\n391\t---\n392\t\n393\t## Prohibited Patterns\n394\t\n395\tJSR enforces ESM-only architecture:\n396\t\n397\t| Prohibited       | Alternative                             |\n398\t| ---------------- | --------------------------------------- |\n399\t| `require()`      | `import`                                |\n400\t| `module.exports` | `export`                                |\n401\t| `declare global` | Module-scoped types                     |\n402\t| `declare module` | Module-scoped types                     |\n403\t| HTTP imports     | `jsr:`, `npm:`, `node:` specifiers only |\n404\t\n405\t---\n406\t\n407\t## Multiple Entry Points\n408\t\n409\tFor packages with multiple exports:\n410\t\n411\t```json\n412\t{\n413\t  \"exports\": {\n414\t    \".\": \"./mod.ts\",\n415\t    \"./providers\": \"./providers.ts\",\n416\t    \"./utils\": \"./src/utils/mod.ts\"\n417\t  }\n418\t}\n419\t```\n420\t\n421\tUsers import as:\n422\t\n423\t```typescript\n424\timport { main } from '@scope/pkg';\n425\timport { OpenAI } from '@scope/pkg/providers';\n426\timport { helper } from '@scope/pkg/utils';\n427\t```\n428\t\n429\tEach entry point should have its own `@module` JSDoc tag.\n430\t\n431\t---\n432\t\n433\t## Dependencies\n434\t\n435\tDeclare in `imports`:\n436\t\n437\t```json\n438\t{\n439\t  \"imports\": {\n440\t    \"@std/assert\": \"jsr:@std/assert@^1.0.0\",\n441\t    \"zod\": \"npm:zod@^3.22.0\"\n442\t  }\n443\t}\n444\t```\n445\t\n446\tSupported specifiers:\n447\t\n448\t- `jsr:@scope/pkg@version` - JSR packages\n449\t- `npm:package@version` - npm packages\n450\t- `node:module` - Node.js built-ins\n451\t\n452\t---\n453\t\n454\t## CI Publishing with Provenance\n455\t\n456\tGitHub Actions OIDC enables tokenless publishing with automatic SLSA provenance:\n457\t\n458\t```yaml\n459\tname: Publish to JSR\n460\ton:\n461\t  push:\n462\t    tags: ['v*']\n463\t\n464\tjobs:\n465\t  publish:\n466\t    runs-on: ubuntu-latest\n467\t    permissions:\n468\t      contents: read\n469\t      id-token: write # Required for OIDC and provenance\n470\t\n471\t    steps:\n472\t      - uses: actions/checkout@v5\n473\t      - uses: denoland/setup-deno@v2\n474\t        with:\n475\t          deno-version: v2.x\n476\t      - run: deno publish\n477\t```\n478\t\n479\t**Requirements for provenance:**\n480\t\n481\t1. Link package to GitHub repo in JSR settings\n482\t2. Include `id-token: write` permission\n483\t3. Provenance generated automatically\n484\t\n485\tNo API tokens needed - JSR uses GitHub OIDC authentication.\n486\t\n487\t---\n488\t\n489\t## Audit Report Template\n490\t\n491\tAfter completing the audit:\n492\t\n493\t```markdown\n494\t## JSR Publishing Audit Results\n495\t\n496\t| Check                | Status |\n497\t| -------------------- | ------ |\n498\t| Required metadata    | ✓ / ✗  |\n499\t| Scoped package name  | ✓ / ✗  |\n500\t| Package description  | ✓ / ✗  |\n501\t| Valid exports        | ✓ / ✗  |\n502\t| No slow types        | ✓ / ✗  |\n503\t| Clean file list      | ✓ / ✗  |\n504\t| ESM only             | ✓ / ✗  |\n505\t| Module documentation | ✓ / ✗  |\n506\t| Symbol documentation | ✓ / ✗  |\n507\t\n508\t### Estimated Score Impact\n509\t\n510\t- Documentation: X/4 factors\n511\t- Best Practices: X/2 factors\n512\t- Discoverability: X/1 factors\n513\t- Compatibility: X/2 factors\n514\t\n515\t### Files Published\n516\t\n517\t[List from dry-run]\n518\t\n519\t### Issues Found\n520\t\n521\t[List any problems]\n522\t\n523\t### Recommendations\n524\t\n525\t[Suggested fixes prioritized by score impact]\n526\t```\n527\t\n528\t---\n529\t\n530\t## Quick Commands\n531\t\n532\t```bash\n533\t# Full audit dry-run\n534\tdeno publish --dry-run --allow-dirty\n535\t\n536\t# Validate documentation\n537\tdeno doc --lint\n538\t\n539\t# Type check\n540\tdeno check **/*.ts\n541\t\n542\t# Lint\n543\tdeno lint\n544\t\n545\t# Format\n546\tdeno fmt\n547\t\n548\t# Publish (when ready)\n549\tdeno publish\n550\t```\n551\t\n552\t---\n553\t\n554\t## Learning the Publish Surface with `deno doc`\n555\t\n556\t`deno doc` renders a package's **public API surface** straight from its exports — the same surface\n557\tJSR scores and publishes. It is the cheapest way to inspect what a unit actually exposes before you\n558\taudit it, and the canonical internal-API tool: AGENTS.md instructs contributors to reach for\n559\t`deno doc <module>` / `deno doc --filter <symbol>` before broad implementation reads — **\"`deno doc`\n560\tis your friend\"** (see `AGENTS.md` Read Order). Use it to learn a surface; use `deno doc --lint` to\n561\thold it to the publish bar.\n562\t\n563\t### Inspect a surface\n564\t\n565\t```bash\n566\t# Full public surface of a workspace package or entrypoint\n567\tdeno doc packages/cli/mod.ts\n568\t\n569\t# Narrow to a single symbol (fastest way to confirm a signature)\n570\tdeno doc --filter scaffoldProject packages/cli/mod.ts\n571\t\n572\t# A remote/JSR module also works\n573\tdeno doc jsr:@std/assert\n574\t```\n575\t\n576\t`deno doc <module>` lists every exported symbol with its resolved type and JSDoc;\n577\t`deno doc --filter\n578\t<symbol>` prints just the matching declaration, which is the cheapest possible\n579\tread for confirming a type contract without opening source.\n580\t\n581\t### npm-dependency rendering\n582\t\n583\tWhen a unit re-exports or returns types from an `npm:` dependency, `deno doc` follows the npm\n584\tpackage's bundled type declarations and renders the resolved types inline — so the printed surface\n585\treflects what consumers see, npm types included. This is why an audit reads the real published shape\n586\trather than the local source signatures alone.\n587\t\n588\t#### npm-without-types workaround\n589\t\n590\tIf an `npm:` dependency ships **no type declarations**, `deno doc` cannot resolve those types and\n591\trenders them as `any` (and may warn about missing types), which understates or muddies the surface.\n592\tResolve it the same way you fix it for type-checking:\n593\t\n594\t- Add a typed shim via the `@types/<pkg>` companion in `imports` (e.g.\n595\t  `\"@types/node\": \"npm:@types/node@...\"`), or\n596\t- Pin a `// @deno-types=\"...\"` reference to a local/declared `.d.ts` at the import site.\n597\t\n598\tWith types resolved, `deno doc` (and `--lint`) render the real surface instead of `any`. Untyped npm\n599\tdeps that surface through your public exports are an audit finding, not just a doc cosmetic — they\n600\tweaken the published `.d.ts` and the no-slow-types posture (see\n601\t[Fixing Slow Types](#fixing-slow-types)).\n602\t\n603\t### JSX/TSX highlighting\n604\t\n605\tFor `.tsx` units (the `@netscript/fresh-ui`-style Preact/Fresh component packages), `deno doc`\n606\tparses and renders JSX/TSX entrypoints and highlights component and prop types in the emitted\n607\tsurface. You can point it at a `.tsx` entrypoint directly (e.g. `deno doc packages/fresh-ui/mod.ts`)\n608\tto inspect exported components the same way you would a plain `.ts` module.\n609\t\n610\t### `deno doc --lint` — the publish-quality bar\n611\t\n612\t`deno doc --lint` is the **documentation-quality gate** for the publish surface. The bar is **0\n613\tdiagnostics on the FULL export surface of each unit** — the same bar the Group 3 user-site reference\n614\tgeneration and the scorecard **A1** factor use. It flags two failure classes that block a clean\n615\tpublish:\n616\t\n617\t- `missing-jsdoc` — an exported symbol with no module/symbol documentation.\n618\t- `private-type-ref` — a public export annotated with a non-exported (private) type, which leaks an\n619\t  unrenderable type into the surface.\n620\t\n621\t```bash\n622\t# Per-unit publish bar: must be 0 diagnostics across all entrypoints\n623\tdeno doc --lint packages/<unit>/mod.ts\n624\t```\n625\t\n626\tWhen the raw output is too noisy to attribute per entrypoint or per file, use the structured runner\n627\t`.llm/tools/run-deno-doc-lint.ts`, which auto-discovers a unit's exports from `deno.json` and emits\n628\tgrouped per-entrypoint / per-file counts:\n629\t\n630\t```bash\n631\tdeno run --allow-read --allow-run .llm/tools/run-deno-doc-lint.ts --root packages/<unit> --pretty\n632\t```\n633\t\n634\t**Census (2026-06-18):** the canonical `deno task publish:dry-run` simulates **25** units and the\n635\treal publish denominator is **26** (the 25 E-wave units plus `@netscript/cli`; `@netscript/cli-e2e`\n636\tis `publish:false` and excluded). At baseline **25 / 26 units are already `deno doc --lint` clean**.\n637\tThe one current `--lint` debt is **`@netscript/fresh-ui`**, which fails with `private-type-ref`\n638\terrors where public component consts (`Accordion`, `Dialog`, `Drawer`, `Popover`, `Sheet`, `Tabs`,\n639\t`Tooltip`) are annotated with private `*Namespace` types. That is a **source/TypeScript fix**\n640\t(export the namespace types), not a doc edit, and is **out of scope for this skill** — track it as\n641\tframework debt.\n642\t\n643\t---\n644\t\n645\t## JSR publish gotchas (NetScript, grounded)\n646\t\n647\tThese are publish-surface traps established across NetScript release cuts. They are consolidated\n648\there as the canonical home; the `deno doc --lint` full-export-surface bar is covered above under\n649\t[`deno doc --lint`](#deno-doc---lint-the-publish-quality-bar) — do not restate it.\n650\t\n651\t**`deno publish --dry-run` is NOT publish-equivalent.** Every trap below passes a clean dry-run and\n652\tonly fails at real publish/install time, because the dry-run analyzes the local source graph rather\n653\tthan loading the module over the remote `https:`/`jsr:` graph a consumer resolves. Treat a green\n654\tdry-run as a static pre-check, never as the release verdict — the verdict is a real-publish\n655\tpreflight plus `e2e-cli-prod` against the published package.\n656\t\n657\t- **Embed assets as generated TypeScript constants, never import attributes or runtime file reads.**\n658\t  The JSR registry can reject `with { type: \"text\" }` or `with { type: \"json\" }` while building the\n659\t  module graph even when local `deno publish --dry-run` passes. A published module also runs over an\n660\t  `https:`/`jsr:` graph where `Deno.readTextFile(...)` /\n661\t  `fromFileUrl(new URL(..., import.meta.url))` have no package filesystem. Generate checked-in\n662\t  `*.generated.ts` constants and protect them with a regeneration-plus-diff freshness gate. Guard:\n663\t  `release:preflight` + `e2e-cli-prod`. This import-attribute ban is conditional and preserves the\n664\t  incident lineage: #138/#142 captured the alpha.6 half-publish, #143 recovered with string\n665\t  constants, and beta.10 partially published before the registry-side failure was tracked as\n666\t  [denoland/deno#35546](https://github.com/denoland/deno/issues/35546). Lift the ban only after that\n667\t  issue is fixed, merged, and released **and** an authenticated canary publish of a text-import\n668\t  probe is green.\n669\t- **Top-level `import.meta`/`fromFileUrl` crashes over https.** Same root cause as above but even\n670\t  for path resolution: a top-level `fromFileUrl(new URL(\"...\", import.meta.url))` throws when the\n671\t  module is imported over `https:`/`jsr:`. Make it lazy (resolve inside the function that needs it)\n672\t  and guard on `import.meta.url`'s protocol before touching the filesystem.\n673\t- **Self-referential subpath import trap (#188).** A package that imports its OWN bare specifier\n674\t  (e.g. `@netscript/x/sub` from inside `@netscript/x`) resolves at publish time against the LATEST\n675\t  PUBLISHED version on JSR, not the in-repo source — so the publish graph fails or silently binds an\n676\t  old version. Fix: use relative imports (`./sub.ts`) within a package; reserve bare specifiers for\n677\t  true cross-package edges.\n678\t- **JSR renders `@module` JSDoc, not README, by default (`readmeSource=jsdoc`).** The package page\n679\t  shows the entrypoint `@module` JSDoc rather than `README.md` when `readmeSource` is `jsdoc` (the\n680\t  default). If the README should be the page, toggle `readmeSource` to `readme` in the JSR UI or via\n681\t  a PATCH to the package config; otherwise ensure the `@module` doc carries the intended landing\n682\t  content.\n683\t- **Prerelease-only packages show `latest=null`.** A package with only prerelease versions has JSR\n684\t  `latest = null`, which renders a blank version badge/README on the package page. This self-heals\n685\t  at the first non-prerelease (stable) publish; no config fix is needed.\n686\t\n687\t## Known Limitations (2024-2025)\n688\t\n689\t- **Private packages**: Not yet available (most requested feature)\n690\t- **JSX publishing**: Not supported\n691\t- **HTTP imports**: Prohibited\n692\t- **Complex inference**: Libraries like Zod/ArkType may require `--allow-slow-types`\n693\t\n694\t\n695\t## .agents/skills/netscript-pr/SKILL.md\n696\t\n697\tOriginal-byte SHA256:486aa7f26376baaf1d467fff962a40500c8b0ca8ef1563151015e2e94392fee3\n698\t\n699\t---\n700\tname: netscript-pr\n701\tdescription: Author NetScript branches, pull requests, and the per-phase structured PR comments used by the harness (research / plan / plan-eval / impl / impl-eval / review summaries), plus umbrella↔sub-PR linking and the namespaced label taxonomy. Use this whenever you are about to create a branch, open or update a PR, post a phase summary comment, split an umbrella into sub-PRs, or apply status/type/area labels in this repo — even if the user just says \"open a PR\", \"push this\", \"comment the results\", or \"mark it ready for review\". Getting the branch name, PR body, and labels right the first time is what keeps the harness board from rotting.\n702\t---\n703\t\n704\t# NetScript PR Authoring\n705\t\n706\tConsistent branches, PR bodies, phase comments, and labels are what let the harness run as a\n707\tmachine: humans and agents read the same structured surface, and automation (Phase D labels +\n708\tProjects v2) keys off it. Sloppy PR hygiene is how a board becomes a graveyard. This skill is the\n709\thouse format.\n710\t\n711\t## Tooling note\n712\t\n713\t`gh` is **not** on PATH in this environment. Use the **GitHub MCP** tools for all PR/issue/label\n714\toperations (`create_pull_request`, `update_pull_request`, `add_issue_comment`, `issue_write`,\n715\t`pull_request_read`, label tools). For ground-truth branch/remote state, spawn git directly (RTK can\n716\tserve stale reads) — see the **netscript-tools** skill.\n717\t\n718\t## Branch naming\n719\t\n720\t`<type>/<slug>` — lowercase, kebab, no trailing dates. Types match the commit/PR taxonomy:\n721\t\n722\t- `feat/`, `fix/`, `chore/`, `docs/`, `refactor/`, `perf/`, `test/`.\n723\t- `plan/` — harness **seed runs** only (planning-only runs whose draft PR carries a board plan,\n724\t  never shippable code); see `.llm/harness/workflow/seed-run.md`.\n725\t- Sub-branches of an umbrella append a scope: `chore/deno-2.8-aspire-13.4-upgrade` (umbrella) →\n726\t  `chore/deno-2.8-aspire-13.4-upgrade-fresh` (sub). Keep the umbrella prefix so the relationship is\n727\t  visible in `git branch` and the PR list.\n728\t\n729\t## Umbrella vs sub-PR (wave-4/5 pattern)\n730\t\n731\t- **Umbrella PR**: the coordinating PR for a multi-slice effort. Body carries the slice checklist and\n732\t  links every sub-PR. Labelled `type:umbrella`.\n733\t- **Sub-PR**: one parallelizable slice on its own sub-branch, targeting the **umbrella branch** (not\n734\t  `main`). Labelled `type:sub-pr` and links back to the umbrella with `Part of #<umbrella>`.\n735\t- **Sequential vs parallel**: only split into sub-PRs when slices are genuinely independent. Slices\n736\t  that all edit `deno.json`/catalog are conflict-prone → keep them **sequential on one branch**, one\n737\t  commit per slice. (This is why PR #44's remediation is sequential, not fanned out.)\n738\t\n739\t## PR body template\n740\t\n741\tAlways structure the body like this so reviewers and automation find the same anchors:\n742\t\n743\t```markdown\n744\t## Summary\n745\t\n746\t<1–3 sentences: what this PR changes and why.>\n747\t\n748\t## Scope\n749\t\n750\t- Archetype / area: <e.g. tooling, packages/fresh, plugins/workers>\n751\t- Part of #<umbrella> · Sub-PRs: #<n>, #<n>   <!-- omit the side that doesn't apply -->\n752\t\n753\t## Slices\n754\t\n755\t- [x] S1 <slice> — <commit>\n756\t- [ ] S2 <slice>\n757\t\n758\t## Validation\n759\t\n760\t- `deno task check:<slice>` — <result>\n761\t- `deno task ci` / `e2e:cli scaffold.runtime` — <result + native-WSL note if applicable>\n762\t\n763\t## Harness\n764\t\n765\t- Run dir: `.llm/runs/<run-id>/`\n766\t- Phase: <research|plan|plan-eval|impl|impl-eval|review> — see phase comments below.\n767\t\n768\t## Drift / Debt\n769\t\n770\t- <DEBT_ACCEPTED rows or \"none\">\n771\t\n772\t## Definition of Done\n773\t\n774\t- [ ] <truthful, evidence-backed completion claim>\n775\t```\n776\t\n777\tKeep `Validation` honest: paste real results/exit codes; if a gate was skipped, say so. A green\n778\tcheckbox with no evidence is how false-green merges happen.\n779\t\n780\t`## Definition of Done` and `## Acceptance` are the authoritative PR-body checklist sections. Any\n781\tunchecked box beneath either heading fails close-gate. `## Slices` is progress tracking, and other\n782\tplanning, rollout, or explanatory checklists are non-authoritative; do not put a durable completion\n783\tclaim outside Definition of Done/Acceptance merely to avoid enforcement.\n784\t\n785\t## Linking issues (auto-close on merge) — MANDATORY\n786\t\n787\tA PR that **fully resolves** an issue MUST put a GitHub **closing keyword** in the **PR body** so the\n788\tmerge auto-closes the issue. Use one of: `Closes #N`, `Fixes #N`, `Resolves #N` (one per issue; put\n789\teach on its own line in the `## Scope` section).\n790\t\n791\t```markdown\n792\t## Scope\n793\t\n794\t- Archetype / area: packages/service\n795\t- Closes #234\n796\t- Part of #301   <!-- umbrella: reference only, do NOT close -->\n797\t```\n798\t\n799\t- **Bare `#N` and `Refs #N` / `Re #N` do NOT auto-close.** This is the exact defect that left 40+\n800\t  merged NetScript PRs with stale-open issues that had to be triaged by hand. If the work fully\n801\t  lands the issue, a closing keyword is not optional.\n802\t"}]}}

The PR template's `## Scope` is the canonical home.\n7\t\n8\t## Merge close-gate (#387)\n9\t\n10\tThe closing-keyword rule above wires the auto-close; this gate governs **when a PR carrying one may\n11\tmerge**. They are separate concerns: a keyword makes the merge *close* the issue, the close-gate\n12\tmakes sure the issue is *actually done* before that happens.\n13\t\n14\tThe machine convention is intentionally narrow:\n15\t\n16\t- A **close-gated PR** is any PR whose body contains a GitHub closing keyword for an issue:\n17\t  `Closes #N`, `Fixes #N`, or `Resolves #N` (including `closed`/`fixed`/`resolved` variants and full\n18\t  GitHub issue URLs).\n19\t- A **close-gated issue checkbox** is either:\n20\t  - any markdown checkbox inside an issue section whose heading contains `acceptance`, `definition\n21\t    of done`, `gate`, or `fitness gate`; or\n22\t  - any markdown checkbox anywhere in the issue body whose checkbox text starts with `gate:`.\n23\t- Ordinary planning, dependency, sub-issue, or rollout checklists outside those sections are **not**\n24\t  close-gated. If a checklist is acceptance, put it under an acceptance/gate heading.\n25\t- Only markdown checkboxes are close-gated and mirrorable. A plain-bullet `Acceptance` section has\n26\t  no mirrorable targets and therefore takes no `acceptance-evidence` block; convert its bullets to\n27\t  markdown checkboxes before adding a block.\n28\t- A checked acceptance/gate box must carry linked evidence in the issue, PR body, or PR phase comment\n29\t  (command output, run URL, CI job, or reviewer/evaluator comment). The automation catches unchecked\n30\t  boxes; the coordinator/evaluator verifies the evidence link quality before closing.\n31\t\n32\tFor the normal evidence-mirroring path, put one fenced `acceptance-evidence` YAML block in the PR\n33\tbody or a PR comment for each closing issue. Map each unchecked box by exact text after trimming, or\n34\tuse its one-based `box-index` when copying a long box would be unwieldy:\n35\t\n36\t````markdown\n37\t```acceptance-evidence\n38\tissue: 1170\n39\tentries:\n40\t  - box: \"Exit code is non-zero only when a current failure exists\"\n41\t    evidence: \"check-close-gate_test.ts fixture; report.ok gate\"\n42\t  - box-index: 2\n43\t    evidence: \"CI run URL\"\n44\t```\n45\t````\n46\t\n47\tThe explicit fields make punctuation—including em dashes in either the box or evidence—ordinary\n48\tdata. During one transition release the mirror still reads the legacy exact\n49\t`## Acceptance evidence` checked-list format and emits a deprecation warning naming the fenced\n50\tformat. New or edited evidence must use the fenced format.\n51\t\n52\tAn acceptance box containing the case-insensitive marker `[post-merge]` is visibly excluded from\n53\tthe merge gate. Use this only for facts that cannot exist until after merge, such as verifying the\n54\tpublished artifact. Verify it in a follow-up issue/PR comment and tick the issue box after merge;\n55\tdo not drop the PR's closing keyword merely to escape a structurally impossible pre-merge check.\n56\t\n57\tWhen the PR carries `status:ready-merge`, CI runs\n58\t`.llm/tools/validation/mirror-acceptance-evidence.ts` before the close-gate. The mirror validates the\n59\tcomplete mapping before it checks matched issue boxes and posts an issue provenance comment linking\n60\tthe PR. Unknown, duplicate, mismatched, or missing entries fail without mutation, naming the issue,\n61\tbox, comparison, and exact repair. The mirror fetches the current issue immediately before PATCH,\n62\tchecks the resulting body hash, retries once after a mid-air edit, and deduplicates its provenance\n63\tcomment by a stable marker. Use `--dry-run` to validate a real PR safely. Issues mentioned without a\n64\tclosing keyword (including epics/umbrellas referenced with `Part of`) are never mutated.\n65\t\n66\tThe mirror and checker fetch the PR, its labels/body/head, comments, and every closing issue live\n67\tthrough the API at execution time. Workflow event payloads supply only repository and PR\n68\tidentifiers: a job re-run retains its original event snapshot, but because every read is live, a\n69\tre-run after labeling works. `openhands-phase-eval.yml` listens to `labeled`, but `ci.yml` — which\n70\truns the acceptance mirror and close-gate — does not. After applying `status:ready-merge`, re-run the\n71\texisting CI workflow with `gh run rerun <run-id>` so those jobs read the live labels. Do not push\n72\tsolely to refresh them: a push moves the head and invalidates an existing IMPL-EVAL verdict. Every\n73\tpretty/JSON verdict prints `headSha`, `evaluatedAt`, and per-issue `number`, `updatedAt`, and\n74\t`bodySha256`, so reviewers can mechanically detect a verdict evaluated against an older issue body.\n75\t\n76\t### Close-gate operator playbook (read before touching a red close-gate)\n77\t\n78\t1. **Pre-flight before labeling.** Validate the evidence mapping locally — this catches every\n79\t   mapping error without burning a CI round trip:\n80\t   `GH_TOKEN=$(gh auth token) deno run --allow-env --allow-net .llm/tools/validation/mirror-acceptance-evidence.ts --repo <owner/name> --pr <n> --dry-run --pretty`\n81\t2. **Red close-gate? Establish currency first.** Run\n82\t   `deno task agentic:pr-checks -- --repo <owner/name> --pr <n> --pretty` before any forensic\n83\t   digging. A red whose classification is `superseded`/`stale-post-merge`/`cancelled` is not a\n84\t   current failure — do not chase it; let the queued current run land or trigger one. Only a\n85\t   `current-fail` close-gate is worth reading.\n86\t3. **Read the verdict's provenance.** Every gate/mirror output prints `headSha`, `evaluatedAt`,\n87\t   and per-issue `updatedAt` + `bodySha256`. If the snapshot predates your latest issue/body edit,\n88\t   the verdict is stale by construction — refresh the run instead of re-deriving the mapping.\n89\t4. **Closing keywords are the gate's only trigger source today.** `Refs #N` / `Part of #N` are\n90\t   never gated or mutated. Beware the side door: a manual **Development-sidebar link** makes\n91\t   GitHub auto-close the issue on merge *without* close-gate ever checking it (#1188 tracks\n92\t   closing that gap). Until #1188 lands, do not manually link issues you are not prepared to see\n93\t   auto-closed unverified — use body references instead.\n94\t5. **Post-merge-only facts** get the `[post-merge]` marker in the issue box (excluded from the\n95\t   merge gate with a notice), never a dropped closing keyword and never a merge-blocking box that\n96\t   only the merge itself can satisfy.\n97\t\n98\tA PR whose `status:` is `research`, `plan`, or `plan-eval` **MUST NOT be merged** — a plan-only\n99\tartifact set can never satisfy an implementation Definition-of-Done. Merge requires\n100\t`status:ready-merge`, and moving to `status:ready-merge` requires all three:\n101\t\n102\t1. **IMPL-EVAL PASS evidence** — the `[PHASE: IMPL-EVAL] [VERDICT: PASS]` phase comment from the\n103\t   evaluator (a separate session).\n104\t2. **DoD checklist complete** — every `- [ ]` in the PR body's Definition-of-Done is checked.\n105\t3. **Referenced-issue acceptance** — for **every** issue closed by the PR body, every close-gated\n106\t   issue checkbox is checked with linked evidence (command, run URL, CI job, or PR comment).\n107\t\n108\tThe exemplar failure is **#260**, closed with its `gate:e2e` box unchecked — the false-done this gate\n109\texists to stop.\n110\t\n111\tEnforcement lives at three points:\n112\t\n113\t1. **Coordinator/evaluator review** — before closing an issue by hand, or before merging a PR whose\n114\t   body carries `Closes #N` / `Fixes #N` / `Resolves #N`, verify every close-gated issue checkbox is\n115\t   checked and has linked evidence.\n116\t2. **CI close-gate** — `.github/workflows/ci.yml` runs the opt-in evidence mirror for\n117\t   `status:ready-merge`, then `.llm/tools/validation/check-close-gate.ts` on pull requests. The\n118\t   checker fails on unchecked PR-body Definition-of-Done/Acceptance boxes and independently fetches\n119\t   each issue closed by the PR body, failing if any close-gated issue checkbox remains unchecked;\n120\t   mirroring is not a bypass.\n121\t3. **PR-template/evaluator checklist** — the PR close-gate checkbox and IMPL-EVAL pass both confirm\n122\t   the same evidence before `status:ready-merge`.\n123\t\n124\tLegitimate exceptions require the explicit `status:close-gate-override` label. This label is an\n125\tauditable escape hatch, not a normal lifecycle stage: the coordinator must leave a PR comment naming\n126\tthe unchecked checkbox, the reason it is safe to close anyway, and the follow-up issue if work\n127\tremains. Do not use the override to hide missing implementation or missing evidence.\n128\t\n129\t## Epic / sub-issue standard\n130\t\n131\tProgram epics use one convention so the board and the run dir agree:\n132\t\n133\t- **Epic issue** — title `Epic: <name>`; labels `type:umbrella` + `epic:<slug>` + the relevant\n134\t  `area:`/`priority:` + a milestone; body = the epic's pillars, a sub-issue checklist, and links.\n135\t  **Never put a closing keyword on an epic** (see the close-gate above) — it closes **by hand** once\n136\t  every child is done.\n137\t- **Sub-issues** — real issues titled `[<epic-slug> S<n>] <slice>`, each carrying `epic:<slug>` (plus\n138\t  its own `area:`/`priority:`/milestone), linked to the epic by `Part of #<epic>` in the body.\n139\t  Exactly **one** PR resolves each sub-issue, and that PR's body carries `Closes #<child>`.\n140\t- GitHub-native sub-issue linkage is **unused repo-wide** today, so it is adopted **opportunistically**\n141\t  as a nice-to-have on top of the `Part of #<epic>` body text — it is not required and carries no\n142\t  migration burden.\n143\t\n144\t## Per-phase structured comments\n145\t\n146\tEach harness phase posts ONE comment so the PR timeline reads as a phase log. Lead with a status\n147\ttoken line so the (future) label automation can parse it:\n148\t\n149\t```markdown\n150\t**[PHASE: IMPL-EVAL] [VERDICT: CHANGES_REQUESTED]**\n151\t\n152\t<one-line headline>\n153\t\n154\t### Findings\n155\t1. **C1 …** — <what + where + fix>\n156\t...\n157\t\n158\t### Next\n159\t- <action + owner>\n160\t```\n161\t\n162\tPhases & their verdict vocabulary:\n163\t\n164\t- `RESEARCH` → (no verdict; summary only)\n165\t- `PLAN` → summary; gate is a separate `PLAN-EVAL` comment with `APPROVED` / `CHANGES_REQUESTED`.\n166\t- `IMPL` → summary of slices landed.\n167\t- `IMPL-EVAL` → `PASS` / `CHANGES_REQUESTED` (the gate that clears merge).\n168\t- `REVIEW` (augment/Fable) → advisory.\n169\t\n170\tThe evaluator must be a **separate session** from the generator (harness rule). Comment, don't edit,\n171\twhen acting as evaluator.\n172\t\n173\t## Draft-PR-on-start (harness)\n174\t\n175\tFor harnessed work, **opening the feature is opening a DRAFT PR** — in the **same session as the\n176\tfirst commit**, where the run-dir bootstrap commit (`.llm/runs/<run-id>/`) is the natural first\n177\tcommit. There is no \"work now, PR later\": the draft PR is the reviewable surface from the first\n178\tcommit onward, so the whole run reads from mobile without cloning or diffing.\n179\t\n180\tThe draft PR body carries these, live from the start (they are the anchors the close-gate and the\n181\tevaluator read):\n182\t\n183\t- an explicit, **checkable Definition-of-Done** (the close-gate reads these boxes);\n184\t- the run-dir path `.llm/runs/<run-id>/`;\n185\t- the slice checklist (`## Slices`);\n186\t- the **live commit list** — the draft PR's own commit list plus per-slice comments are the commit\n187\t  trail (there is no `commits.md`);\n188\t- drift / debt (`## Drift / Debt`).\n189\t\n190\t## Draft ↔ ready\n191\t\n192\t- Open multi-slice work as a **draft** PR; per-commit CI (Phase C tier 1) runs on drafts but is\n193\t  non-blocking.\n194\t- Flip to **ready for review** only when the slice checklist is complete and IMPL-EVAL is expected\n195\t  to pass. Readiness alone never triggers OpenHands. For an explicitly cloud-driven fallback, add\n196\t  `openhands` before the transition; automation then enters `status:impl-eval` and dispatches once\n197\t  unless `impl-eval:skip` is present. Before the transition, select at most one optional\n198\t  `eval:model:qwen` or `eval:model:glm` override. Do not also dispatch a manual `@openhands-agent`\n199\t  run for the same head.\n200\t\n201\t## Label taxonomy (namespaced — Phase D)\n202\t\n203\tExactly one `status:` at a time; `type:`/`area:`/`ci:` as needed. The single-status rule is what lets\n204\ta board column reflect reality.\n205\t\n206\t- `type:` — `umbrella`, `sub-pr`, `chore`, `feat`, `fix`, `docs`, `refactor`, `perf`, `test`\n207\t- `status:` — `triage` (incoming issues), `research`, `plan`, `plan-eval`, `impl`, `impl-eval`,\n208\t  `augment-review`, `ci-fail`, `ready-merge`, `shipped` (terminal), `close-gate-override`\n209\t- `area:` — `cli`, `fresh`, `fresh-ui`, `plugins`, `auth`, `deps`, `aspire`, `tooling`, `database`,\n210\t  `kv`, `sdk`, `service`, `config`, `telemetry`, `ai-core`, `plugin-ai`, `docs`\n211\t- `priority:` — `p0` (release blocker), `p1`, `p2`, `p3`\n212\t- `ci:` — `skip-e2e`, `skip-scaffold`, `full` (manual overrides for the path-filtered CI);\n213\t  `impl-eval:skip` — attributed escape hatch for an opted-in ready-for-review IMPL-EVAL;\n214\t  `eval:model:qwen`, `eval:model:glm` — mutually exclusive override for the next status-driven\n215\t  OpenHands phase; `docs-eval:skip` is a deprecated compatibility label; `gate:` — `e2e`, `jsr`\n216\t- OpenHands fallback — `openhands` explicitly opts into the cloud runner; PR readiness never\n217\t  implies it.\n218\t- `epic:` — groups every issue/PR belonging to a program epic (e.g. `epic:ai-stack`,\n219\t  `epic:deployment`); the epic's own umbrella issue carries `type:umbrella`.\n220\t- `wave:` — scheduling band that drives the **milestone** (see below): `v1`, `v1-min`, `defer`.\n221\t- flags — `rfc` (an RFC tracking issue/PR; see `rfcs/README.md`), `breaking`, `good first issue`,\n222\t  `help wanted`\n223\t\n224\tThe machine-readable label set (names + colors + descriptions) is mirrored in\n225\t[`.github/labels.yml`](../../../.github/labels.yml) for a future label-sync workflow; keep the two in\n226\tsync. Add labels there first — never delete an existing label (it strips the label off live issues).\n227\t\n228\t**Every open issue and PR carries at least one `type:` and one `area:` label, and every open issue\n229\tcarries a milestone.** New issues land with `status:triage` (the issue forms apply it automatically)\n230\tuntil triaged.\n231\t\n232\tSource of truth stays the harness run artifacts under `.llm/runs/`. Labels + Projects v2 are a\n233\t**view and a trigger**, not the record. When you advance a phase, move the `status:` label in the\n234\tsame action you post the phase comment, so the board never lags the timeline.\n235\t\n236\t### Stage-label lifecycle\n237\t\n238\tThe `status:` labels are a **lifecycle**, not free-form tags — a harness PR walks exactly one path,\n239\tmoving the label in the **same action** as each phase comment:\n240\t\n241\t`status:research → status:plan → status:plan-eval → status:impl → status:impl-eval →\n242\tstatus:augment-review` (optional advisory) `→ status:ready-merge`\n243\t\n244\tExactly one `status:` at every point in the open lifecycle. On a completed close, atomically remove\n245\tthe phase label and apply terminal `status:shipped`; do this for issues closed by a merged PR and for\n246\tcompleted issues closed by hand. A not-planned/wontfix close is the sole exception: remove the phase\n247\tlabel and leave the closed item with no `status:` label, because it did not ship. Reopening an item\n248\trequires restoring exactly one appropriate non-terminal phase label. Enforcement exists because a\n249\tpractice audit found ~50% non-compliance: recent merged PRs shipped with **zero** labels or with\n250\t`status:` frozen mid-lifecycle (merged still at `status:plan`). The close-gate keys off\n251\t`status:ready-merge`, so a missing or frozen `status:` before merge is a merge hazard, not a cosmetic\n252\tlapse.\n253\t\n254\t`status:close-gate-override` is outside the normal lifecycle and must be used only for the audited\n255\texception path described in the close-gate section.\n256\t\n257\t> Phase D (the Action that enforces single-status, syncs label→Project column, and fires the right\n258\t> workflow per status) is deferred to the repo-process-automation umbrella. Until it lands, apply\n259\t> labels manually per this taxonomy so the future automation has clean data.\n260\t\n261\t## Milestones (release mapping)\n262\t\n263\tAssign every open issue and PR to the explicit release that owns it: `0.0.2` through `0.0.9`, or\n264\t`Backlog / Triage` when it is accepted but unscheduled. The `wave:*` labels are scheduling bands and\n265\tmay span several releases; they do not determine a milestone. Epics and umbrellas use the cut in\n266\twhich their coordinated scope is expected to complete. Keep milestone scope in sync with the live\n267\troadmap and issue board rather than inferring it from a wave label.\n268\t\n269\t## Issue and PR templates\n270\t\n271\tContributors and agents file through the GitHub-native forms — do not hand-author raw issues when a\n272\tform fits:\n273\t\n274\t- Issue forms live in [`.github/ISSUE_TEMPLATE/`](../../../.github/ISSUE_TEMPLATE/): `bug_report`,\n275\t  `feature_request`, `rfc_proposal`, `documentation`, plus `config.yml` (contact links). They\n276\t  auto-apply the incoming labels (`bug`/`enhancement`/`rfc`/`documentation` + `status:triage`).\n277\t- The PR body follows [`.github/pull_request_template.md`](../../../.github/pull_request_template.md),\n278\t  which is the PR body template above plus the closing-keyword `## Scope` convention.\n279\t- Substantial/breaking changes go through the RFC process in\n280\t  [`rfcs/README.md`](../../../rfcs/README.md) before implementation.\n281\t\n282\tThis skill is the single canonical reference for NetScript's GitHub process — label taxonomy,\n283\tmilestones, closing-keyword rule, templates, and RFC entry point. `CONTRIBUTING.md` is the\n284\thuman-facing mirror; `.github/labels.yml` is the machine-readable label set.\n285\t\n286\t## Path-filter awareness (Phase C)\n287\t\n288\tWhen opening a docs-only PR, proactively apply `ci:skip-e2e`. Also apply `ci:skip-scaffold` when the\n289\tchange does not need the scaffold-static gate. These labels make the intended cheap lane explicit\n290\teven when path classification would auto-skip the jobs. Apply `ci:full` instead when a docs-only PR\n291\tmust exercise the full CI surface; it is the precedence-winning escape hatch. Don't fight the\n292\tfilter by editing workflows per PR.\n293\t\n294\t\n295\t## .agents/skills/deno/SKILL.md\n296\t\n297\tOriginal-byte SHA256:258e202bdc90412ebff65d837df53e09c2e237ecc552a50b167f68babd4f62a9\n298\t\n299\t---\n300\tname: deno\n301\tdescription: \"Diagnostic instrument for the Deno runtime and toolchain. Reach for it the moment something is wrong and you do not know why. USE FOR: a process that exits silently or hangs, a dev server that 'died', type errors the editor does not show (or shows and the CLI does not), 'Import X not a dependency', unresolved/stale modules, permission denials, leaking or hanging tests, lockfile and dependency-age failures, reading a package's API. DO NOT USE FOR: Fresh/Preact/islands/Tailwind authoring (follow the project's Fresh documentation and conventions); NetScript release/dependency policy (follow the repository's checked-in release and dependency tooling). INVOKES: deno check, deno info, deno task, deno test, deno lint, deno fmt, deno doc, deno coverage, deno bench, deno clean, deno add/install/outdated/why, bash. Verified on Deno 2.9.3 — re-verify flags on other versions.\"\n302\t---\n303\t\n304\t# Deno Toolchain Diagnostics\n305\t\n306\t**The runtime will tell you what is wrong. Ask it.** Every symptom below has a command that answers\n307\tit in seconds. Guessing, re-running, and re-reading source is what burns hours.\n308\t\n309\tEverything here was executed on **Deno 2.9.3** (`deno --version`). Behaviour marked _(2.9)_ is\n310\tversion-sensitive — re-check with `deno <cmd> --help` before trusting it on another release.\n311\t\n312\t## The exit-code trap — read this first\n313\t\n314\t**A pipeline reports the _last_ stage's status.** Verified: `deno check main.ts` exits 1; but\n315\t\n316\t```bash\n317\tdeno check main.ts | tail -1   # -> prints \"error: Type checking failed.\"  and exits 0\n318\t```\n319\t\n320\tThis is true whether you pipe on the command line **or bake the pipe into a `deno.json` task** — a\n321\ttask defined as `\"check\": \"deno check main.ts | tail -1\"` reports exit 0 while type checking fails.\n322\t\n323\tFixes, in order of preference: **do not pipe**; or `set -o pipefail`; or read `${PIPESTATUS[0]}`.\n324\t\n325\tThree more sources of a lying exit 0, all verified:\n326\t\n327\t| Command                                         | Silent success                                            | Why                                                                                                                                           |\n328\t| ----------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |\n329\t| `deno check <path excluded by deno.json>`       | prints `Warning No matching files found.` and **exits 0** | `deno lint` / `deno fmt --check` / `deno test` instead print `error: No target files found.` and exit **1**. `deno check` is the odd one out. |\n330\t| `deno run file.ts` on a file with type errors   | runs and exits 0                                          | `deno run` does **not** type-check. Use `deno check`, or `deno run --check`.                                                                  |\n331\t| `deno info file.ts` with an unresolvable import | prints `(resolve error)` and **exits 0**                  | Read the tree, not the code.                                                                                                                  |\n332\t\n333\t**Rule: verify the artefact, never the exit code.** Did the file appear, did the port open, did the\n334\ttype error text vanish. Generators, installers and builds all exit 0 while producing nothing.\n335\t\n336\tIn an initialized NetScript project, use\n337\t`deno run --allow-read --allow-run .llm/tools/run-deno-check.ts --root <path> --pretty`. It\n338\tenumerates explicit inputs, reports `selection.filesSelected`, and fails when selection is empty,\n339\tclosing the excluded-file exit-0 trap.\n340\t\n341\t## CLI command reference\n342\t\n343\t| Task                                                       | Command                                                            |\n344\t| ---------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------ |\n345\t| Type-check the whole project (honours `include`/`exclude`) | `deno check`                                                       |\n346\t| Type-check one file + its graph                            | `deno check path/to/file.ts`                                       |\n347\t| Type-check a file the config excludes                      | `deno check --no-config path/to/file.ts`                           |\n348\t| Also type-check npm/remote deps                            | `deno check --all <file>`                                          |\n349\t| Show the resolved module graph                             | `deno info <file-or-specifier>`                                    |\n350\t| Machine-readable graph + on-disk paths                     | `deno info <specifier> --json`                                     |\n351\t| Show `DENO_DIR` and every cache location                   | `deno info` (no args)                                              |\n352\t| Who pulls in a package                                     | `deno why <pkg>`                                                   |\n353\t| List tasks                                                 | `deno task` (no args)                                              |\n354\t| Lint                                                       | `deno lint` / `--compact` / `--json` / `--rules`                   |\n355\t| Format check (diff + exit 1)                               | `deno fmt --check`                                                 |\n356\t| Run tests                                                  | `deno test [--filter <s>] [--fail-fast[=N]] [--reporter=dot]`      |\n357\t| Tests touching a file / changed files                      | `deno test --related=<file>` / `deno test --changed`               |\n358\t| Catch leaks _(2.9: opt-in)_                                | `deno test --sanitize-ops --sanitize-resources [--trace-leaks]`    |\n359\t| Coverage                                                   | `deno test --coverage=cov` then `deno coverage cov --detailed`     |\n360\t| Benchmarks                                                 | `deno bench [--filter <s>] [--json]`                               |\n361\t| Public API of a module                                     | `deno doc <specifier>` / `--filter <Symbol>` / `--lint <files...>` |\n362\t| Re-fetch one dependency                                    | `deno check --reload=npm:<pkg> <file>`                             |\n363\t| See what a cache wipe would cost                           | `deno clean --dry-run`                                             |\n364\t| Debugger                                                   | `deno run --inspect-brk <file>` / `--inspect-wait`                 |\n365\t| Op-level trace                                             | `deno run --trace-ops=<op,op> <file>`                              |\n366\t| Verbose runtime log                                        | `deno run -L debug <file>`                                         |\n367\t\n368\t**Always prefix `NO_COLOR=1`** when you will read or grep the output. Deno emits ANSI escapes even\n369\twhen stdout is redirected to a file — verified with `od -c` on `deno doc > file`.\n370\t\n371\t## Key workflows\n372\t\n373\t### Debugging issues\n374\t\n375\tMatch the symptom, run the command, read the named field.\n376\t\n377\t**\"The process exits with no output\" / \"the dev server died silently.\"**\n378\t\n379\t1. Stop backgrounding it. Run it in the foreground and keep stderr:\n380\t   `deno task dev > /tmp/dev.log 2>&1; echo \"exit=$?\"` — never `| tee`, that eats the exit code.\n381\t2. Read the exit code. `0` = nothing kept the event loop alive (an aborted `AbortController`, a\n382\t   server that never listened). `1` = uncaught error, and the text is on **stderr**. `124` = your\n383\t   own `timeout`. `137` = SIGKILL / OOM.\n384\t3. Port conflicts look like\n385\t   `error: Uncaught (in promise) AddrInUse: Address already in use\n386\t   (os error 98)` — stderr only.\n387\t   If you discarded stderr you saw \"silent\".\n388\t4. Still opaque: `deno run -L debug <entry>`. It logs every module load, V8 code-cache hit, and ends\n389\t   with `received module evaluate Ok(())` — that line proves the module evaluated, so the problem is\n390\t   after evaluation, not in resolution.\n391\t5. Attach a debugger: `--inspect-brk` (break at first line) or `--inspect-wait` (wait for a client,\n392\t   then run). Both default to `127.0.0.1:9229`.\n393\t\n394\t**\"It hangs.\"** `deno run --trace-ops=op_timer_schedule,op_read <file>` — bare `--trace-ops` is a\n395\tflood, always pass the comma-separated filter. Look for a `Dispatched` with no matching `Completed`.\n396\t\n397\t**\"Type errors, but the editor is fine\" (or the reverse).** The editor is the LSP: it checks only\n398\topen files. `deno check` follows real module graphs and obeys `deno.json`.\n399\t\n400\t- `deno check` (no args) checks **every** project file — the editor never does this.\n401\t- `deno check file.ts` checks _only that file and what it imports_. A task defined as\n402\t  `deno check main.ts` proves nothing about the other 200 files.\n403\t- If `deno check <file>` says `Warning No matching files found.`, the path is **excluded** in\n404\t  `deno.json`. Re-run with `--no-config` to check it anyway.\n405\t- Errors inside npm/remote packages are skipped by default; `deno check --all <file>` includes them.\n406\t\n407\t**\"Type errors from generated code\" (Prisma client, codegen output).** Generated directories are\n408\talmost always in `exclude`, so plain `deno check` skips them and `deno check <that file>` exits 0\n409\tsilently. Do both of these before touching the generator:\n410\t\n411\t```bash\n412\tNO_COLOR=1 deno check --no-config path/to/generated/client.ts   # the real diagnostics\n413\tNO_COLOR=1 deno info path/to/generated/client.ts                # which .d.ts is actually loaded\n414\t```\n415\t\n416\t**`Import \"zod\" not a dependency and not in import map`.** This is not a framework bug. It is\n417\tresolution. `deno check` already prints the fix as a hint (`try running deno add npm:zod`). Confirm\n418\twith the graph:\n419\t\n420\t```bash\n421\tNO_COLOR=1 deno info src/thing.ts\n422\t# └── Import \"zod\" not a dependency ...  (resolve error)     <- unresolved edge\n423\t# └── npm:/zod@4.4.3 (4.35MB)                                <- resolved edge\n424\t```\n425\t\n426\t`deno info <specifier> --json` gives `npmPackages[\"zod@4.4.3\"].localPath` — the exact directory in\n427\tthe cache. Read the real `.d.ts` there instead of guessing at the types.\n428\t\n429\t**\"A permission error.\"** Deno names the capability _and_ the exact resource:\n430\t`NotCapable: Requires env access to \"HOME\", run again with the --allow-env flag`. Grant the\n431\tnarrowest thing that satisfies it — `--allow-env=HOME`, `--allow-read=./data`,\n432\t`--allow-net=127.0.0.1:8000`. Scoping is literal: `--allow-net=example.com` does **not** permit\n433\tbinding `0.0.0.0:8124`. In non-interactive/agent runs add `--no-prompt` (or `DENO_NO_PROMPT=1`) so a\n434\tmissing permission fails immediately instead of blocking on an invisible prompt.\n435\t`DENO_TRACE_PERMISSIONS=1` adds stack traces.\n436\t\n437\t**\"A test hangs or leaks.\"** _(2.9)_ **Ops and resource sanitizers are off by default since 2.8** —\n438\ta test that leaves a timer or an open file passes. Turn them on:\n439\t\n440\t```bash\n441\tdeno test --sanitize-ops --sanitize-resources          # or DENO_TEST_SANITIZE_OPS=1\n442\tdeno test --sanitize-ops --trace-leaks                 # adds the stack where the leak started\n443\t```\n444\t\n445\t`--trace-leaks` turns `A timer was started in this test, but never completed` into the exact source\n446\tline. Narrow the run with `--filter <substring-or-regex>`, `--fail-fast`, `--related=<file>` (note\n447\tthe `=`; a space errors), or `--changed` (needs a git repo). `--no-run` type-checks the test files\n448\twithout executing them. `--reporter=dot` for compact output.\n449\t\n450\t**\"It worked yesterday\" / stale-cache suspicion.**\n451\t\n452\t```bash\n453\tNO_COLOR=1 deno info                       # DENO_DIR + remote / npm / gen cache paths\n454\tdeno check --reload=npm:<pkg> <file>       # re-fetch ONE dep; `npm:` alone reloads all npm\n455\tDENO_DIR=/tmp/deno-probe deno check <file> # reproduce against a pristine cache, destroys nothing\n456\tdeno clean --dry-run                       # \"Removed <dir> (728938 files, 13.88GB)\" then aborts\n457\t```\n458\t\n459\tPrefer an isolated `DENO_DIR` over `deno clean`. `deno clean` wipes the whole shared cache;\n460\t`deno clean -e <files>` retains what those files need.\n461\t\n462\t"}]}}
