Name subpaths explicitly:\n33\t  `deno doc jsr:@std/testing/mock`.\n34\t- **If there is no root export it errors — and the error lists every subpath.**\n35\t  `deno doc\n36\t  jsr:@std/testing` fails with `Unknown export '.'` followed by\n37\t  `./bdd ./mock ./snapshot ...`. That failure is the cheapest way to enumerate a package's\n38\t  entrypoints.\n39\t- **It is enormous.** `deno doc npm:zod` is 4,355 lines. Always `deno doc --filter <Symbol> <spec>`.\n40\t\n41\t`deno doc <dir>` documents every file in that directory (including `deno.json`, oddly).\n42\t`deno doc --lint <file...>` requires explicit files — it will not infer them from an export map.\n43\t\n44\t### Dependencies, lockfile, and dependency age\n45\t\n46\t```bash\n47\tdeno add npm:zod jsr:@std/path      # writes deno.json imports + deno.lock\n48\tdeno install                        # install everything the config declares\n49\tdeno outdated [--latest] [--update] # inventory; --update rewrites specifiers\n50\tdeno why <pkg>                      # provenance\n51\tdeno ci                             # frozen install from the lockfile, for CI\n52\t```\n53\t\n54\t`--frozen` turns lockfile drift into a real failure with a diff:\n55\t`error: The lockfile is out of date. Run 'deno install --frozen=false' ...`.\n56\t\n57\t_(2.9, unstable)_ **`--minimum-dependency-age` defaults to 24 hours.** A version published minutes\n58\tago fails to resolve with\n59\t`A newer matching version was found, but it was not used because it was\n60\tnewer than the specified minimum dependency date`.\n61\tAlias `--min-dep-age`; values are minutes (`120`), ISO-8601 durations (`P2D` — years are rejected),\n62\tor a date (`2025-09-16`); `0` disables. The durable fix is `\"minimumDependencyAge\"` in `deno.json`,\n63\tnot a flag on one command.\n64\t\n65\t**Two traps around that flag, both verified:**\n66\t\n67\t1. **`deno task` does not accept `deno run` flags.** `deno task --minimum-dependency-age=0 dev`\n68\t   fails with `error: unexpected argument '--minimum-dependency-age' found`. `deno task` has its own\n69\t   small flag set (`--cwd`, `--eval`, `--filter`, `--jobs`, `--if-present`, `--recursive`, `-c`).\n70\t   Anything after the task **name** is appended to the task's command line, not parsed by Deno.\n71\t   Options: put the flag in the task, use `deno task --eval \"deno run --min-dep-age=0\n72\t   main.ts\"`,\n73\t   or run the underlying `deno run` directly.\n74\t2. **`deno x` does not pass it to the child.** `deno x` applies the flag while resolving/installing\n75\t   the binary, then re-execs the tool as a _separate process_ whose argv is\n76\t   `deno run --allow-all file:///…/deno_x_cache/npm-<tool>/…` — confirmed by `ps`. The flag is\n77\t   absent, so anything the tool resolves at runtime falls back to the default 24-hour policy. Set\n78\t   `minimumDependencyAge` in `deno.json` when a `deno x` tool needs a different policy.\n79\t\n80\t## Important rules\n81\t\n82\t- **Never conclude from an exit code you obtained through a pipe.** `cmd | tail` reports `tail`.\n83\t- **`deno run` does not type-check.** \"It ran\" is not evidence about types. Only `deno check` is.\n84\t- **`deno check <excluded-file>` exits 0 with a warning.** If output is\n85\t  `Warning No matching files\n86\t  found.`, you checked nothing — re-run with `--no-config`.\n87\t- **Reach for `deno info` before reading source.** Nearly every \"framework bug\" that turns out to be\n88\t  an import-map, version, or cache problem is visible in one `deno info` tree.\n89\t- **Prefix `NO_COLOR=1`** on anything you will parse — Deno colours output even when redirected.\n90\t- **Enable test sanitizers explicitly** _(2.9)_. Green tests do not mean no leaks.\n91\t- **Do not run `deno clean` to test a stale-cache hypothesis.** Use `DENO_DIR=<tmpdir>` — it proves\n92\t  the same thing and destroys nothing. Treat `deno clean` and unscoped `--reload` as needing\n93\t  approval; prefer `--reload=<specifier>`.\n94\t- **Grant the narrowest permission the error names**, never `-A`, when diagnosing.\n95\t- **Add `--no-prompt`** in unattended runs so a permission gap fails instead of hanging.\n96\t- Prefer `docs.deno.com` and `deno <cmd> --help` over recalled flags. Several 2.9 behaviours\n97\t  (sanitizers off by default, the 24-hour dependency-age default) invert older documentation.\n98\t\n99\t### Verification notes\n100\t\n101\t- `deno lint --fix` is documented as fixing \"rules that support it\", but on 2.9.3 it modified no\n102\t  file across four attempts (`prefer-const`, `no-unused-vars`, `eqeqeq`, `no-inferrable-types`) and\n103\t  still exited 1. **Re-read the file after running it; do not assume it fixed anything.**\n104\t- `deno test` flags `--repeats`, `--retry`, `--shard`, `--update-snapshots` were confirmed present\n105\t  in `--help` but not exercised.\n106\t- The `deno x` child-process finding was verified by inspecting the live process tree, not by\n107\t  triggering a real age rejection inside a tool's runtime resolution.\n108\t- All `deno check` / `deno info` / `deno test` / `deno doc` behaviours above were reproduced on a\n109\t  scratch project. The claim that the **editor** checks only open files is standard LSP behaviour\n110\t  and was _not_ re-verified here — the CLI half of the comparison was.\n111\t\n112\t\n113\t## docs/architecture/doctrine/02-public-surface.md\n114\t\n115\tOriginal-byte SHA256:32299c4df2c00800bc78e56d0974c31b7d2cdcb13fed582c08d62b21120cb454\n116\t\n117\t# The Public Surface — `mod.ts` as Enterprise Contract\n118\t\n119\tAxioms governed: A1, A2, A3.\n120\t\n121\tThe published surface is the only part of a package the outside world\n122\tsees. It is also the only part the JSR audit, the doc score, and the\n123\ttype checker enforce. Every other doctrine concern (folder shape,\n124\thelpers, base classes) follows from getting the surface right.\n125\t\n126\t## What `mod.ts` is\n127\t\n128\t`mod.ts` is the package's contract with its callers. It is:\n129\t\n130\t- a *manifest* of named exports,\n131\t- a *map* into the package's documentation,\n132\t- a *boundary* across which dependencies become public.\n133\t\n134\tIt is not:\n135\t\n136\t- a kitchen sink of every internal symbol,\n137\t- a barrel file forwarding from twenty other modules,\n138\t- a backwards-compatibility shim layer.\n139\t\n140\tA reader who opens `mod.ts` should be able to learn what the package\n141\tdoes, what verbs it offers, and how to start using it without leaving\n142\tthe file. If `deno doc <package>` is unreadable, `mod.ts` is broken.\n143\t\n144\t## Surface axioms in operational form\n145\t\n146\t### A1 — Design types first\n147\t\n148\tBefore writing a builder or a runtime class, write the types the\n149\tcaller will see. This is a literal step in the workflow:\n150\t\n151\t1. Sketch the public types in `types.ts` (or `mod.ts` if small).\n152\t2. Write the README's \"Quick start\" block as if those types existed.\n153\t3. Only then, implement.\n154\t\n155\tThis is *Readme Driven Development* applied to the package boundary.\n156\tTom Preston-Werner: *until you've written about your software, you\n157\thave no idea what you'll be coding*.\n158\t\n159\t### A2 — Simple over easy\n160\t\n161\tA new caller arriving at a package gets:\n162\t\n163\t- One named entry point (`createX`, `defineX`, or `startX` —\n164\t  pick the verb based on the archetype, see\n165\t  [`06-archetypes.md`](./06-archetypes.md)).\n166\t- One paragraph of explanation in the JSDoc.\n167\t- One runnable example showing the smallest real use.\n168\t\n169\tWe do not optimize for the sentence \"look how easy this is.\" We\n170\toptimize for the sentence \"I read the surface and I now know what\n171\tit does.\"\n172\t\n173\t### A3 — 80% in one chained call\n174\t\n175\tThe smallest real example reads as:\n176\t\n177\t```ts\n178\timport { defineSaga } from '@netscript/sagas';\n179\t\n180\tconst saga = defineSaga('user-registration')\n181\t  .initially((s) => s.on('UserRegistered').transitionTo('welcoming'))\n182\t  .during('welcoming', (s) => s.on('WelcomeEmailSent').complete())\n183\t  .build();\n184\t```\n185\t\n186\tAdvanced configuration unfolds via additional `withX()` methods. It\n187\tdoes not appear as required arguments to the entrypoint.\n188\t\n189\t## Naming conventions\n190\t\n191\t| Verb           | When to use                                                         |\n192\t| -------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------- |\n193\t| `defineX(...)` | Returns a frozen *definition*. No runtime work yet. Builders.        |\n194\t| `createX(...)` | Constructs and returns a *runtime* object owning state and IO.       |\n195\t| `startX(...)`  | Constructs *and* starts a runtime; returns a `{ stop() }` handle.    |\n196\t| `useX(...)`    | Hook-style accessor inside a request/render/handler scope (Fresh).   |\n197\t| `withX(...)`   | Builder method only. Always returns a new builder; never mutates.    |\n198\t\n199\tAnything that does not match should be renamed or removed.\n200\t\n201\t### Type naming\n202\t\n203\t- **Types and interfaces**: `PascalCase`. No `I` prefix. No `_T` suffix.\n204\t  `WorkerJob`, not `IWorkerJob` or `WorkerJobT`.\n205\t- **Plain object types** are preferred over `interface` for data; we use\n206\t  `interface` for ports/contracts that may be implemented by multiple\n207\t  classes.\n208\t- **Discriminated unions** for branching shapes:\n209\t  `type ResourceKind = 'service' | 'plugin' | 'package';`\n210\t- **Branded primitives** for identifiers that should not interchange:\n211\t  `type CorrelationId = string & { readonly __brand: 'CorrelationId' };`\n212\t- **Result-shaped returns** where failure is a normal outcome:\n213\t  `type Result<T, E> = { ok: true; value: T } | { ok: false; error: E };`.\n214\t  Use `throw` for programmer errors; use `Result` for expected failures\n215\t  the caller must consider.\n216\t\n217\t### Method and function naming\n218\t\n219\t- Verbs read as imperatives: `start`, `stop`, `apply`, `inspect`,\n220\t  `verify`, `summarize`.\n221\t- A boolean returner is a question: `isReady`, `hasMigrations`,\n222\t  `canRetry`. We do not use `getIsReady`.\n223\t- A side-effect-free getter omits `get`: `name`, `kind`, `version`.\n224\t  We use `get` only when computation is non-trivial or async.\n225\t\n226\t### Subpath exports\n227\t\n228\tSubpath exports are first-class. They split a large surface across\n229\tnamed entries that callers can import individually:\n230\t\n231\t```jsonc\n232\t// deno.json\n233\t{\n234\t  \"name\": \"@netscript/sagas\",\n235\t  \"exports\": {\n236\t    \".\": \"./mod.ts\",\n237\t    \"./builders\": \"./src/builders/mod.ts\",\n238\t    \"./testing\": \"./src/testing/mod.ts\",\n239\t    \"./adapters/postgres\": \"./src/adapters/postgres/mod.ts\"\n240\t  }\n241\t}\n242\t```\n243\t\n244\tWhy subpaths matter:\n245\t\n246\t- **Bundle size.** Callers pay only for what they import.\n247\t- **Layering.** Adapter-specific code (Postgres, Redis) does not\n248\t  pollute the default surface.\n249\t- **Testing helpers** are separated from production exports; the JSR\n250\t  audit does not demand example coverage on testing-only utilities.\n251\t\n252\tRule: if a `mod.ts` would re-export more than ~20 named symbols,\n253\t*split into subpaths*. Twenty is a soft heuristic; the hard rule is\n254\t\"can a reader skim the export list and recognize each name's role?\"\n255\t\n256\t## Documentation as part of the surface\n257\t\n258\tEvery public export has:\n259\t\n260\t1. A JSDoc summary line (one sentence, present tense).\n261\t2. A `@param` for each non-obvious argument.\n262\t3. A `@returns` describing the shape and ownership of the result.\n263\t4. At least one `@example` block with idiomatic TypeScript that\n264\t   compiles. The example is the manual.\n265\t\n266\t```ts\n267\t/**\n268\t * Define a worker job that the runtime can dispatch by name.\n269\t *\n270\t * @example\n271\t * ```ts\n272\t * import { z } from 'zod';\n273\t *\n274\t * declare const emailClient: { send(message: { to: string }): Promise<void> };\n275\t *\n276\t * const sendEmail = defineJob('send-email')\n277\t *   .input(z.object({ to: z.string().email() }))\n278\t *   .handle(async (job) => {\n279\t *     await emailClient.send(job.input);\n280\t *   })\n281\t *   .build();\n282\t * ```\n283\t */\n284\texport function defineJob(name: string): JobBuilder {\n285\t  // ...\n286\t}\n287\t```\n288\t\n289\tThe JSR doc score caps at 100. The doctrine treats anything below as\n290\tbroken.\n291\t\n292\t### Application context in examples\n293\t\n294\tAn example often needs something the application owns: a router, a database client, a contract, a\n295\tmodel provider. Declare it visibly at the top of the fence with a typed stand-in, so a reader\n296\tcopying the example sees exactly what they must supply:\n297\t\n298\t```ts\n299\timport { createRPCHandler, type ServiceRouter } from '@netscript/service';\n300\t\n301\tdeclare const router: ServiceRouter;\n302\t\n303\tconst rpcHandler = createRPCHandler(router);\n304\t```\n305\t\n306\t- **Every name is bound.** Package exports are imported the way a consumer imports them; only\n307\t  application-owned values are declared.\n308\t- **Stand-ins use real types.** Prefer an exported type from the documented package, a sibling\n309\t  `@netscript/*` package, or the upstream package the example wires\n310\t  (`import type { Hono } from 'hono'`). Declare a minimal local shape only for data the application\n311\t  itself defines. Never `any`.\n312\t- **About three lines at most.** If an example needs more stand-ins, trim it to what the documented\n313\t  symbol demonstrates instead of declaring more context.\n314\t- **No suppressions.** No `@ts-ignore`, `@ts-expect-error`, `as any`, or fence attributes that skip\n315\t  checking.\n316\t\n317\t`deno task docs:jsdoc-examples` compiles every published example against its public specifiers; an\n318\tundeclared name fails it.\n319\t\n320\t## What does not belong on the surface\n321\t\n322\t- **Implementation classes that exist only because we needed to share\n323\t  state between functions.** Internal classes are not exported.\n324\t- **Test fakes and mocks.** They live under `./testing` and are\n325\t  imported through that subpath.\n326\t- **Constants that change between versions** (formatter strings,\n327\t  error message templates). They are private; if callers need to\n328\t  branch on them, expose a typed enum-like.\n329\t- **`I*`-style interface aliases for class fields.**\n330\t- **Re-exports of upstream packages** (`zod`, `@std/*`). If a caller\n331\t  needs Zod, they import Zod. We do not become a vendor.\n332\t\n333\t## Stability levels\n334\t\n335\tA published export is one of:\n336\t\n337\t- **Stable** — semver covered. Breaking changes require a major bump.\n338\t- **Experimental** — declared via `@experimental` JSDoc tag. May\n339\t  break in a minor release. Never used by another `packages/*`\n340\t  package.\n341\t- **Internal** — not exported. Even if it is reachable through a\n342\t  subpath, the JSDoc declares `@internal` and the JSR audit excludes\n343\t  it from the public score.\n344\t\n345\tThe default is **stable**. Experimental status is opt-in and time-\n346\tlimited.\n347\t\n348\t### Deprecation and removal convention\n349\t\n350\tA stable exported symbol scheduled for removal uses this machine-readable JSDoc form:\n351\t\n352\t```ts\n353\t/**\n354\t * @deprecated{removal: 0.2} Use `createCurrentThing` instead.\n355\t */\n356\texport function createLegacyThing(): LegacyThing;\n357\t```\n358\t\n359\tThe value is the first `major.minor` release line in which the symbol must no longer be exported.\n360\tDeprecation must therefore land before that line, with migration guidance in the same JSDoc tag.\n361\tThe public-surface diff gate warns when an export carrying this tag remains present at or beyond its\n362\tdeclared removal line. Removing the symbol is still a semver-major surface change and requires an\n363\texplicit major declaration until the stable-line policy makes that release intent authoritative.\n364\t\n365\t## Sanctioned exception: slow-types for oRPC-bound packages\n366\t\n367\tOne exception to the \"no slow types\" bar is sanctioned and\n368\tdocumented. Packages that bind a NetScript contract or service\n369\tseam to oRPC's real `@orpc/contract` builder types (`oc`,\n370\t`Schema`, `AnySchema`, the `ContractProcedureBuilderWith*`\n371\tfamily) MAY set `--allow-slow-types` on their publish dry-run.\n372\t\n373\tRationale: those upstream types are `declare`d internals, so\n374\t`deno doc --lint` inherently emits `private-type-ref` \"slow\n375\ttypes\" diagnostics that only `--allow-slow-types` waives.\n376\tBinding to the real builder types is the *sound* implementation\n377\t— it removed a phantom `'~orpc': any` erasure in the base\n378\tcontract seam. Demanding zero slow types would force that\n379\tunsound erasing cast back in. Soundness wins; the slow-types\n380\tdiagnostic is the accepted cost.\n381\t\n382\tThe boundary is strict: this exception applies *only* to\n383\toRPC-bound packages — `packages/contracts` and any plugin\n384\t`-core`/`services` package that extends the base contract or\n385\tservice seam (`BaseContractRoute` / `BaseContractOutputRoute`\n386\tfrom `@netscript/contracts`). Any other package that sets\n387\t`--allow-slow-types` is a finding and must carry a debt entry.\n388\tThe `audit-jsr-package.ts` fitness gate encodes this allow-list:\n389\tsanctioned packages report the slow-types diagnostic as an INFO\n390\tnote; every other package still reports it as a WARN finding.\n391\t\n392\t## Concrete repo examples\n393\t\n394\t### `packages/fresh/builders/mod.ts` (1,110 LOC)\n395\t\n396\tVerdict: a single 1,110-line file *is* the public builder surface.\n397\tIt is currently the closest thing the repo has to a model package,\n398\tbut the size means a reader cannot keep the surface in their head.\n399\tDoctrine remediation: split by builder concern (route builder,\n400\tcomponent builder, query builder, etc.), expose them through\n401\tsubpaths, keep the top-level `mod.ts` to a manifest.\n402\t\n403\t### `packages/plugin/types.ts` (1,005 LOC)\n404\t\n405\tVerdict: a single 1,000-line file labeled `types.ts`. By definition\n406\tit bundles unrelated types into one bag. Remediation: split per\n407\tconcept (`plugin-definition.ts`, `plugin-loader.ts`,\n408\t`plugin-validation.ts`, `plugin-runtime.ts`). Re-export the public\n409\tones from `mod.ts`. The JSR doc score for the package will rise\n410\tbecause each smaller file carries focused JSDoc.\n411\t\n412\t### `packages/streams/` (5 files, 393 LOC)\n413\t\n414\tVerdict: appropriate. A small contract package should not be\n415\tinflated.\n416\t\n417\t## Surface checklist for review\n418\t\n419\tBefore merging a `packages/*` change that touches `mod.ts`:\n420\t\n421\t- [ ] Every export has a JSDoc summary and at least one `@example`.\n422\t- [ ] Every export uses the verb conventions (`define`, `create`,\n423\t      `start`, `with`, `use`).\n424\t- [ ] The README's quick-start block compiles when copied verbatim.\n425\t- [ ] No `I*` interface prefixes; no `*_T` suffixes.\n426\t- [ ] No re-exports of upstream packages.\n427\t- [ ] Subpath exports are used if the symbol count exceeds 20 or\n428\t      the responsibilities exceed three.\n429\t- [ ] `deno doc <package>` reads end-to-end as a manual.\n430\t- [ ] `deno publish --dry-run` is clean — no portability\n431\t      warnings, and no slow types *unless* the package is\n432\t      oRPC-bound (see \"Sanctioned exception\" above).\n433\t\n434\t## Published example compilation contract\n435\t\n436\tThe JSDoc gate enforces invalid syntax and usage that disagrees with a published signature, as well\n437\tas undeclared names and import/fence integrity. Type errors have no deferred allowance. An example\n438\tmay not skip checking to hide an API mismatch.\n439\t\n440\t\n441\t## docs/architecture/doctrine/09-anti-patterns-and-fitness-functions.md\n442\t\n443\tOriginal-byte SHA256:be35a323a5943178a131996c4343114f3e3bd0b69edaaeb4d68a3f285bdac302\n444\t\n445\t# Anti-Patterns and Fitness Functions\n446\t\n447\tAxioms governed: A14 (and the negative form of every other axiom).\n448\t\n449\tThe doctrine survives only if it is enforced by something other than goodwill. This page does two\n450\tthings:\n451\t\n452\t1. Names the anti-patterns the doctrine forbids, with concrete examples from this repo.\n453\t2. Describes the fitness functions — executable gates — that turn each prohibition into a check that\n454\t   runs on every PR.\n455\t\n456\tThe phrase _fitness function_ is from Neal Ford, Rebecca Parsons, and Patrick Kua's _Building\n457\tEvolutionary Architectures_ (2017): an objective integrity assessment of an architectural\n458\tcharacteristic. Tests are architecture, not just verification.\n459\t\n460\t## Anti-pattern catalog\n461\t\n462\t### AP-1. Monolithic file\n463\t\n464\tA single `.ts` file beyond two screenfuls (≈300 LOC) without a genuine reason — typically a long but\n465\tconceptually simple case-statement.\n466\t\n467\tExamples in this repo:\n468\t\n469\t| File                                                    | LOC   | Likely cause                                       |\n470\t| ------------------------------------------------------- | ----- | -------------------------------------------------- |\n471\t| `cli/src/commands/init/pipeline.ts`                     | 1,869 | Pipeline orchestration + IO + telemetry + dispatch |\n472\t| `cli/src/templates/aspire/helpers/generators_test.ts`   | 1,472 | A god test file                                    |\n473\t| `workers/executor/task-executor.ts`                     | 1,287 | Executor class accumulating concerns               |\n474\t| `cli/src/capabilities/plugin/official-plugin-copier.ts` | 1,203 | Procedural copy logic with branching               |\n475\t| `shared/utils/datetime.ts`                              | 1,112 | Reinvention of `@std/datetime`                     |\n476\t| `fresh/builders/mod.ts`                                 | 1,110 | All builders in one file                           |\n477\t| `kv/tests/bridge_test.ts`                               | 1,039 | God test file                                      |\n478\t| `plugin/types.ts`                                       | 1,005 | Bag of unrelated types                             |\n479\t| `config/schema.ts`                                      | 945   | Bag of schemas                                     |\n480\t| `sagas/transports/list-transport.ts`                    | 847   | One transport with all concerns inline             |\n481\t\n482\tRemediation: every file above is on the active remediation list in\n483\t[`10-codebase-verdict-and-handoff.md`](./10-codebase-verdict-and-handoff.md).\n484\t\n485\t### AP-2. Helper that renames a platform primitive\n486\t\n487\tA function or class that wraps `Deno.*`, Web Platform, or `@std/*` without adding a real seam. (See\n488\t[`04-modules-and-helpers.md`](./04-modules-and-helpers.md).)\n489\t\n490\t### AP-3. God interface\n491\t\n492\tAn interface with more than three or four methods that is \"the contract for everything our adapter\n493\tdoes.\" Gradually accretes optional methods that not all adapters implement.\n494\t\n495\tRemediation: split by behavior. Most god interfaces fragment naturally into `Reader`, `Writer`,\n496\t`Walker`, `Watcher`.\n497\t\n498\t### AP-4. Cross-package implementation inheritance\n499\t\n500\t`extends BaseFromAnotherPackage`. Forbidden. Use registration against an extension axis instead.\n501\t(See [`07-composition-and-extension.md`](./07-composition-and-extension.md).)\n502\t\n503\t### AP-5. Multi-level base lattice without subtype distinction\n504\t\n505\t`AbstractFooBase → FooBase → AbstractFoo → Foo`. Each layer must name a real distinction; otherwise\n506\tcollapse.\n507\t\n508\t### AP-6. Base class with concrete methods\n509\t\n510\tA \"convenience\" `run()` that orchestrates abstract methods is the gateway drug. Belongs on the\n511\trunner, not the base.\n512\t\n513\t### AP-7. Telescoping factory\n514\t\n515\tFunctions with eight positional arguments. Use a typed options object, or split into a builder.\n516\t(Bloch Item 2.)\n517\t\n518\t### AP-8. Premature DI container\n519\t\n520\tA container introduced \"for testability\" when the package has six collaborators wired by one\n521\tfactory. Replace with constructor injection; tests use direct replacement.\n522\t\n523\t### AP-9. Premature abstraction (the Wet Codebase failure)\n524\t\n525\tTwo callers with similar shapes deduped behind one helper that grows flags as a third caller\n526\tdiverges. Replace with two clearly- named sibling implementations.\n527\t\n528\t### AP-10. Defensive `try/catch` inside handlers\n529\t\n530\tA handler that swallows or remaps every error has hidden the supervisor's job. Throw rich errors;\n531\tlet the supervisor decide.\n532\t\n533\t### AP-11. Hidden globals\n534\t\n535\tModule-load-time `Deno.openKv()`, `new Logger()`, or `process.env`-reading singletons. Composition\n536\troot only.\n537\t\n538\t### AP-12. `Date.now()` and `setTimeout` in handlers\n539\t\n540\tThe clock is a port. Schedule via the clock; read time via the clock. Exception: trace timestamps\n541\tcaptured for diagnostics, where monotonic reads are local.\n542\t\n543\t### AP-13. `console.log` in published code\n544\t\n545\tPublished packages emit structured events through a telemetry port or a normalized error reporter.\n546\t`console.*` is reserved for the CLI's presentation layer or for `examples/` scripts.\n547\t\n548\t### AP-14. Re-exporting upstream packages\n549\t\n550\t`export { z } from 'zod';`. Consumers import Zod. We do not become a vendor for upstream surface.\n551\t\n552\t### AP-15. `interface IFoo` / `type FooT`\n553\t\n554\tHungarian or .NET-style prefixes on TypeScript names. Forbidden. Names use the noun for the thing.\n555\t\n556\t### AP-16. `utils/`, `helpers/`, `common/`, `lib/` folders\n557\t\n558\tGeneric folder names hide unnamed concerns. Rename for the role or move into the appropriate\n559\t`application/`, `adapters/`, or `diagnostics/` folder.\n560\t\n561\t### AP-17. `interfaces/` folder for the package's own interfaces\n562\t\n563\tInterfaces are _ports_ (consumed contracts, in `ports/`) or _type declarations_ (colocated with the\n564\tthings they describe). The folder name `interfaces/` blurs the distinction.\n565\t\n566\t### AP-18. Test snapshots of giant generated strings\n567\t\n568\tThe CLI E2E prompt called these out specifically. Tests assert _semantics_ — parsed structure, file\n569\texistence, command exit code, event counts — not entire generated strings. Snapshot tests are\n570\tallowed only where the literal text _is_ the public contract (stable CLI help banner,\n571\tintentionally-stable error code text).\n572\t\n573\t### AP-19. Permissions assumed silently\n574\t\n575\tA package that calls `fetch` without declaring its network requirement, or `Deno.openKv()` without\n576\tdeclaring KV. The README documents required permissions and the package may check them at startup\n577\twith a clear error.\n578\t\n579\t### AP-20. Workspace `compilerOptions.lib` override missing `deno.unstable`\n580\t\n581\tA workspace member that overrides `compilerOptions.lib` must include `\"deno.unstable\"` or transitive\n582\timports of unstable APIs (`Deno.Kv`) produce type errors. (See `CLAUDE.md`.) The doctrine elevates\n583\tthis from gotcha to lint.\n584\t\n585\t### AP-21. Flat command-surface folder\n586\t\n587\tA `presentation/`, `routes/`, or `handlers/` folder with more than 12 immediate children is a flat\n588\tlist with a path prefix. Vertical slicing (R-FOLD-LAYERING-MODE) groups by feature. The cardinality\n589\tcap (R-FOLD-CARD) enforces the split. Examples in this repo at audit time include any CLI\n590\tpresentation folder with 30+ command files.\n591\t\n592\t### AP-22. Useless re-export barrel\n593\t\n594\tA `mod.ts` inside `src/` whose only purpose is `export * from './x.ts'` for files in the same\n595\tfolder. Sub-directory barrels duplicate information (the file system already lists the folder),\n596\tforce a second source of truth for naming, and hide which symbols are actually used by the importer.\n597\tAllowed barrels: package root `mod.ts`, declared subpath-export entry files, the package's\n598\t`testing.ts`. All other `mod.ts` / `index.ts` under `src/` are violations unless the file genuinely\n599\tcontains aggregation logic (constructing one symbol from many).\n600\t\n601\t### AP-23. Inline command body in composition\n602\t\n603\tA composition root that contains `.command(...)`, `.option(...)`, `.action(...)`, route registration\n604\tchains, or message handler inline definitions has buried application code in wiring code. The\n605\tcomposition becomes the largest file in the package and the hardest to refactor. Composition\n606\tdeclarativity (R-COMP-DECL) requires the composition body to be wiring only; presentation is defined\n607\telsewhere and _referenced_ by the composition.\n608\t\n609\t### AP-24. Switch-over-tagged-union instead of registry\n610\t\n611\t```ts\n612\t// AP-24\n613\tfunction getEngineAdapter(engine: DbEngine): DbAdapter {\n614\t  switch (engine) {\n615\t    case 'postgres':\n616\t      return new PostgresAdapter();\n617\t    case 'mysql':\n618\t      return new MySqlAdapter();\n619\t    case 'sqlite':\n620\t      return new SqliteAdapter();\n621\t  }\n622\t}\n623\t```\n624\t\n625\tEvery new variant requires editing the switch. Replace with a typed registry (`DbEngineRegistry`)\n626\tpopulated at composition. Switch is acceptable only when the union is closed by domain constraint\n627\t(e.g. `'left' | 'right'`) and the cases are not implementations.\n628\t\n629\t### AP-25. Side effect in non-edge file\n630\t\n631\tA non-edge file (any file outside `bin/`, `examples/`, the package's adapters, or a designated edge\n632\tfolder) that calls `Deno.exit`, `Deno.cwd`, `Deno.env`, `Deno.build`, `Deno.readDir`,\n633\t`Deno.readFile`, `Deno.writeFile`, `Deno.Command`, `console.log`, `console.error`, `console.warn`,\n634\t`fetch`, `Date.now`, or `setTimeout` outside the cases listed in §\"Effects at edges\" of the relevant\n635\tarchetype.\n636\t\n637\tThe remedy is constructor injection of the appropriate port. The archetype docs name the exact path\n638\tconstraints; the doctrine records the universal prohibition.\n639\t\n640\t## Fitness functions — the executable gates\n641\t\n642\tEach anti-pattern above maps to a check. The doctrine declares the check; the next engineering doc\n643\timplements it as a script in `.llm/tools/`.\n644\t\n645\t### F-1. File-size lint\n646\t\n647\tWalks `packages/**/*.ts` and `plugins/**/*.ts`. Flags files over 500 LOC; fails over 800. Allows\n648\topt-out via a `// arch:size-ok\n649\t<reason>` comment that requires a justification on the PR.\n650\t\n651\t### F-2. Helper-reinvention scan\n652\t\n653\tStatic check that compares helper bodies against a known list of `@std/*` and Web Platform\n654\tprimitives. Flags helpers that wrap them with no extra logic. Implemented as an AST-walking script.\n655\t\n656\t### F-3. Layering check\n657\t\n658\tStatic dependency-direction check. For each `packages/*` package:\n659\t\n660\t- Files in `domain/` may import `@std/*`, the package's own `domain/`, and external types only.\n661\t- `ports/` may import `domain/` only.\n662\t- `application/` may import `domain/` and `ports/` only.\n663\t- `adapters/` may import `domain/`, `ports/`, and external clients.\n664\t- `presentation/` may import `application/` and `domain/` only.\n665\t\n666\tImplemented via `deno doc` AST traversal or a manual graph walk.\n667\t\n668\t### F-4. Inheritance audit\n669\t\n670\tFor each `class X extends Y`:\n671\t\n672\t- Y must live in the same package (no cross-package inheritance).\n673\t- Y must be `abstract` and contain _only_ abstract members.\n674\t- Inheritance depth ≤ 3.\n675\t\n676\tImplemented via the TypeScript compiler API.\n677\t\n678\t### F-5. Public surface audit\n679\t\n680\t`deno doc --json <package>` runs and the doctrine asserts:\n681\t\n682\t- Every exported symbol has a JSDoc summary.\n683\t- Every exported function has `@param` and `@returns`.\n684\t"}]}}

JSR publishability gate\n8\t\n9\t`deno publish --dry-run` runs in CI for every package. Slow types and unportable filenames fail the\n10\tbuild.\n11\t\n12\t### F-7. Doc-score gate\n13\t\n14\tJSR's documentation score must be 100 for stable packages. Mismatch fails the build.\n26\t\n27\t### F-10. Test-shape audit\n28\t\n29\tStatic check that no `_test.ts` exceeds 500 LOC. Test files that fail are flagged for splitting.\n30\t\n31\t### F-11. Forbidden-folder lint\n32\t\n33\tForbids `utils/`, `common/`, `lib/`, `helpers/`, `interfaces/` under `packages/*/src/`. Allows\n34\t`application/`, `adapters/`, `domain/`, `ports/`, `runtime/`, `state/`, `middleware/`, `presets/`,\n35\t`registry/`, `diagnostics/`, `presentation/`, `testing/`, `internal/` per the role vocabulary.\n36\t\n37\t### F-12. Naming-convention lint\n38\t\n39\tForbids `interface I*`, `type *_T`, `class *Impl`, `class Abstract*` where the class is not actually\n40\tabstract.\n41\t\n42\t### F-13. Saga and runtime invariants\n43\t\n44\tCustom checks for stateful packages:\n45\t\n46\t- Every `defineSaga()` declaration has at least one terminal transition.\n47\t- Every `defineWorker()` declares a retry policy.\n48\t- Every long-running runtime exposes a `stop()` method.\n49\t- Every async public method whose body performs IO accepts an `AbortSignal`.\n50\t\n51\t### F-14. Console-log lint\n52\t\n53\tForbids `console.*` outside `presentation/` and `examples/`.\n54\t\n55\t### F-15. Re-export-of-upstream lint\n56\t\n57\tForbids `export * from 'npm:...'` and `export { ... } from 'jsr:...'` when the source is not a\n58\t`@netscript/*` package or `@std/*`.\n59\t\n60\t### F-16. Folder-cardinality lint\n61\t\n62\tWalks every directory under `packages/*/src/` and `plugins/*/src/`. Fails any directory with more\n63\tthan 12 immediate children (combined files + subdirectories) and any path nested more than 4 levels\n64\tbelow `src/`. Maps to AP-21 and R-FOLD-CARD.\n65\t\n66\tPer-archetype overrides may relax the cap (e.g. an `assets/` folder of `.template` files is exempt,\n67\tdeclared in the archetype doc). The override is named, not implicit.\n68\t\n69\t### F-17. Abstract-derived co-location lint\n70\t\n71\tFor each `abstract class X` declared under `packages/*/src/`:\n72\t\n73\t- Locate every `class Y extends X` in the same package.\n74\t- If the count of concrete subclasses is ≥ 2, assert the abstract and all subclasses live in the\n75\t  same folder.\n76\t- The folder name is the plural concept (heuristic: matches `<concept-name>s` or\n77\t  `<concept-name>-<plural-suffix>`).\n78\t\n79\tMaps to R-FOLD-AD-COLOC. Implemented via the TypeScript compiler API.\n80\t\n81\t### F-18. Sub-barrel lint\n82\t\n83\tForbids `mod.ts` and `index.ts` files inside `packages/*/src/**` _subdirectories_ (depth ≥ 2 from\n84\tthe package root). Allowed locations: package-root `mod.ts`, files declared as a subpath export in\n85\tthe package's `deno.json`, the package's `extension-points.ts`, and `testing.ts`. Maps to AP-22.\n86\t\n87\tA sub-barrel that genuinely aggregates (constructs one symbol from many, not just re-exports) is\n88\tallowed via a `// arch:barrel-ok\n89\t<reason>` comment, recorded in the audit registry.\n90\t\n91\t### F-19. Scoped source gate runners\n92\t\n93\tCheck, lint, and formatting evidence for package/plugin quality runs targets source TypeScript, not\n94\tthe whole repository by directory accident. The gates run over `.ts` and `.tsx` source under the\n95\towned or current-wave package/plugin roots, exclude generated output (`.generated/`, generated\n96\tclient trees, scaffold scratch), and exclude future-wave packages that the current plan does not\n97\town.\n98\t\n99\tDoctrine-root discovery covers the top-level units under `packages/*` and `plugins/*`. The nested\n100\t`packages/cli/e2e` workspace member is an end-to-end harness for the CLI, not an independently\n101\tpublished top-level doctrine unit, so it is intentionally excluded from that root set.\n102\t\n103\tRaw root `deno check .`, `deno lint`, or `deno fmt --check` over scratch workspaces, copied\n104\ttemplates, generated output, Markdown, or legacy line-ending-only drift is not a package-quality\n105\tverdict source unless the plan explicitly owns a repo-wide normalization pass. For ordinary\n106\tpackage-quality work, use the scoped wrapper family:\n107\t\n108\t```powershell\n109\tdeno run --allow-read --allow-run .llm/tools/run-deno-check.ts --root <pkg> --ext ts,tsx --exclude <generated-or-future-wave-regex>\n110\tdeno run --allow-read --allow-run .llm/tools/run-deno-lint.ts --root <pkg> --ext ts,tsx --exclude <generated-or-future-wave-regex>\n111\tdeno run --allow-read --allow-run .llm/tools/run-deno-fmt.ts --root <pkg> --ext ts,tsx --exclude <generated-or-future-wave-regex>\n112\t```\n113\t\n114\t## How fitness functions ship\n115\t\n116\tThe doctrine prescribes the _checks_; implementation lives in `.llm/tools/`:\n117\t\n118\t- `check-file-sizes.ts`\n119\t- `check-helper-reinvention.ts`\n120\t- `check-layering.ts`\n121\t- `check-inheritance.ts`\n122\t- `check-doc-coverage.ts`\n123\t- `check-workspace-lib.ts`\n124\t- `check-naming.ts`\n125\t- `check-saga-invariants.ts`\n126\t- `check-console-usage.ts`\n127\t- `check-upstream-reexport.ts`\n128\t- `check-folder-cardinality.ts`\n129\t- `check-abstract-coloc.ts`\n130\t- `check-sub-barrels.ts`\n131\t\n132\tEach script:\n133\t\n134\t- runs as `deno task arch:check:<name>`,\n135\t- emits structured JSON output compatible with the `.llm/tools/run-deno-*.ts` wrapper family,\n136\t- exits non-zero on violation,\n137\t- supports a `--allow <package>` opt-out (with mandatory justification recorded in\n138\t  `.llm/arch-debt.md`).\n139\t\n140\tA composite task `deno task arch:check` runs all of them.\n141\t\n142\t## Architectural debt as a registry\n143\t\n144\tWhen a violation is acknowledged but cannot be fixed in the same PR, it is recorded in\n145\t`.llm/arch-debt.md`:\n146\t\n147\t```md\n148\t## packages/cli/src/commands/init/pipeline.ts — AP-1 (1,869 LOC)\n149\t\n150\t- Reason: pending refactor under refactor/cli-e2e-validation-suite\n151\t- Owner: <name>\n152\t- Target: split per concern by 2026-Q3\n153\t- Linked plan: .llm/tmp/run/refactor-cli-e2e-validation-suite/plan.md\n154\t```\n155\t\n156\tThe registry is the doctrine's pressure release. Without it, the gates either become noise (silenced\n157\teverywhere) or block work (blocking everywhere). With it, debt is _named_, _owned_, and\n158\t_time-bounded_.\n159\t\n160\t## Fitness-function checklist for review\n161\t\n162\t- [ ] PR runs `deno task arch:check`. All checks pass or are explicitly opted-out with a\n163\t      `arch-debt.md` entry.\n164\t- [ ] New code never introduces a new `arch-debt.md` entry without a time-bounded plan.\n165\t- [ ] Removed `arch-debt.md` entries match the fix in the PR.\n166\t- [ ] New fitness functions added in the same PR as the doctrine change that motivated them (gates\n167\t      and rules ship together).\n168\t\n169\t\n170\t## .llm/harness/evaluator/protocol.md\n171\t\n172\tOriginal-byte SHA256:cf48f3132820c2a6f492462b9701ecd45947c3e0cf96316fa04ecf3a26709241\n173\t\n174\t# Evaluator Protocol\n175\t\n176\tThis protocol governs **IMPL-EVAL**, the **final** evaluator pass. The Plan-Gate's **PLAN-EVAL** is\n177\ta separate, earlier pass governed by `plan-protocol.md`. Both passes are separate sessions.\n178\t\n179\tThe evaluator is a separate session from the generator. Its job is to verify the approved plan\n180\tagainst the changed state, not to continue implementation.\n181\t\n182\tSelect the evaluator from the run's workload tier in `workflow/lane-policy.md`. The typed resolver\n183\tmust skip any candidate from the selected generator's vendor family, then choose the first healthy,\n184\tcapable, allowance-proven transport in the canonical provider order. Never infer a model, effort,\n185\ttransport, or fallback from historical lane names.\n186\t\n187\t`complex` and `architecture` are privileged rows. Do not select either from inferred complexity: the\n188\trun must contain explicit owner or milestone-coordinator authorization and its rationale. When that\n189\tevidence is absent, evaluation is capped at the `feature` row.\n190\t\n191\tThe supervisor triggers the evaluator; a sub-agent never auto-dispatches one. Record the tier,\n192\tphase, fallback reason, requested and observed route identity, session, exact head, and expense\n193\tdecision where a paid OpenCode route is selected. OpenRouter paid-training eligibility is allowed by\n194\towner preference and is not a routing blocker.\n195\t\n196\tIMPL-EVAL remains mandatory unless the owner explicitly waives it. Re-steer the same evaluator\n197\tsession after a failure and obey the tier-specific loop limit:\n198\t\n199\t- simple: the owner did not specify a maximum; record this explicitly rather than inventing one;\n200\t- straightforward, feature, complex: maximum five, notify the owner after three;\n201\t- architecture: maximum three, notify the owner after two;\n202\t- documentation: use the tier's policy with a hard maximum of two.\n203\t\n204\tThese notification points do not freeze unrelated work and do not replace a smaller explicit limit.\n205\tThe selected generator and evaluator must remain different vendor families in every cycle.\n206\t\n207\t## Required Inputs\n208\t\n209\t| Input                                        | Required                    | Purpose                                                |\n210\t| -------------------------------------------- | --------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------ |\n211\t| `workflow/run-loop.md`                       | yes                         | run-loop phases and design checkpoint rules            |\n212\t| `verdict-definitions.md`                     | yes                         | verdict rules                                          |\n213\t| selected archetype profile                   | yes for package/plugin work | doctrine gates, concept of done, and false-done states |\n214\t| selected scope overlays                      | when applicable             | frontend/service/docs gates                            |\n215\t| run `plan.md`                                | yes                         | approved scope                                         |\n216\t| run `plan-eval.md`                           | when PLAN-EVAL selected     | conditional planning verdict                           |\n217\t| run `worklog.md`                             | yes                         | design checkpoint and generator evidence               |\n218\t| run `context-pack.md`                        | yes when present            | resumable state                                        |\n219\t| run `drift.md`                               | yes when present            | plan/doctrine drift                                    |\n220\t| draft-PR commit list + per-slice PR comments | yes when commits exist      | implementation history (the commit trail)              |\n221\t| `debt/arch-debt.md`                          | yes                         | debt delta                                             |\n222\t\n223\t## Operating Rules\n224\t\n225\t1. Evaluate against the approved plan and archetype gates.\n226\t2. Verify either the selected Plan-Gate passed before implementation (`plan-eval.md` = `PASS`) or\n227\t   the run recorded a justified `PLAN-EVAL: N/A` before implementation. Missing both is a process\n228\t   failure.\n229\t3. Verify the Design checkpoint exists in `worklog.md` and commit slices follow it. Missing design\n230\t   evidence is a finding.\n231\t4. Verify each commit slice has its named gate passing.\n232\t5. Check the Concept of Done (run-loop § 5 + archetype profile) for each slice.\n233\t6. Run or manually verify the applicable gates independently.\n234\t7. Treat missing evidence as a finding.\n235\t8. Name doctrine violations by AP code when possible.\n236\t9. Use `FAIL_DEBT` when the only blocking issue is unrecorded or malformed architecture debt.\n237\t10. Use `FAIL_RESCOPE` when the plan is materially wrong, not merely incomplete.\n238\t11. Do not fix implementation except for minimal read-only validation commands.\n239\t12. Verify the **close-gate** (`netscript-pr` → \"Merge close-gate (#387)\") is honored before any\n240\t    `status:ready-merge` / `Closes #N` merge: for every referenced issue, its acceptance criteria\n241\t    and every `gate:` checkbox are checked with linked evidence, and the PR's Definition-of-Done\n242\t    checklist is complete. An unchecked `gate:` box on a referenced issue (the #260 failure) blocks\n243\t    the pass.\n244\t13. Verify every agent brief/prompt (implementation, evaluation, side-fix) carries a `## SKILL`\n245\t    chapter naming the relevant skills (harness rule; a missing SKILL chapter in a brief is a\n246\t    finding). PR bodies are governed by the `netscript-pr` templates and do NOT require a `## SKILL`\n247\t    chapter — never raise its absence from a PR body as a finding.\n248\t14. For a **cut or release-gating run**, verify the **release-gate class** (`gates/release-gates.md`\n249\t    — `scaffold.runtime`, `e2e-cli-prod`, and the composite release gate) is green with\n250\t    raw-exit-code evidence before any `status:ready-merge` / release. A red or unrun release gate on\n251\t    a release cut blocks the pass. The gate definitions are owned by #309 release engineering (the\n252\t    `netscript-release` skill); the evaluator confirms they ran, it does not redefine them.\n253\t    Non-release runs treat this rule as `n/a`.\n254\t\n255\t## Output\n256\t\n257\tWrite `.llm/runs/<run-id>/evaluate.md` using `templates/evaluate.md`.\n258\t\n259\t## Evidence Standard\n260\t\n261\tEvery `PASS` row must have evidence: command, file, trace, route, consumer path, or debt entry. A\n262\tblank `PASS` is not a pass.\n263\t\n264\t\n265\t## .llm/harness/evaluator/verdict-definitions.md\n266\t\n267\tOriginal-byte SHA256:adf719c74f3ae1c627f5fac065324d23b87281b0acd2e73d4d138716833610d7\n268\t\n269\t# Verdict Definitions\n270\t\n271\tEvaluator verdicts are stable values. Use exactly one.\n272\t\n273\t## PASS\n274\t\n275\tUse `PASS` when all are true:\n276\t\n277\t- approved scope is complete,\n278\t- required static gates pass or are not applicable,\n279\t- required fitness gates pass, are not applicable, or have valid debt entries,\n280\t- required runtime and consumer gates have evidence,\n281\t- no unrecorded doctrine violation was introduced or deepened,\n282\t- docs and run artifacts are updated enough for resume.\n283\t\n284\t## FAIL_PLAN\n285\t\n286\t`FAIL_PLAN` is emitted only by PLAN-EVAL at the Plan-Gate. The plan is incomplete or unsound (an\n287\tunchecked `gates/plan-gate.md` box). Returns the run to Plan & Design; no implementation slice may\n288\tbe committed. Two `FAIL_PLAN` cycles, then escalate to the user.\n289\t\n290\t## FAIL_FIX\n291\t\n292\tUse `FAIL_FIX` when the plan remains valid but the implementation or docs need more work. Examples:\n293\t\n294\t- a required gate fails,\n295\t- evidence is missing,\n296\t- a path/link is wrong,\n297\t- a consumer import was not updated,\n298\t- a false-done state from the profile is present.\n299\t\n300\t## FAIL_RESCOPE\n301\t\n302\tUse `FAIL_RESCOPE` when the approved plan is materially wrong or too small. Examples:\n303\t\n304\t- the task requires a package/API redesign not named in the plan,\n305\t- the selected archetype is wrong,\n306\t- a docs run uncovers code reality that needs a separate implementation plan,\n307\t- external/runtime validation cannot be performed without adding new scope.\n308\t\n309\t## FAIL_DEBT\n310\t\n311\tUse `FAIL_DEBT` when code/docs may otherwise satisfy the plan, but architecture debt handling is\n312\tinvalid. Examples:\n313\t\n314\t- a doctrine violation is introduced without an `arch-debt.md` entry,\n315\t- an entry lacks owner, target, reason, linked plan, or status,\n316\t- new work deepens an existing debt entry without updating it,\n317\t- a run closes a debt entry without evidence that the relevant gate now passes.\n318\t\n319\t`FAIL_DEBT` means the required fix is debt bookkeeping or debt rationale, not necessarily\n320\timplementation.\n321\t\n322\t## Choosing Between Verdicts\n323\t\n324\t| Condition                                                    | Verdict        |\n325\t| ------------------------------------------------------------ | -------------- |\n326\t| Plan incomplete or unsound at the Plan-Gate (PLAN-EVAL only) | `FAIL_PLAN`    |\n327\t| Code/docs incomplete, plan valid                             | `FAIL_FIX`     |\n328\t| Plan/archetype/scope invalid                                 | `FAIL_RESCOPE` |\n329\t| Only debt registry is blocking                               | `FAIL_DEBT`    |\n330\t| All applicable gates and debt rules satisfied                | `PASS`         |\n331\t\n332\t\n333\t## .llm/harness/workflow/run-loop.md\n334\t\n335\tOriginal-byte SHA256:0adebf021aa499317ea1cb6d59df97c74f1fcac1a27db1e3f5b8a06ce89a0120\n336\t\n337\t# Run Loop\n338\t\n339\tHarness runs in nine phases: Bootstrap, Research, Plan & Design, Plan-Gate, Implement, Gate,\n340\tEvaluate, Release, Close.\n341\t\n342\tThe loop has one mandatory evaluator pass and one conditional pass: **PLAN-EVAL** is used before\n343\timplementation when the work is genuinely complex, decision-heavy, or benefits from adversarial plan\n344\tadvice; **IMPL-EVAL** runs after implementation unless the owner explicitly waives it. Every\n345\tevaluator pass that runs uses a separate session.\n346\t\n347\t## 1. Bootstrap\n348\t\n349\t1. Identify the scope and affected package/plugin surfaces.\n350\t2. Select the smallest doctrine archetype that fits.\n351\t3. Apply any scope overlay for frontend, service, or docs work.\n352\t4. Read `debt/arch-debt.md` for relevant open debt.\n353\t5. Read the current doctrine verdict in\n354\t   `docs/architecture/doctrine/10-codebase-verdict-and-handoff.md`.\n355\t6. Seed `plan.md`.\n356\t\n357\t## 2. Research\n358\t\n359\tProduce `research.md` before locking the plan. Research exists to make the plan true, not merely\n360\tplausible.\n361\t\n362\t1. **Re-baseline** — if any plan, audit, or run was carried in from another branch or repo,\n363\t   re-derive its facts against the current `main`. A carried-in plan is a starting skeleton, never\n364\t   ground truth. Record what changed.\n365\t2. **Findings** — the concrete facts the plan depends on (existing dispatch infrastructure, real\n366\t   counts, stub depth, empty arrays, legacy config keys). State each finding so a reviewer can\n367\t   verify it.\n368\t3. **jsr-audit surface scan** (package/plugin waves) — apply the `jsr-audit` skill's publishability\n369\t   rubric to the _current_ public surface and record the slow-type / surface risks the plan must\n370\t   address.\n371\t4. **Open questions** — anything unresolved the plan must close.\n372\t\n373\t## 3. Plan & Design\n374\t\n375\tOne phase, two artifacts; it ends by putting the draft PR into the **\"Plan & Design — READY FOR\n376\tREVIEW\"** state.\n377\t\n378\t### 3a. plan.md\n379\t\n380\t`plan.md` names:\n381\t\n382\t- the archetype and the current doctrine verdict;\n383\t- the expected gate set (from the archetype matrix) and scope overlays;\n384\t- **architecture decisions LOCKED** with rationale;\n385\t- an **open-decision sweep** — every decision still open, each marked \"safe to defer\" or \"must\n386\t  resolve now\"; any decision that would force rework if deferred must be resolved before the\n387\t  Plan-Gate;\n388\t- a **risk register** with mitigations;\n389\t- debt implications;\n390\t- deferred scope.\n391\t\n392\t### 3b. Design checkpoint (worklog.md)\n393\t\n394\tBefore creating implementation files, record a `## Design` section in `worklog.md` with:\n395\t\n396\t1. **Public surface** — exported functions, entry points, CLI commands.\n397\t2. **Domain vocabulary** — types, interfaces, discriminated unions the implementation needs today\n398\t   (not speculatively).\n399\t3. **Ports** — consumed abstractions. Only create a port when the plan names a real external\n400\t   dependency or a testability seam the slice exercises.\n401\t4. **Constants** — finite domain values as constants with derived union types. Name known IDs\n402\t   (suites, gates, phases, plugins) here before creating files.\n403\t5. **Commit slices** — ordered list. Each slice names what it introduces, which gate proves it\n404\t   works, and which files it creates or changes. Target < 30.\n405\t6. **Deferred scope** — capabilities intentionally left out and why.\n406\t7. **Contributor path** — how a new developer navigates the result to add a feature.\n407\t\n408\tEvery file created during implementation must trace back to a concept named here. Files that exist\n409\tonly to satisfy a folder template are speculative seams — they do not belong in the plan.\n410\t\n411\t### 3c. Commit and open the plan PR\n412\t\n413\tCommit the plan + design artifacts. Put the draft PR into the **\"Plan & Design — READY FOR REVIEW\"**\n414\tstate with a body summarizing findings, locked decisions, commit slices, risk register, and selected\n415\tgates (the PR #95 shape). The body must say: _do not merge until any selected Plan-Gate and the\n416\tmandatory final evaluator pass are complete (unless the owner explicitly waives IMPL-EVAL)._\n417\t\n418\t## 4. Conditional Plan-Gate (PLAN-EVAL)\n419\t\n420\tPLAN-EVAL is required only for genuinely critical or complex topics: material architecture or\n421\tpublic-contract decisions, multi-package/multi-PR sequencing, destructive or release/runtime risk,\n422\tor an unresolved trade-off whose wrong answer would force meaningful rework. Wanting generic\n423\tadversarial advice is not sufficient. When selected, it is a **hard stop**: no implementation slice\n424\tmay be committed until its verdict is `PASS`.\n425\t\n426\tSkip PLAN-EVAL for routine documentation corrections, mechanical fixes, generated refreshes, and\n427\tbounded test/gate repairs whose issue body and current evidence already provide the contract, scope,\n428\tacceptance criteria, and gates. Record `PLAN-EVAL: N/A` plus the concrete risk-selection reason in\n429\t`worklog.md`/`phase-registry.md`; do not create a ceremonial evaluator session. Tier-A slice review\n430\tand IMPL-EVAL still apply.\n431\t\n432\tPLAN-EVAL is a **separate session**. The plan evaluator:\n433\t\n434\t1. reads `evaluator/plan-protocol.md` and `gates/plan-gate.md`;\n435\t2. reads `research.md`, `plan.md`, and the `## Design` section;\n436\t3. checks every box in `gates/plan-gate.md`;\n437\t4. writes `plan-eval.md` from `templates/plan-eval.md`;\n438\t5. emits `PASS` or `FAIL_PLAN`.\n439\t\n440\t`FAIL_PLAN` returns the plan with the specific unchecked items. Two `FAIL_PLAN` cycles are allowed\n441\tbefore escalation to the user.\n442\t\n443\t## 5. Implement\n444\t\n445\t1. Implement one commit slice at a time in the Design order.\n446\t2. After each slice:\n447\t   - run the slice's named gate,\n448\t   - **Slice review gate (Amendment A1)** — before the sign-off commit, the Tier-A supervisor\n449\t     substantively reviews the landed slice (correctness, coherence with already-landed slices,\n450\t     doctrine-fit, gaps/overreach). No implementation lane (Tier B/C/D) self-certifies; passing\n451\t     automated gates is not a sign-off. See `workflow/lane-policy.md` §\"Slice review gate (Amendment\n452\t     A1)\" for the lane-agnostic invariant this step enforces.\n453\t   - commit (the **sign-off commit**, the supervisor's) with a message that names what the slice\n454\t     proves, not what it contains,\n455\t   - push, then comment on the draft PR with the slice scope, commit hash, and gate evidence — the\n456\t     draft-PR commit list + per-slice PR comments are the commit trail (there is no `commits.md`),\n457\t   - update `worklog.md` + `context-pack.md` as part of the same slice (a slice whose commit does\n458\t     not touch the run dir is incomplete),\n459\t   - run the **post-slice reconcile loop** (below) and write one reconcile note in `worklog.md`.\n460\t3. Append `drift.md` when facts diverge from the plan, RFC, or doctrine.\n461\t\n462\tBefore committing, inspect `git status --short` and do not fold in unrelated user changes. If an\n463\tunrelated change shares a file you must edit, work with the current content instead of reverting it.\n464\t\n465\t### Concept of Done (per slice)\n466\t\n467\tA slice is done when:\n468\t\n469\t- every file in the slice is reachable from the public surface or a test,\n470\t- no file exists to satisfy a folder shape without being used,\n471\t- constants replace string literals for finite domain vocabularies,\n472\t- public functions have a JSDoc one-liner (what it does, not how),\n473\t- a contributor can extend the slice by reading one file and copying a pattern,\n474\t- the slice's gate passes.\n475\t\n476\t### Post-slice reconcile loop\n477\t\n478\tAfter each slice completes (and before starting the next), reconcile the GitHub surface with the\n479\trun:\n480\t\n481\t1. **Sweep related issues** — check their state, any needed `status:`/`area:`/milestone moves, and\n482\t   that the resolving PR carries the right closing keyword (`Closes #N` for full resolution, a bare\n483\t   reference for partial work — see `netscript-pr`).\n484\t2. **Read new issue/PR comments** since the last sweep (evaluator verdicts, reviewer notes,\n485\t   OpenHands output).\n486\t3. **Record readjustments** — fold plan changes into `plan.md`; append `drift.md` when reality\n487\t   diverged from the plan or doctrine.\n488\t\n489\tWrite **one reconcile note per slice** in `worklog.md`.\n490\t\n491\t## 6. Gate\n492\t\n493\tBefore handing off, the generator runs the full gate set required by the archetype matrix and scope\n494\toverlays:\n495\t\n496\t- static gates,\n497\t- fitness gates when implemented or explicitly pending for Phase A,\n498\t- runtime gates when the run touches runtime behavior,\n499\t- consumer gates when exports or downstream contracts change,\n500\t- **jsr-audit** as a required gate for package/plugin waves,\n501\t- **the code-quality gate** (`deno task quality:scan` + `deno task arch:check`) as a required gate\n502\t  for any wave touching `packages/**` or `plugins/**`.\n503\t\n504\t**The scoped check/lint/fmt wrappers are necessary but NOT sufficient.** They pass code containing\n505\t`any` and they honor inline `// deno-lint-ignore no-explicit-any`, so they cannot catch the two\n506\tviolation classes that reached `main` in the beta.9 CLI wave (#745): (1) `any` + manual casting\n507\t(`Command<any,…>` under a lint-ignore, `as unknown as`/`as any`), and (2) host-side hardcoded\n508\tplugin-name coupling (`plugin.name === 'auth'`, `kind === 'ai'`). Running `quality:scan` (which\n509\tfails on both) and `arch:check` (doctrine fitness) per slice is what catches them. A green scoped\n510\twrapper is never a substitute for these two gates on a framework-source wave. A\n511\t`// deno-lint-ignore` added to make a wrapper pass is itself a finding — never silence a gate to\n512\tgreen it.\n513\t\n514\tGate **evidence is wrapper-sourced and mandatory**: type-check / test / lint / format from the\n515\tstructured wrappers (`.llm/tools/run-deno-check|test|lint|fmt.ts`), doc-lint from\n516\t`deno task doc:lint`, and dependency / publishability from the `deno task deps:*` wrappers — raw\n517\troot `deno check .` / `deno fmt --check` and hand-rolled registry curls are non-verdicts. The full\n518\ttool surface is indexed in `workflow/tooling.md`; the command map and gotchas live in the\n519\t**netscript-tools** and **netscript-deno-toolchain** skills (do not restate them here).\n520\t\n521\tRecord results as tables in `worklog.md`.\n522\t\n523\t## 7. Evaluate (IMPL-EVAL)\n524\t\n525\tThe final evaluation is mandatory unless the owner explicitly waives it, and always uses a separate\n526\tsession. Operating instructions live in `evaluator/protocol.md`.\n527\t\n528\t1. Read `evaluator/protocol.md` and `evaluator/verdict-definitions.md`.\n529\t2. Read the archetype profile, overlays, research, plan, plan-eval, worklog, context pack, drift,\n530\t   and the draft-PR commit list + per-slice PR comments (the commit trail).\n531\t3. Verify the Design checkpoint exists and was followed.\n532\t4. Verify commit slices match the Design checkpoint.\n533\t5. Verify either the selected Plan-Gate passed (`plan-eval.md` = `PASS`) before implementation, or\n534\t   the run recorded a justified `PLAN-EVAL: N/A` under §4 before implementation began.\n535\t6. Run the applicable gate set independently.\n536\t7. Fill `evaluate.md` from `templates/evaluate.md`.\n537\t8. Emit `PASS`, `FAIL_FIX`, `FAIL_RESCOPE`, or `FAIL_DEBT`.\n538\t\n539\tTwo consecutive terminal IMPL-EVAL failures are allowed on one leaf. After the second, stop the\n540\tevaluator loop, release the evaluator lease, keep the canonical implementation author available\n541\trather than frozen, and surface the exact unresolved decision plus both verdicts to the user in the\n542\tprimary coordinator task. Do not infer a third evaluator/fix loop; continue every independent lane.\n543\t\n544\t## 8. Release (release-cutting runs only)\n545\t\n546\tRuns that cut or gate a release call the hard release gates here — **`e2e-cli-prod`,\n547\t`scaffold.runtime`, and the release-gate class**. The harness-side single source for this class —\n548\twhich gate is required when, and the evidence bar — is `gates/release-gates.md`. This phase does\n549\t**not** define the gates themselves: their definitions, sequencing, and race-free production\n550\tverification are owned by **#309's release engineering** (see the `netscript-release` skill).\n551\tReference and call them here; do not redefine them. For a run that does not cut a release, this\n552\tphase is a no-op.\n553\t\n554\t## 9. Close\n555\t\n556\t1. If the run failed and you do not know what is still running, `behavior.service-health` timed out,\n557\t   ports are already in use, or a `postgres-*` container appeared that you did not start, run\n558\t   `deno task maint:leak-check -- --slice-dir <run-dir> --worktree <worktree>`. Report foreign and\n559\t   unknown-owner resources; never mutate them. Preview owned cleanup with `maint:teardown` and add\n560\t   `--apply` only when positive ownership proof is present.\n561\t2. Update `context-pack.md`.\n562\t3. Update `debt/arch-debt.md` for any created or closed debt entries.\n563\t4. Promote repeated lessons to `lessons/` only when the promotion rule is met.\n564\t5. Write a dated `.llm/YYYY-MM-DD-description.md` session record.\n565\t\n566\t\n567\t## .llm/harness/archetypes/SCOPE-docs.md\n568\t\n569\tOriginal-byte SHA256:11bbcf4317f7ed797c3851223a885c323be7f3f38899f35d31a76c5fc44c7885\n570\t\n571\t# Scope Overlay — Docs\n572\t\n573\tUse this overlay for RFCs, README files, knowledge-base pages, harness docs, run artifacts, and\n574\tdoctrine-facing documentation.\n575\t\n576\t## Doctrine Boundary\n577\t\n578\tDocs may describe either current state or doctrine target state. If the work introduces adapters,\n544\truntime lifecycle, state, or a fluent API as the main product, select a larger archetype.\n545\t\n546\tCurrent examples are listed in `docs/architecture/doctrine/06-archetypes.md` under \"Archetype\n547\tassignments for current packages.\"\n548\t\n549\t## Minimum Folder Shape\n550\t\n551\tUse the canonical shape in\n552\t`docs/architecture/doctrine/06-archetypes.md#archetype-1--small-contract`. The review question is\n553\tnot whether every folder exists; it is whether the package stayed small and avoided invented layers.\n554\t\n555\t## Skills to Activate\n556\t\n557\t- `netscript-doctrine`\n558\t- `jsr-audit` when publishability or JSR score is in scope\n559\t\n560\t## Read First\n561\t\n562\t1. This profile.\n563\t2. `../gates/archetype-gate-matrix.md`.\n564\t3. `docs/architecture/doctrine/06-archetypes.md#archetype-1--small-contract`.\n565\t4. `docs/architecture/doctrine/02-public-surface.md`.\n566\t5. The package README, `mod.ts`, `deno.json`, and tests.\n567\t6. Relevant debt entries in `../debt/arch-debt.md`.\n568\t\n569\t## Required Gates in Order\n570\t\n571\t1. Static gates: narrow `deno check` when justified, structured package check/test,\n572\t   `deno task fmt:check`, `deno task lint`, `deno task doc:lint`, and `deno publish --dry-run` when\n573\t   package scope.\n574\t2. Fitness gates: F-1, F-5, F-6, F-7, F-8, F-10, F-11, F-12, F-14, F-15, F-16, F-17, F-18, F-19.\n575\t3. Runtime gates: none by default.\n576\t4. Consumer gates: optional unless exports, names, or validation semantics changed.\n577\t\n578\t## Anti-Patterns to Watch For\n579\t\n580\t- AP-1: one schema/type file that grows into a bag of concepts.\n581\t- AP-2: helpers that rename Zod, Web Platform, or `@std/*` primitives.\n582\t- AP-7: telescoping parse or factory functions.\n583\t- AP-9: premature abstraction across two similar schemas.\n584\t- AP-14: re-exporting upstream libraries as part of the package surface.\n585\t- AP-16: `utils/`, `helpers/`, or `common/` folders.\n586\t- AP-22: a `mod.ts` inside `src/` that only re-exports the sibling type/schema files next to it.\n587\t- AP-25: a load-time side effect in a file that should hold only pure types and parse logic.\n588\t\n589\t## False-Done States\n590\t\n591\t- The package compiles but the README does not document the type table or permissions.\n592\t- A single large `schema.ts` remains because \"it is only types.\"\n593\t- The public surface exports every internal type instead of a curated contract.\n594\t- Validation changed but consumer examples were not checked.\n595\t\n596\t## Rescope Triggers\n597\t\n598\t- The package needs adapters or external IO.\n599\t- The package needs lifecycle, state, or background work.\n600\t- The exported symbol count suggests subpaths or a split package.\n601\t- The current doctrine verdict is `Restructure` or `Rewrite`.\n602\t\n603\t## Design Checkpoint Expectations\n604\t\n605\tThe design checkpoint names the contract boundary first: public types, exported schemas, README\n606\tsections, and semantic tests. Implementation follows that surface.\n607\t\n608\tThe design section in `worklog.md` must include:\n609\t\n610\t- public surface (types, schemas, parse/validate functions),\n611\t- constants for finite domain values,\n612\t- commit slices with gates,\n613\t- contributor path for adding a new type or schema.\n614\t\n615\t## Concept of Done\n616\t\n617\tBeyond the universal slice checklist in `workflow/run-loop.md`:\n618\t\n619\t- the README documents the type table and permissions,\n620\t- the public surface exports a curated contract, not every internal type,\n621\t- no single schema file has grown into a bag of unrelated concepts,\n622\t- validation semantics are tested, not just compilation.\n623\t\n624\t## Historical Notes\n625\t\n626\tSmall packages are allowed to remain small. Adding generic layers to make them look\n627\tenterprise-shaped is a doctrine violation, not a maturity upgrade.\n628\t\n629\t\n630\t## .llm/harness/archetypes/ARCHETYPE-5-plugin.md\n631\t\n632\tOriginal-byte SHA256:41abbc01781524e355d3436690673cc241a90e46693006a4aacbde23dbc1893c\n633\t\n634\t# Archetype 5 — Plugin Package\n635\t\n636\t**Thinness law (framing for this whole profile).** Convention-bearing primitives — contracts, base\n637\tservices, schema/runtime conventions, event/kind vocabularies — live in `@netscript/*` **core**. A\n638\tfirst-party `plugins/*` package is **thin userland glue**: it **wires and composes** core-owned\n639\tprimitives into a concrete integration and **re-exports** sibling contracts; it does **not** redefine\n640\tcontracts, re-implement a core convention, or own what core should own. The reference shape is\n641\t`auth-core` + its thin adapters: the core package carries the convention, the plugin/adapter is the\n642\tminimal wiring that binds it to a provider. Read every section below through that lens — a plugin\n643\tthat \"owns\" a contribution axis is a smell, not the target.\n644\t\n645\t## Doctrine Reference\n646\t\n647\t- Axioms: A1, A2, A5, A7, A8, A9, A10, A11, A12, A13, A14.\n648\t- Primary sections:\n649\t  - `docs/architecture/doctrine/05-folder-structure.md`\n650\t  - `docs/architecture/doctrine/06-archetypes.md#archetype-5--plugin-package`\n651\t  - `docs/architecture/doctrine/07-composition-and-extension.md#plugin-discovery-and-loading`\n652\t"}]}}

This profile follows doctrine; it does\n32\tnot rewrite it.\n33\t\n34\t## Skills to Activate\n35\t\n36\t- `netscript-doctrine`\n37\t- `jsr-audit` when the plugin is published or exported\n38\t- `aspire` when runtime/service validation is required\n39\t\n40\t## Read First\n41\t\n42\t1. The **core-owned contracts and conventions** the plugin wires — the base contract/service in\n43\t   `@netscript/*` and any sibling package contracts it re-exports or consumes. Establish what core\n44\t   already owns before reading the plugin, so you can see whether the plugin thinly reuses it.\n45\t2. `docs/architecture/doctrine/06-archetypes.md#archetype-5--plugin-package`.\n46\t3. `docs/architecture/doctrine/07-composition-and-extension.md#plugin-discovery-and-loading`.\n47\t4. Static gates: plugin check slice, package check if sibling contracts changed, fmt, lint, doc\n55\t   lint, publish dry-run when relevant.\n56\t2. Runtime gates: required when plugin services, workers, sagas, triggers, or database contributions\n59\t   are touched.\n60\t4. Consumer gates: required for plugin loader, host imports, and sibling package contracts.\n61\t\n62\t## Anti-Patterns to Watch For\n63\t\n64\t- **Fat plugin owning what core should own**: the plugin defines a contract, base service, schema\n65\t  convention, or event/kind vocabulary that is convention-bearing and therefore belongs in\n66\t  `@netscript/*` core. Push it down into core and have the plugin import it (thinness law; cf. the\n67\t  `auth-core` + adapters reference shape).\n68\t- **Plugin re-implements a core convention instead of importing it**: re-deriving a\n69\t  loading/registration/schema/runtime convention locally rather than reusing the core-owned one.\n70\t  Import the convention; do not fork it.\n71\t- AP-11: plugin load side effects or implicit discovery magic.\n72\t- AP-14: redefining sibling package contracts instead of re-exporting.\n73\t- AP-16: generic folders that hide plugin contributions.\n74\t- AP-19: service/database permissions not declared.\n75\t- AP-1: `mod.ts` or `contracts.ts` accumulating every plugin concern.\n76\t- AP-22: sub-directory `mod.ts` barrel re-exporting a contribution folder instead of the\n77\t  package-root surface.\n78\t- AP-23: plugin discovery/registration wiring that inlines service or handler bodies instead of\n79\t  referencing them.\n80\t- AP-24: switch-over-kind dispatch (engine/event/adapter) instead of a typed registry populated at\n81\t  composition.\n82\t- AP-25: `Deno.*` / `console.*` / `fetch` side effects in a non-edge plugin file instead of an\n83\t  injected port.\n84\t\n85\t## False-Done States\n86\t\n87\t- Plugin compiles but `verify-plugin.ts` is missing or stale.\n88\t- Runtime declarations changed without host/loader validation.\n89\t- Database schema contribution exists but is not referenced from the expected plugin folder.\n90\t- The plugin **redefines or re-implements** a contract/convention (worker/saga/trigger contract,\n91\t  loading/schema convention) already owned by core or a sibling — a fat-plugin false-done: it looks\n92\t  complete but has duplicated what it should have imported.\n93\t\n94\t## Rescope Triggers\n95\t\n96\t- A plugin change requires sibling package contract redesign.\n97\t- Host loader semantics need to change.\n98\t- Runtime validation requires Aspire resources not available in session.\n99\t- The plugin folder shape is wrong enough to require restructuring first.\n100\t- Convention-bearing logic discovered inside the plugin belongs in core — pushing it down is a\n101\t  core-package change, not a plugin edit.\n102\t\n103\t## Design Checkpoint Expectations\n104\t\n105\tThe design checkpoint names, for each axis the plugin wires, **which core/sibling primitive it\n106\tcomposes and re-exports** (never redefines): contracts, service entrypoints, database,\n107\tjobs/sagas/triggers/streams, verification, host discovery, and consumer impact.\n108\t\n109\tThe design section in `worklog.md` must include:\n110\t\n111\t- the axes the plugin wires, with the named core/sibling primitive each one composes and the plugin\n112\t  files that wire it,\n113\t- sibling package contracts consumed or **re-exported** (explicitly: none redefined),\n114\t- constants for plugin config, event kinds, or schema identifiers — sourced from core vocabularies\n115\t  where core owns them,\n116\t- commit slices: reuse-core-contracts first, then service/runtime wiring, then verification, then\n117\t  host integration,\n118\t- contributor path for adding a new wired axis (which core primitive to import, where to wire it).\n119\t\n120\t## Concept of Done\n121\t\n122\tBeyond the universal slice checklist in `workflow/run-loop.md`:\n123\t\n124\t- `verify-plugin.ts` exists and passes,\n125\t- runtime declarations are validated against host loader expectations,\n126\t- database schema contributions reference the expected plugin folder,\n127\t- sibling and core contracts are **re-exported/imported, not redefined**, and no convention-bearing\n128\t  logic that belongs in core was re-implemented in the plugin.\n129\t\n130\t## Historical Notes\n131\t\n132\tPlugins are **thin integration points**, not capability owners. A green plugin-only check is not\n133\tenough when the host loader or a sibling/core contract changes — and it is actively misleading when\n134\tthe plugin passed only because it re-implemented a convention core should own. The fat-plugin failure\n135\tmode (owning contracts/conventions that belong in `@netscript/*` core) is the highest-value thing to\n136\tcatch in review; the `auth-core` + adapters split is the reference for how thin the plugin should be.\n137\t\n138\t\n139\t## Exact canonical debt entry (.llm/harness/debt/arch-debt.md:2932)\n140\t\n141\tFull fileSHA:d7ada055665ff1d9efc031fbdbbda608040ab161f1d297fd12a14b05f3ad91e3\n142\t\n143\t## Fresh/workers reader headers — native public type closure\n144\t\n145\t- **ID:** `U3-NATIVE-READER-HEADER-TYPE-CLOSURE`\n146\t- **Reason:** U3 preserves exact native client0.2.7 `HeadersRecord` identity for\n147\t  string/sync/async values.
